## 5. Surface / API Inventory

### New surfaces

- `/forge:init` — install/refresh the per-repo layer (region file, AGENTS.md splice, `.codex/`, gitignore block, `.forge/`, eval fixtures)
- `/forge:commit` — the 5-step fail-closed commit gate chain
- `/forge:worktree-merge` — the 4-gate merge chain with locked rebase reintegration
- `/forge:drift` — periodic semantic drift review over the FR-161 Drift summary schema v1 JSON
- `/forge:learn` — advisory `review-periodic` analysis over mechanical journal patterns, committed run archives, and committed gotchas
- `codex_orch_tools.py validate --gates` — Level B gate enforcement profile
- Plugin PreToolUse hook (`scripts/forge/commit-guard.sh`) — blocks `git commit`/`git push` on halt or missing gate-pass marker
- Plugin PostToolUse hook (`scripts/forge/invariant-guard.sh`) — advisory invariant feedback after Edit/Write
- Plugin Stop/SessionStart hooks — the Stop union of telemetry aggregation plus a drift-staleness nudge, and the SessionStart drift-staleness nudge, in forge-initialized repos
- `agents/review-final.md` — read-only Claude reviewer with binding verdict
- `scripts/forge/{check-halt.sh,acquire-commit-lock.sh,release-commit-lock.sh,run-evals.sh,invariant-guard.sh,configure-dcg.sh,drift-check.sh,drift-staleness.sh,aggregate-telemetry.sh,commit-guard.sh,install.sh}` and `scripts/forge/{check-test-quality.py,archive-run.py,audit-commitments.py,emit-decision-event.py,journal-patterns.py}` — executable governance script surfaces
- `scripts/forge/run-scoped-mutation.py` — Python-interpreter-loaded governance helper (the merge path invokes it as `python3 <path>` and the drift path imports it), so it is outside the executable-script inventory
- `scripts/forge/forge_cli/` — the Python-interpreter-loaded package the Forge CLI is being split into (bead forge-plugin-95e): `scripts/forge/cli.py` remains the sole invoked entry point and shim that FR-221's matcher and the FR-223 corpora pin; the shim puts its own directory on `sys.path` and re-imports each moved name by an explicit named list so every historical module attribute stays resolvable, tests reach the package through one shared loader (`tests/_cli_loader.py`), no file under it is executable, and it is outside the executable-script inventory exactly as `run-scoped-mutation.py` is. Phase 1 (bead forge-plugin-95e.2) holds `envelope.py` (reason codes, `Refusal`, `FrozenError`, `Outcome`) and `policy.py` (the committed-policy parser, whose fence helpers are the canonical patch seams); phase 2a (bead forge-plugin-95e.3) adds `runtime.py`, the one canonical module for the patchable controls (the clock, the bounded process runner, the merge lifecycle flag, the Revision-9 state controls, the path roots, the lazily imported coordination modules, and the fast-tier mechanical-skip predicate), which every module of the split reads by attribute so one in-memory patch on `forge_cli.runtime` disables a control everywhere while the shim forwards attribute reads of those names to it; the remaining clusters move only through that module. The FR-230 phase-3/4 manifest's `subjects.production` set covers every package module the shim imports beside `cli.py` and `commit-guard.sh`, so a change to a moved definition changes the subject candidate exactly as a shim edit does. The split changes no verb, diagnostic, reason code, `--help` byte, or corpus
- `scripts/forge/cli.py` — the Forge CLI commit-chain slice (FR-210..FR-224): a persisted chain state machine invoked as `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" <verb>`, owning commit-chain sequencing, staging, candidate identity, evidence capture, and finalize. No PATH binary named `forge` is installed (the Foundry `forge` collision); `forge <verb>` anywhere in this spec is shorthand for that invocation form. Phase-gated: ships only after the FR-223 precondition evals pass
- Phase-3 Forge CLI merge surfaces specified by FR-230..FR-237: `status`, `merge start|refresh|verify|gate run|approve|finalize|recover|cleanup|abort`, and the shared `review request|attach|disposition` verbs. These are normative future surfaces, not implementation delivered by Revision 7.
- Phase-4 `forge push`, DM-015 activation parsing, and the v2 PreToolUse matcher/raw-denial behavior specified by FR-238..FR-241. These are normative future surfaces, not implementation delivered by Revision 7.
- Additive phase-3/4 v2 reason-code and hook-vector artifacts in the exact DM-016 inventory. Every v1 artifact remains immutable.
- Revision-9 typed writer, batch recovery, structured binding, ingest, chain-evidence, and archive-rerender contracts; the additive v3 reason-code corpus extends only `forge-cli/2` and preserves every v1/v2 row byte-identically.

### Modified surfaces

- `/forge:workflow`, `/forge:orchestrate`, `/forge:report` — vendored skills renamed into the `forge` namespace; workflow gains gate steps and the gated close sequence (`validate --gates → run_closed → validate --gates → archive → report.md`); orchestrate gains role routing, detached launch mechanics, and hardening rules
- `hooks/hooks.json` — add PostToolUse invariant feedback and the plugin-level SessionStart drift-staleness registration; make Stop the union contract in FR-093
- `scripts/forge/install.sh`, `system/template/forge-project.md`, and `tests/test_commit_and_region_template.py` — migrate the same closed region inventory from nine to the exact DM-003 fourteen-region order
- `scripts/forge/acquire-commit-lock.sh [<lock-path>]` and `scripts/forge/release-commit-lock.sh [<lock-path>]` — generalize both helpers to accept one optional explicit repository-relative lock-path argument. When the argument is omitted, both helpers default to `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` and preserve FR-055's current behavior and output byte-for-byte; FR-157 reserves the explicit `.forge/tmp/events.lock` path for the prune read-and-replace and uses recovery-only lock-state revalidation for emitters waiting on a live pruner
- `scripts/forge/aggregate-telemetry.sh` — required code changes: consume the FR-157 decision-event stream before any no-Markdown/no-unit return; extend the standalone CSV schema to all thirteen counters and, in a Forge-initialized repository, emit the CSV including the `__decision_totals__` row whenever `events.jsonl` exists, independently of whether any per-unit `*.md` decision logs or fenced telemetry blocks exist; accept paired `--since <UTC-ISO-8601> --until <UTC-ISO-8601>` bounds plus append-only `--append-csv <path> --session <session-id>` Stop mode; and implement §9's exit-2 contract for a missing or malformed CLI argument, unpaired/malformed/reversed window arguments, or a read/write failure. The Stop surface serializes session-identified appends, while `drift-check.sh` constructs its JSON counters directly without a CSV artifact
- `system/seeds/validation-snippets/stacks.md` — add per-stack assertion, mutation-tool, and property-library seed metadata required by FR-139
- `tests/test_docs_contract.py` — assertions migrated to the merged prose (same contract-testing approach)
- `tests/replay/long-run-001/` — journal extended with gate verifications so the replay passes `validate --gates`
- Future phase-3/4 implementation surfaces governed by this revision: `scripts/forge/cli.py`, `scripts/forge/commit-guard.sh`, `hooks/hooks.json`, installed hook templates, `.forge-manifest` parsing and init/re-init rendering, `/forge:worktree-merge`, the installed Beads close-protocol block, CLI help/routing inventories, and their conformance tests. Revision 7 changes none of those files.
- Revision-9 implementation surfaces governed by this amendment: `scripts/codex_orch_tools.py`, `scripts/codex_orchestrator/`, `scripts/forge/cli.py`, `scripts/forge/archive-run.py`, `scripts/forge/audit-commitments.py`, the workflow/orchestration/commit/merge skill text that calls those surfaces, and their focused fixtures, corpora, and conformance tests. Revision 9 changes none of those implementation files itself.

### Deferred From This Spec

- Upstream PRs to alexzh3/codex-orchestrator — post-implementation work per the contribution table in `0001-founding-decisions.md`
- Cost/token capture in the eval runner — upstream open decision; the >20% cost threshold stays advisory and manually recorded
- A `forge-sync` upstream-diff command — upstream assessment is manual against the `UPSTREAM` manifest until cherry-pick volume justifies tooling
- Claude agents beyond `review-final` (debugger, security-auditor, test-runner, simplifier, docs-writer, council-seat, taskify-agent, code-reviewer) — outside the D1/D2 role split; reassess after real usage
- GPU compute gating (`references/compute.md` nvidia-smi section) — retained verbatim in the vendored reference but not integrated with the gate chain
- Renumbering the constitution's missing SEC-10 — principle IDs are cited by other documents; renumbering breaks references for zero behavioral gain
- Phase-5 removal of DM-006 markers, their parser, or their tests — FR-242 defines admission only; Revision 7 does not authorize deletion, weakening, or dormant-code cleanup.
- Phase-6 implementation of `forge plan seal --file <plan>` or `forge proposal unseal` — FR-243 reserves the future names and honest best-effort claim only; no executable surface, parser branch, storage, or confidentiality claim is authorized.

### Revision 9 Non-goals

Revision 9 deliberately does not authorize the proposal's journal-layer generation algebra, reviewer quorum, or FR-124 plan/proposal binding; chain state retains generation authority and the journal carries only the minimal DM-001 chain-event binding. It also defers every pre-close manifest or manifest-authentication subsystem, a close barrier, streaming or prospective capacity estimators, generated closure/reserve tables, manifest capacity accounting, automatic garbage collection, and garbage-collection economics. The legacy phase-0 path therefore has no recovery-manifest artifact, and normal close has no archive-manifest member. `landed-external`, any other new journal enum, post-`run_closed` appends, rewriting an existing DM-008 archive, and embedding the necessarily later archive-commit chain into the archive it creates are non-goals rather than alternate paths. Only the per-chain 2 MiB event-log embedding threshold, digest-only `UNEMBEDDED` fallback, and 16 MiB final-archive limit are adopted size controls; broader capacity migration is deferred.

The deferred generation subsystem specifically includes journal-owned `generation_id`, `(task,chain,generation)` authority, landed/superseded generation tuples, supersession rules, terminal-task `binding_summary`, and aggregate digests. The deferred FR-124 seal includes plan/proposal paths, digests, byte counts, roles, creation order, and same-generation binding; existing FR-124 stays unchanged. The deferred close/authentication family includes pre-close manifest `M`, `archive_manifest`, authenticated manifest-backed normal mode, manifest self-reference reconstruction, sorted bound-chain locking, the proposed audit snapshot/seal, and every close-time capacity reservation. The deferred legacy-manifest family includes separate manifest `L`, `archive prepare-legacy`, `--legacy-recovery-manifest`, manifest embedding, and second-active-manifest state. The unadopted capacity family includes every proposed journal/record/string/array/chain/state/basis/evidence/audit/legacy/discrepancy/intent/receipt/manifest/auxiliary cap except the two adopted archive limits, oversized-legacy/capacity migration, streaming estimation, and closure/recovery reserve calculation. Manifest retention, auxiliary reclamation, generalized recovery ownership/quorum, and automatic GC are likewise deferred; completed-intent unlink remains required transaction cleanup rather than GC. No prose-authoritative `legacy-inferred` grammar or legacy cutoff is authorized: pre-activation prose is display-only. The exact new spellings selected by Revision 9 are authority from this revision, not claims that the adjudication had already supplied them.

---

## 6. Data Model Changes

**DM-001**: Journal gate-verification convention (no schema change — a naming convention over the existing `verification` entry):

```jsonl
{"type":"verification","id":"check-07","task":"task-02","criterion":"gate-1: project tests","method":"command","check":"<the forge-project.md gate1-test-command>","result":"passed","observation":"41 tests passed; exit code 0.","recorded_at":"2026-08-08T14:00:00Z"}
{"type":"verification","id":"check-08","task":"task-02","criterion":"gate-2: lint and types","method":"command","check":"ruff check . && mypy src/","result":"passed","observation":"clean; exit code 0.","recorded_at":"2026-08-08T14:02:00Z"}
{"type":"verification","id":"check-09","task":"task-02","criterion":"gate-3: review-final verdict","method":"inspection","check":"review-final subagent over git diff <baseline-sha>..HEAD","result":"passed","observation":"PASS; 0 CRITICAL/MAJOR findings; iteration 2 of 8.","recorded_at":"2026-08-08T14:20:00Z"}
```

Rules: a gate verification's `criterion` MUST begin with exactly `gate-1: `, `gate-2: `, or `gate-3: ` (lowercase, single space after colon). Gate 3's criterion MUST be exactly `gate-3: review-final verdict`. A gate `result` uses the upstream enum; a BLOCK verdict is recorded as `result: "failed"` with the verdict and finding count in `observation`. A gate-3 verification's `check` field MUST name the exact reviewed candidate — the reviewed commit range with full SHAs (task/merge reviews) or the staged-diff SHA-256 (commit reviews). That candidate identity is threaded unchanged through the pipeline: the same hash/SHA appears in the gate-pass marker (DM-006), in any control-class approval prompt, and in the reintegration push. This is a convention over existing fields; no schema change.

Revision-9 amendment to **DM-001**: a `verification` or `decision` produced from a Forge commit or merge chain MAY carry one known `binding` object, and every chain-derived gate, approval, skip, or landing record written by an activated writer MUST carry it. The object has exactly this retained, generation-free shape:

```json
{
  "schema": "forge-gate-binding/1",
  "source_record": {
    "chain_id": "c-...",
    "event_digest": "64-lowercase-hex"
  },
  "candidate": {
    "kind": "staged-diff-sha256",
    "value": "..."
  },
  "review": {
    "verdict": "PASS",
    "iteration": 2,
    "reviewer_role": "review-final",
    "package_digest": "64-lowercase-hex"
  },
  "binding_id": "64-lowercase-hex"
}
```

`source_record` has exactly `chain_id` and `event_digest`; the digest MUST name one replay-valid event's DM-012 source projection in that chain. For an event without an event-carried journal batch the source projection is the ordinary event and this value equals its event digest; for a batch-carrying event the projection rule makes the value nonrecursive while the event's ordinary final digest still covers the complete batch. `candidate.kind` is exactly `staged-diff-sha256`, `git-commit`, or `git-range`. The first has a 64-lowercase-hex string value, `git-commit` has one full Git object ID string, and `git-range` has exactly `{"base":"<full-object-id>","head":"<full-object-id>"}`. `review` is null for a non-review gate or landing fact and otherwise has exactly the four keys shown: `verdict` is `PASS` or `BLOCK`, `iteration` is a positive integer, `reviewer_role` is `review-cheap` or `review-final`, and `package_digest` is 64 lowercase hex. `binding_id` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the DM-013 canonical bytes of exactly `{schema,source_record,candidate,review}`; it is not part of its own preimage. Journal records do not carry `generation_id`, generation numbers, generation digests, supersession algebra, or aggregate generation summaries: replayed chain state remains the sole generation authority.

The outer record supplies task and gate context. A Gate-3 `PASS` binding requires `result: "passed"`; a Gate-3 `BLOCK` binding requires `result: "failed"`. A chain-derived `decision.outcome` is exactly `chain-approval`, `chain-skip`, `chain-landing`, or (Revision 13) `chain-abort`, which makes its binding purpose structured without making `resolution` prose authoritative. A `chain-abort` binding, like a landing, carries no review tuple and retains the exact candidate the aborted chain abandoned; its source event is that chain's `chain_aborted` event. A landing binding retains the exact candidate object reviewed by its gates; it does not switch from `staged-diff-sha256` to the produced `git-commit`, for example. The replayed landing event proves the staged-diff-to-commit or merge mapping and carries the landed object separately. The reviewer role MUST match the existing route required for that gate and candidate; in particular, `gate-3: review-final verdict` accepts only `review-final`. A binding is accepted only when replay of `source_record.chain_id` proves the exact event digest, candidate, review tuple, current chain generation, and the outer record's task, gate criterion/result, or decision outcome as applicable; one source event cannot be repurposed under a different outer context. Equality is byte-for-byte after the canonical type checks above. A stale, partial, malformed, unknown-version, or conflicting higher-authority binding never falls through to prose.

New runs activate this contract only by the exact known field `run_started.writer_contract: "forge-journal-binding/1"`, injected by `run-open`; the declaration governs that record and every later record in the run. A pre-activation run remains legacy and cannot acquire the contract through a later declaration. For legacy rendering, prose is escaped display material only and never authorization evidence: an unresolved or ambiguous row displays exactly `UNBOUND`. The gate table is built from resolved bindings and gains the exact columns `Binding source` and `Binding status`. A legacy `decision` string is preserved as bounded escaped text under `Decision (legacy field)`, together with its physical journal line and raw-line SHA-256; a record containing both legacy and structured decision shapes displays both and records a discrepancy.

The complete Revision-9 discrepancy-code set is fixed, unique, and closed:

```text
ambiguous_legacy_candidate
ignored_nonreview_verdict
legacy_decision_shape
missing_chain_artifact
result_verdict_conflict
snapshot_changed
structured_chain_mismatch
unbound_approval
```

`structured_chain_mismatch`, `result_verdict_conflict`, `unbound_approval`, `missing_chain_artifact`, and `snapshot_changed` are authoritative discrepancies and refuse ordinary archive creation. The remaining codes describe legacy display ambiguity; they remain visible beside `UNBOUND` and are never guessed into authority. Adding, deleting, or respelling a code is a control-class change.

**DM-002**: `validate` output payload gains one key when `--gates` is passed (absent otherwise, preserving upstream shape):

```json
{"ok": true, "issues": [], "warnings": [], "non_passing_verifications": [], "profile": "gates"}
```

**DM-003**: `forge-project.md` (repo root, committed). Contains, in order: a header naming the plugin and install date; a compact DVRR spine (operating model, instruction priority, git policy, untrusted-input rule, risk/authority classes — static text, no regions); pointers to the plugin skills; then exactly fourteen regions in the order below. The first nine retain the shipped pre-Revision-2 order and the five Revision-2 regions follow them, so installer migration is deterministic:

| Region | Content | Required for gates |
|---|---|---|
| `project-overview` | 5–15 line project description, tech stack, CI, repository facts | no |
| `file-categories` | one `\| category \| file patterns \|` row per stack + generic `bash`/`docs`/`config`/`control` rows | yes (Gate 2 / commit Step 1) |
| `stack-validations` | per-category executable validation commands | yes (Gate 2 / commit Step 2) |
| `gate1-test-command` | targeted test command + always-run blast-radius suite | yes (Gate 1) |
| `changelog-policy` | changelog gate or the explicit text "No changelog gate is configured for this repository." | no |
| `review-prompt-project-focus` | 3–5 review-focus bullets | no |
| `project-triggers` | 3–8 prose `\| Pattern \| Required Checks \|` rows used only as review-prompt context; mechanically inert | no |
| `completeness-project-items` | 2–4 review-completeness checklist items | no |
| `agent-project-context` | 3–8 lines of per-repo context injected into every Codex agent prompt | no |
| `mutation-testing` | `\| category \| command \| changed-files form \| timeout \|` rows, or one explicit infeasible-stack declaration per stack | no |
| `invariants` | `\| invariant \| check command \| enforcement point \|` rows; enforcement point is `commit`, `merge`, or `hook` | no |
| `risk-tiers` | a `\| tier \| path patterns \|` table, a `\| formatting-only category \|` opt-in table, and the fixed dependency-manifest pathspec block below | no |
| `drift-config` | exactly `cadence: <positive-integer>d`, `retention: forever\|<positive-integer>d` for committed reports, and `event-retention: <positive-integer>d` of at least `366d` for `events.jsonl`; defaults are `14d`, `forever`, and `400d` respectively, with 400 days exceeding four UTC quarters | no |
| `trigger-paths` | zero or more positive repository-relative Git pathspec globs, one per `\| Path pattern \|` row, used only for tiering | no |

Region markers and the unfilled sentinel use upstream syntax: `<!-- FORGE:REGION <name> BEGIN -->` / `<!-- FORGE:REGION <name> END -->`, with an embedded `<!-- forge-init: ... -->` comment marking a region unfilled. The shipped `gate1-test-command` default body is `echo "forge: Gate 1 test command not configured — run /forge:init before merging" >&2; exit 1`.

Inside the `risk-tiers` region, the template MUST ship this plugin-owned block verbatim; `/forge:init` MUST preserve it when filling or refreshing the surrounding region. It is part of committed `forge-project.md`, and therefore control-class. The classifier and guard consume this same committed block under FR-151/FR-154 rather than maintaining separate manifest lists:

```text
<!-- FORGE:DEPENDENCY-MANIFEST-PATHS BEGIN -->
package.json
package-lock.json
yarn.lock
pnpm-lock.yaml
requirements*.txt
pyproject.toml
poetry.lock
uv.lock
Cargo.toml
Cargo.lock
go.mod
go.sum
Gemfile
Gemfile.lock
pom.xml
build.gradle*
composer.json
composer.lock
<!-- FORGE:DEPENDENCY-MANIFEST-PATHS END -->
```

Phase-1.1 amendment to DM-003, documenting shipped behavior without creating a new policy surface: `changelog` is the sole policy-configurable mutating gate; phase 1 has no generic mutating-gate registry, mutability column, or `mutating: true` declaration. The whole stripped `changelog-policy` body means no gate only when it full-matches, case-insensitively and with dot matching newlines, the following grammar:

```regex
No changelog gate (?:is configured|applies)(?: for| to)?(?: this)? .*?repository\.
```

Every other body MUST contain exactly one nonempty, NUL-free fenced `bash` or `sh` cell and at least one declared output. Outputs come from two shipped parser channels. A case-insensitive multiline search contributes only the first matching `Output path:` or `Output paths:` prose line; later matching prose lines are ignored. Separately, every Markdown row whose normalized first logical cell is `output`, `output path`, or `output paths` contributes in document order. The prose-channel tokens are accumulated first, followed by the Markdown-row tokens; values are comma-split, trimmed, and first-occurrence deduplicated. Surrounding backticks are removed at the shipped parser boundary: from each comma token on the first prose line, and from the whole logical Markdown cell before that cell is comma-split. A successful changelog run adds those outputs to the chain path set, stages them explicitly, recomputes the candidate, invalidates downstream evidence, and reruns classification before recording the gate against the new candidate.

**DM-004**: AGENTS.md splice block: `/forge:init` inserts (or refreshes) the full rendered content of `forge-project.md` between `<!-- FORGE:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- FORGE:END -->` markers in the repo's `AGENTS.md`, creating the file when absent. Content outside the markers is never modified. CLAUDE.md receives the line `@forge-project.md` (appended when missing; file created when absent).

**DM-005**: `.forge-manifest` (repo root, committed) — line-oriented keys: `forge_version: 1`, `plugin_ref: <git describe or SHA of the plugin>`, `installed: <YYYY-MM-DD>`, `project_name: <name>`, `default_branch: <branch>`, `init_completed: true|false`, one `region: <name>` line per filled region.

**DM-006**: Content-addressed gate-pass marker `.forge/tmp/authorized/<staged-diff-sha256>` — `<staged-diff-sha256>` and line 1 are both the SHA-256 hex of the exact bytes of the authorized `git diff --cached` output; line 2 is the UTC ISO-8601 timestamp at authorization. Line 3 and later are annotations. The guard accepts exactly three shapes: (a) the 2-line reviewed `standard`/`hard` form; (b) the 3-line user-skip form whose line 3 is exactly `skip: user-directed`; or (c) the 4-line fast form whose line 3 is exactly `tier: fast` and whose line 4 matches `policy: <sha>`, where `<sha>` is the full hexadecimal commit object ID emitted by `git rev-parse HEAD`. Annotations MUST NOT be duplicated, combined across forms, or reordered. The guard rejects every other line count, annotation, or value shape as malformed. The marker is stale 30 minutes after line 2; this grammar and staleness rule are unchanged from Revision 2.

**DM-007**: Repo-local state layout: `.forge/evals/tasks/` (committed promoted fixtures + `.result` baselines), `.forge/evals/candidates/` (committed advisory fixture proposals, never consumed by a gate), `.forge/history/` (committed run archives, drift reports, migration reports, and gotchas per DM-008), and `.forge/tmp/` (gitignored: locks, content-addressed markers under `authorized/`, append-only or session-scoped telemetry, the `decisions/` directory, run registry, halt audit log, transient drift JSON under `drift/`, and the operator-cleared CRITICAL state file `.forge/tmp/drift-block`). Gitignore block appended by init (guarded by its `# --- forge agent system --- #` header line): `/.forge/tmp/`, `/.forge/chains/`, `.worktrees/`, `/AGENT_HALT`, `/AGENT_HALT_*`, `*.local.md` (the `/.forge/chains/` entry ships with the FR-210 phase-1 revision; earlier installs gain it on re-init). The block MUST NOT ignore `.forge/history/` or `.forge/evals/candidates/`. The block file persists locally until an operator clears it, but because it is inside the already-ignored `.forge/tmp/` it does not alter any clean-tree predicate.

**DM-008**: `.forge/history/` is committed, append-only skill output with exactly four layouts: `runs/<run-id>.md` for the durable-intent archive, `drift/<date>.md` for a drift report, `migrations/<date>.md` for a migration report, and `gotchas.md` for learned failure shapes. `<run-id>` is the journal run directory name. For `drift/` and `migrations/`, `<date>` is a collision-free UTC filename timestamp `YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSSZ`; if that name already exists, the writer appends `-02`, `-03`, and so on before `.md`. Forge automation MUST NOT overwrite, truncate, delete, or amend any existing history file. The sole exception is that `/forge:learn` MAY append new lines to `.forge/history/gotchas.md` while preserving every existing byte; no other automation gains or may delegate this append right. Forge skills never prune this directory; `drift-config`'s report `retention` key is the minimum operator retention period and does not authorize automated report deletion.

Revision-9 reaffirmation of **DM-008**: a new renderer, a newly discovered binding discrepancy, or improved chain evidence never authorizes regenerating an existing run archive. The already-committed coordination, merge-chain, and other run archives remain byte-identical historical documents; diagnostics about them are recorded elsewhere. Revision 9 may create only an archive path absent from the committed history, including the still-absent phase-0 archive, and the `gotchas.md` exception above remains the sole in-place history mutation.

**DM-009**: `.forge/history/migrations/<date>.md` is the mandatory committed migration report defined by FR-186. It is a DM-008 history file and therefore uses the same collision-free `<date>` rule and no-overwrite rule.

**DM-010**: Journal owner sidecar `.codex-orchestrator/runs/<run-id>/owner` contains exactly three UTF-8, LF-terminated lines and no others: `pid: <positive-base-10 FORGE_SESSION_PID>`, `host: <nonempty hostname>`, and `started_at: <UTC ISO-8601>`. It is created atomically in the same run-open critical operation that appends `run_started`. `FORGE_SESSION_PID` is an environment contract injected by a long-lived harness: it MUST name one live same-host owner in the same PID namespace and MUST be inherited unchanged by every fresh tool shell for that session. No `--session-pid` flag exists, and skills MUST NOT export or substitute shell `$$`, `$PPID`, or any transient tool-process PID as the identity. Missing or malformed identity MUST retain exactly `forge: FORGE_SESSION_PID must be exported as a positive base-10 integer`; a syntactically valid PID that is dead or whose same-host liveness cannot be verified MUST refuse exactly `forge: FORGE_SESSION_PID does not name a live same-host session owner`. Both refusals occur before owner, journal, registry, lock, or marker mutation. A stale PID in an existing same-host owner sidecar remains eligible for atomic takeover by a different current identity that has itself passed this liveness check; a same-host live different PID or a different/unverifiable host remains foreign ownership. A takeover MAY be audited with an existing `decision` entry; it MUST NOT add a journal type. This identity is a same-user coordination primitive, not authentication.

**DM-011**: Repo-scoped run registry `.forge/tmp/run-registry.json` retains the canonical sorted-key schema `{"open_runs":[{"run_id":"<run-id>","scope":["<positive repository-relative Git pathspec>"]}],"schema_version":1}`. `open_runs` is sorted bytewise by `run_id`; each nonempty `scope` is deduplicated and bytewise sorted. Registry reading, reconciliation, admission, and atomic replacement are serialized under the worktree-transparent `.forge/tmp/run-registry.lock`; the registry is transient coordination state, not a journal or history file. Reconciliation is two-phase under that lock: first read and validate the complete registry snapshot without deriving truth from directory enumeration; then use `lstat` to classify every runs-root child, read the journals that are eligible for reconciliation, derive lifecycle and scope state in memory, and compare that result with the validated snapshot. Before publishing any unrelated registry replacement, revalidate every excluded placeholder's observed inode, type, and emptiness; a changed or unprovable observation fails closed.

Global reconciliation MUST silently exclude a runs-root child only when it is either (a) a non-dot regular file — non-run-state, silently ignored; this codifies the shipped behavior pinned by the committed stray-file regression test, where a hook-deposited documentation stub in the runs root must not wedge coordination — or (b) a non-dot, real non-symlink directory, absent from the registry, completely empty, ownerless, and without `journal.jsonl`; it MUST NOT exclude any other directory or non-regular child. It MUST leave that placeholder byte-for-byte untouched and emit no journal entry, warning, stdout, or registry member. A same-ID open MUST NOT adopt, overwrite, or delete it and instead refuses exactly `forge: new run refused — run <run-id> directory exists without journal.jsonl`. An unregistered, ownerless, nonempty real directory with no `journal.jsonl` refuses exactly `forge: new run refused — run directory <path> lacks journal.jsonl`, where `<path>` is the validated deterministic repository-relative run-directory path obtained from the inspected directory entry, never an unvalidated caller spelling; an entry that cannot be rendered safely is inspection ambiguity. Owner presence takes precedence over that nonempty classification: registered orphans, symlinks or broken links, owner-bearing journal-less directories, dot-prefixed crash state, inspection errors, and present-but-empty, malformed, unreadable, or non-file journals retain exactly `forge: new run refused — run registry unavailable`. Explicit `validate <orphan>` and `monitor --run-id <orphan>` retain their existing missing-journal behavior; the global exclusion does not apply to targeted operations.

Successor scope reservation is derived from the validated journal graph under the same lock; no registry schema field is added. Each `run_started.successor_of` is a directed predecessor edge. Each unresolved open or retired descendant leaf's effective reservation is the union of its own admitted scope and every unresolved retired ancestor's admitted scope; `open_runs` continues to store only each open run's own canonical declared scope. The derived lifecycle is exactly: (1) a retired run with no successor is a scope-reserving retired leaf; (2) opening its unique direct successor atomically transfers that effective reservation to the successor; (3) an open successor is protected through `open_runs` plus the derived ancestral union; (4) a retired successor becomes the new scope-reserving leaf; (5) successfully closing a successor with judgment `passed` or `blocked` releases that branch's complete retired ancestry; (6) a failed or rolled-back close releases nothing; and (7) commit, merge, archive, worktree removal, and directory deletion never release scope. For `A(retired) → B(retired) → C`, C may name B without A blocking admission, and C's readmission ignores A and B as separate conflicts while its effective reservation still carries their unresolved scopes and it still checks unrelated reserving leaves. Historical forks remain readable as a DAG: every open or retired leaf independently carries the shared ancestral union, and a shared ancestor is released only after every descendant branch has successfully closed. New branching from a predecessor whose reservation was already transferred is refused. A dangling edge, cycle, duplicate edge, duplicate run identity, otherwise unprovable lineage, or lifecycle/registry mismatch remains persisted coordination ambiguity and fails closed with the unchanged registry-unavailable refusal.

**DM-012**: Commit-chain state (FR-210..FR-224). One materialized-state JSON per chain at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.json` plus an append-only event log at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.events.jsonl`, with per-chain evidence transcripts and review artifacts under `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/`. `.forge/chains/` is gitignored working state under the DM-007 layout via the `/.forge/chains/` entry in the init gitignore block; when a run is open, the archived chain file and events log travel with the FR-170 run archive. `chain_id` has the form `c-<UTC compact timestamp>-<4 hex>`; the schema identifier is `forge-chain/1`; `kind` is `commit` (merge chains arrive with their own later spec revision). Top-level keys are exactly `schema`, `chain_id`, `kind`, `state`, `created_at`, `last_event_at`, `inactive_after`, `repo_head`, `policy_source`, `paths`, `staging`, `candidate`, `tier`, `steps`, `review`, `approval`, `authorization`, `commit_result`. `candidate.sha256` is the SHA-256 of the exact `git diff --cached` bytes computed by the CLI after it stages; every evidence record is bound to the candidate hash current when the record was written, and records bound to a stale hash are dead. Commands write the event first, then the materialized state; on startup a divergence is replayed from events, and an irresolvable divergence is an exit-2 frozen chain. The sole exception to event-replay reconstruction is the finalize window: recovery in `committing` observes HEAD per FR-219. The events log is append-only; automation never edits, truncates, or replaces it. `user_skip` records inside `steps` are written only by the operator-bound `forge commit skip` verb and carry `{directed_by, reason, argv_digest, journaled_at}`.

Revision-9 amendment to **DM-012**, also applying to the DM-014 merge-chain family: both materialized chain schemas add the exact top-level keys `run_binding` and `journal_outbox`. `run_binding` is null for an unbound chain or has exactly `run_id`, `task_id`, `repository`, and `policy_digest`; it is established only at start, identifies one active task in the named run, and is immutable for that chain. DM-014's retained top-level `run` projection is null exactly when `run_binding` is null and otherwise equals `run_binding.run_id`; it is never a second authority. `journal_outbox` is null when drained or has exactly `idempotency_key`, `batch_digest`, `record_count`, and `source_event_digest`. A pre-Revision-9 chain file that lacks both added keys reads as `run_binding: null` and `journal_outbox: null`; every Revision-9 write includes both keys explicitly, and any other key-set deviation remains a frozen chain. A consequential event in a run-bound chain carries one `journal_batch` object with exactly `idempotency_key`, `batch_digest`, `record_count`, and `records`; `records` is the ordered array of ordinary FR-010 records constructed by the typed builders.

The event-carried batch has one nonrecursive construction order. The **carrier** is `payload.details` for a DM-012 commit event and `payload` for a DM-014 merge event; this preserves the commit family's exact outer payload keys `at`, `details`, `event`, and `state`. First construct the exact ordinary event with `carrier.source_event_digest` and `carrier.journal_batch` absent, any snapshotted `payload.state.journal_outbox` set to null, and its final `digest` member absent; the family's existing event-digest algorithm over that projection is `source_event_digest`. Next construct every binding against that digest and the canonical records. `batch_digest` is exactly FR-019's `batch_sha256`, the lowercase SHA-256 of the exact ordered LF-terminated canonical record bytes, and the chain batch's `idempotency_key` equals its 64-lowercase-hex `source_event_digest`. Its FR-019 `request_sha256` is the digest of `{"schema":"forge-journal-builder-request/1","verb":"chain outbox-drain","repository":<canonical-repository-identity>,"run_id":<run-id>,"inputs":{"chain_id":<chain-id>,"source_event_digest":<source-event-digest>,"batch_digest":<batch-digest>,"record_count":<record-count>}}`. Then add `source_event_digest`, the complete `journal_batch`, and the matching pending outbox to the carrier/state snapshot and calculate the event's ordinary final digest over the complete augmented event under the unchanged family algorithm. Thus the final digest covers the exact batch, the batch binds its source projection, and no digest is in its own transitive preimage. Replay MUST verify both digests and their construction order. The request digest, idempotency key, batch digest, record count, and exact batch bytes MUST agree across the event-derived request, projected/materialized outbox, FR-019 intent and receipt, and journal suffix. Only the final digest advances the chain head and supplies the next event's previous-digest link; `source_event_digest` is never used for chain linkage. That complete digest-chained event is appended and fsynced before materialized-state projection.

The CLI then drains the identical batch through FR-019's transaction and appends the exact event literal `journal_receipted`; this additively extends each family's event enum, and Revision 13 adds exactly one further commit-family literal, `abort_disposition_recorded` (below). It is a non-consequential acknowledgement, carries no `journal_batch`, and never creates another journal record. Its carrier has exactly `idempotency_key`, `batch_digest`, and `receipt_digest`, where `receipt_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete canonical LF-terminated builder-receipt line. Before append, the CLI verifies the receipt's key, batch digest, and record count against the exact pending outbox; the event clears only that outbox. Replay reconstructs the one pending outbox from an unreceipted event. A pending outbox blocks later state transitions, finalization, task finish, and run close. Gate completions produce `verification`; approvals, skips, and landing facts produce `decision`; no chain event implicitly completes a task.

The existing journal sidecar lock is the outer serialization point for every start or mutation of a run-bound chain and for every typed journal mutation; a chain-state lock is never held while that journal lock is acquired. A run-bound chain operation acquires the journal lock first and retains it through the complete transaction. Its consequential sequence is: acquire the applicable chain lock; append/fsync the event and project its pending outbox; release the chain lock while retaining the journal lock; drain the idempotent batch under that already-held journal lock; reacquire the chain lock; recheck outbox identity and append/fsync `journal_receipted`; release the chain lock; then release the journal lock. Pending-outbox recovery uses the same outer-to-inner order and completes before another transition is admitted.

Under that same journal lock, `journal task-finish` and `run-close` inspect every engine-owned direct-child chain state whose `run_binding` names the task or run, acquiring and releasing at most one applicable chain lock at a time in bytewise chain-ID order. They recheck replay, binding, landing, and null-outbox predicates and append the terminal journal record before releasing the journal lock. `journal task-finish` inspects only chains whose binding names the finishing task; a bound chain naming another task of the same run is skipped there and inspected by `run-close`. An authenticated terminal abort satisfies the landing predicate as an explicit non-landing disposition, carrying its `chain-abort` decision when one was drained (Revision-13 amendment to FR-210/FR-222 below). Because creation and every mutation of a matching bound chain require that same outer lock, no new matching chain or outbox can enter between the terminal check and append. This is the complete Revision-9 cross-store protocol: it uses the already-required journal lock and at most one chain lock at a time, and introduces no persistent close-barrier artifact, cross-chain lock set, or manifest.

For every chain cited by a run, the archive contains a `## Chain evidence` subsection ordered bytewise by chain ID. It renders the materialized-state file as exact UTF-8 bytes in a human-readable Markdown fence. The opening metadata line is exactly `<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-STATE v1 bytes=N sha256=H fence=F -->`; `N` and `H` describe only the raw state bytes and `F` is the decimal deterministic fence width, the greater of 3 and one more than the longest backtick run in those bytes. That line is followed by one LF and a fence of exactly `F` backticks plus `json` and one LF. The renderer then copies exactly `N` bytes, writes one separator LF only when those bytes do not end in LF, writes the closing fence of exactly `F` backticks and one LF, then writes exactly `<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-STATE -->` and one LF. A decoder uses `N`, not the optional separator, to recover the state bytes.

An event log of at most 2 MiB (2,097,152 bytes) is embedded exactly through the inherited marker grammar:

```text
<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE v1 encoding=base64url bytes=N sha256=H -->
<one-line base64url payload without padding>
<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE -->
```

Here the decoded payload is the exact raw event-log bytes, not a JSON wrapper. The opening marker is followed by one LF, the possibly empty unpadded base64url payload and one LF, then the closing marker and one LF; a zero-byte log therefore has an empty payload line. For a larger event log the archive carries no payload and uses exactly:

```text
<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE v1 encoding=UNEMBEDDED bytes=N sha256=H -->
<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE -->
```

For `UNEMBEDDED`, the opening marker is followed by one LF, the closing marker, and one LF with no payload line. The event count, first/last timestamps when present, digest-chain head, selected binding IDs, journal-line mappings, and every discrepancy remain human-readable beside these blocks. `UNEMBEDDED` is a visible size disposition, never permission to omit the digest or authority-bearing materialized state. The complete UTF-8 archive MUST be at most 16 MiB (16,777,216 bytes), checked before destination creation or replacement; overflow refuses rather than truncating. Snapshot each chain with no-follow owner-controlled opens, replay and compare state, hash the exact bytes, and recheck file identities/sizes after rendering; a missing, replaced, or changed authority artifact is fail-closed.

The commit chain started solely to commit `.forge/history/runs/<run-id>.md` through `commit start --archive-run-id <run-id>` is created after that run closed and is therefore expressly exempt from carrying `run_binding`, producing a journal outbox, or embedding itself in the archive it creates. This self-carriage exemption applies only to that one archive-only candidate; every pre-close cited chain remains bound and embedded.

**DM-013**: `env_fingerprint` — the SHA-256 of a canonical sorted-key JSON record `{command_digest, cwd, platform, policy_digest, python_version, repo_head}` attached to every CLI-recorded gate and scan run. It is an identity-and-context record, NOT an independence proof: two back-to-back CLI runs on the same machine are not two independent environments, and no spec text may claim they are — the claim is "the CLI observed both runs". A fingerprint mismatch between the two required gate-1 runs voids the pair: "twice consecutively" means twice in the same observed context. Every evidence record additionally carries the plain-field HEAD observed when it was written, and `commit_result` records head-at-commit, so an archive reader can see which base each gate ran against.

Phase-1.1 fixes the shipped DM-013 normalization exactly as follows:

```text
canonical(x) =
  UTF-8(json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True,
                   separators=(",", ":"),
                   ensure_ascii=False))
```

`canonical(x)` has no trailing LF and no `sha256:` prefix. The two digests are:

```text
command_digest =
  lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(exact subprocess argv array))

preimage = {
  "command_digest": command_digest,
  "cwd": os.path.realpath(repository_root),
  "platform": sys.platform,
  "policy_digest": lowercase_hex_sha256(exact pinned policy bytes),
  "python_version": "<major>.<minor>.<micro>",
  "repo_head": <full current HEAD>
}

env_fingerprint = lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(preimage))
```

No environment mapping, locale, architecture value, or outer Forge invocation contributes. The mismatch trigger examines the two newest current-candidate Gate-1 records without filtering out non-PASS results: only when both adjacent records are PASS, neither has `pair_voided` true, and their fingerprints differ does it mark every unvoided current-candidate Gate-1 PASS, including that adjacent pair, void and require two fresh Gate-1 runs. An intervening non-PASS or an already-voided member therefore prevents that comparison from firing; in particular, the first fresh PASS after a voided PASS does not retrigger mismatch, and two later adjacent unvoided matching PASS records can satisfy Gate 1 without voiding the earlier PASS. The persisted reason is exactly `DM-013 env_fingerprint mismatch voided the Gate-1 pair`.

**DM-014**: Merge-chain state (phase-3 authority; implementation deferred). A merge chain uses the DM-012 chain-ID namespace and storage family — `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.json`, `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.events.jsonl`, and `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/` artifacts — but has the distinct identity:

```text
schema: forge-merge-chain/1
kind: merge
```

Its top-level keys are exactly:

```text
schema, chain_id, kind, state, created_at, last_event_at,
inactive_after, owner, run, repository, worktree, branch, target,
policy_source, candidate, tier, steps, review, approval,
authorization, integration, cleanup
```

`chain_id` retains DM-012's `c-<UTC compact timestamp>-<4 hex>` grammar; `created_at`, `last_event_at`, and `inactive_after` are UTC ISO-8601 strings, with inactivity defaulting to 24 hours after `last_event_at`; `owner` is the initiating session identity; `run` is the explicit run ID or null; `repository` is the main-checkout realpath; `worktree` has exactly `path`, `git_dir`, `common_dir`, and `claim`, where the first three are canonical/absolute identities and `claim` has exactly `status`, `path`, `inode`, and `digest` with status `unpublished`, `owned`, `releasing`, or `released`. `released` means no logical owner. A never-published release retains `inode: null` and the intended-record digest; its authoritative ownership intent plus release events prove that no claim was acquired. An acquired release retains its historical inode/digest. Once that chain's `ownership_released` event is authoritative, its terminal projection and status-idempotence never depend on the reusable pathname's later inode/content and a later claimant is never misclassified as divergence in the released predecessor. The deterministic path may be absent, may still name that chain's same recorded inode/content as a non-owning tombstone, or may name a later strict-valid claimant for the identical worktree. A current claimant is authoritative either when its `ownership_claimed` event matches the inode/content or, in the sole publish-before-event crash window, when the inode/content exactly matches its durable `ownership_intent` plus `unpublished` projection. Its immediate predecessor fields MUST name the most recently released claimant and exact `ownership_released` digest. Slot-lineage validation follows those immutable predecessor edges for at most the number of chain records, requires every referenced chain/digest/worktree identity to match, and rejects cycles, missing edges, or forks; such failure freezes the current claimant/admission but never retroactively corrupts an already released predecessor. `branch` is the full local branch ref; and `target` contains exact remote `origin`, destination `refs/heads/<configured-default-branch>`, and the manifest commit used to discover it. `policy_source` contains the committed policy OID and digest. Before the first successful fixed-tip fetch, `candidate` and `tier` are null and `steps`, `review`, `approval`, and `authorization` have their exact empty shapes; this is the sole pre-generation bootstrap state. The remaining objects hold only the state-specific evidence defined by FR-232..FR-237; absent state-specific values are null or empty arrays/objects, never omitted top-level keys.

Each candidate generation binds fixed values, never symbolic refs:

```text
remote = "origin"
destination_ref = "refs/heads/<configured-default-branch>"
remote_tip = <full fetched OID>
candidate_head = <full worktree HEAD OID>
diff_sha256 = SHA-256(exact bytes of git diff <remote-tip>...<candidate-head>)
policy_commit = <full candidate or integrated HEAD OID>
policy_digest = SHA-256(exact committed forge-project.md bytes)
worktree_identity = {path, git_dir, common_dir}
generation = <positive integer>
```

`generation_digest` is `lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(<the exact nine-field mapping above>))`. The first successful fixed-tip fetch computes and materializes generation 1 as one result; the generation then increments exactly once whenever any of the eight identity fields preceding `generation` changes. Its newly assigned positive integer is included in the nine-field digest and does not recursively trigger another increment. Every gate, review, approval, rebase, push, recovery, and cleanup record carries the generation digest on which it acted. User-facing control approval names `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha>`; the stored approval additionally carries the chain ID and generation digest. When only `remote_tip` changes and FR-235's exact remote-only matrix row proves `candidate_head`, `diff_sha256`, `policy_commit`, `policy_digest`, `worktree_identity`, remote, and destination byte-identical, the implementation MAY retain Gate 3, Gate 4, and control approval only by appending an immutable `generation_carried_forward` event containing both generation digests and that equality proof; records are never rewritten or silently rebound. Any other generation change invalidates them according to FR-235.

The event log retains DM-012's append-only rule and adds, rather than inherits, digest chaining. Every merge event has exactly `schema`, `chain_id`, `sequence`, `at`, `event`, `generation_digest`, `previous_digest`, `payload`, and `digest`; `schema` is `forge-merge-event/1`, `sequence` starts at 1 and increments by one, the first `previous_digest` is 64 ASCII zeroes, every later `previous_digest` equals the immediately preceding event's `digest`, and `digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for the other eight fields. `event` is exactly one of `chain_started`, `ownership_intent`, `ownership_claimed`, `ownership_release_intent`, `ownership_released`, `gate_recorded`, `review_requested`, `review_attached`, `review_disposition`, `approval_recorded`, `generation_refreshed`, `generation_carried_forward`, `epoch_intent`, `fetch_intent`, `fetch_result`, `rebase_intent`, `rebase_conflict`, `rebase_result`, `reverification_result`, `push_intent`, `push_observed`, `cleanup_intent`, `cleanup_result`, `condition_recorded`, `lock_release_result`, `aborted`, or `closed`.

`generation_digest` is normally the current 64-lowercase-hex generation digest. It is null only while `candidate` is null, and in that bootstrap state only `chain_started`, `ownership_intent`, `ownership_claimed`, `fetch_intent`, a failed `fetch_result`, `condition_recorded`, `lock_release_result`, `ownership_release_intent`, `ownership_released`, or `aborted` is legal. The bootstrap `fetch_intent` payload fixes repository/worktree/branch/target, pre-fetch candidate HEAD, policy digest, operation nonce, and attempt number; its event `digest` is the FR-236 fence's `intent_digest`. A successful initial fetch/tip resolution computes the complete candidate tuple before append, and its `fetch_result` carries the new non-null generation-1 digest while atomically materializing that candidate. Every later event carries a non-null digest; a later `fetch_intent` carries the current generation and its successful `fetch_result` carries the retained or successor generation. A state-changing command MUST append and fsync its intent/result event, atomically replace and fsync the materialized state, and fsync the containing directory before reporting persistence; an event/state divergence replays from events unless FR-236 requires observation of Git or the remote.

Revision-12 composite-bootstrap amendment to **DM-014**: This supersedes only the preceding successful-bootstrap timing sentence insofar as it could permit construction of a successful `fetch_result` before the full-patch bytes and their digest exist. The complete nine-field generation MUST exist before the successful `fetch_result` is constructed; in both run-bound and unbound bootstraps its `diff_sha256` MUST equal FR-231/FR-236's authenticated `full_patch_output_digest`, and that event atomically materializes the already-complete immutable generation.

Revision-10 amendment to **DM-014**: every `epoch_intent.payload.delta.integration.epoch` has exactly `operation_nonce`, `generation_digest`, `intent_digest`, `started_at`, and `gate_plan`, and the materialized `integration.epoch` has that same exact member set. `gate_plan` has exactly `status`, `generation_digest`, `policy_digest`, `suite`, `suite_digest`, `cursor`, and `seal_event_digest`. For `status: "unsealed"`, the other six members are null. For `status: "sealed"`, the generation and policy digests are lowercase SHA-256 values, `suite` is the deterministic ordered array below and MAY be empty, `suite_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for `suite`, `cursor` is the zero-based next unrecorded position in `0..len(suite)`, and `seal_event_digest` is the authenticated event digest that sealed the plan. An empty sealed suite with cursor zero is complete and is distinct from an unsealed plan.

A normal `authorized → rebasing` `epoch_intent` MUST carry the complete unsealed object at that exact payload location; omission is invalid. Its hashed payload carries `integration.epoch.intent_digest: null`, and authenticated replay replaces only that slot with the outer event digest, so the event log rather than materialized state alone commits to the initial plan. The existing `reverification_failed → reverifying` retry transition MUST persist a fresh `epoch_intent` carrying a directly sealed full-suite plan before any child because that generation and policy are already known and no later event may seal that retry plan; its hashed `intent_digest` and `gate_plan.seal_event_digest` slots are null and replay fills both with that event's digest.

Exactly one matrix-selection event may change an epoch plan from unsealed to sealed. The closed sealer set is `fetch_result` when no integration result remains to be learned, including a pure-unchanged empty-suite row; `rebase_result` when it materializes the integrated generation and policy; and `generation_carried_forward` when that event itself materializes the final successor generation before reverification. The sealing event's `payload.delta.integration.epoch.gate_plan` carries the complete sealed object with `seal_event_digest: null`; authenticated replay substitutes only the outer event digest and requires every other payload and projected-state plan member byte-identical. It atomically binds its outer generation digest, the current candidate and committed-policy digests, `integration.epoch.generation_digest`, and the plan's generation and policy digests. No other event may seal or replace the plan, and the epoch's immutable identity remains `intent_digest`.

For a nonempty suite, each member has exactly `kind` and `id`. `kind` is `gate` and `id` is the applicable canonical FR-233 gate ID, or `kind` is `scoped-mutation` and `id` is the exact non-gate literal `scoped-mutation`. The order is Gate 1, scoped mutation, applicable stack gates in bytewise category order, applicable invariant gates in increasing numeric row order, then `assertion-sensor`. Before each child, the CLI re-derives that order from the plan's sealed generation and committed policy and requires byte equality plus the same digest. A gate child requires `status: sealed` and `cursor < len(suite)`; push intent requires `cursor == len(suite)`. Thus an empty sealed plan admits the existing no-suite push path but no gate child or `gate_recorded` event.

The cursor selects exactly one child identity. The corresponding `gate_recorded` payload's step delta adds exactly `gate_plan_position`, `gate_intent_digest`, and `inflight_digest`; `gate_plan_position` has exactly `seal_event_digest`, `suite_digest`, `cursor`, `kind`, and `id`. In that same fsynced event/state transition its integration delta advances cursor from `n` to `n + 1`; no skip, unrelated event, or replay may advance it, and a stale projection replays that event. Transporting a `scoped-mutation` position this way does not make mutation a gate or satisfy a gate criterion. A crash after child authorization but before `gate_recorded` retains one exact outstanding position. A legitimate successor generation or policy learned before sealing selects the successor plan; a gate or scoped mutation that changes the tuple after sealing takes FR-235's ordinary reclassification/parking path and clears the epoch. Neither is `frozen-chain`. A missing or replaced plan member, unauthorized sealer, bad digest/cursor, result for another position, or authenticated-event/materialized-state divergence is exact `frozen-chain`.

`integration` has the exact control keys `condition`, `primary_condition`, `epoch`, `remote_movement_count`, `intent`, `observed`, `pre_rebase`, `conflict`, and `push`; evidence payloads under the last five keys are the exact records required by FR-235/FR-236 and are generation-bound except for DM-014's explicit pre-generation fetch bootstrap. `push` is null before the first push intent and otherwise has exactly `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, `result`, `attempted_heads`, and `landed_head`; its current fields describe the latest attempt, `result` is null until classified and then has DM-017's exact result shape, `attempted_heads` is the nonempty append-only array of every full intended HEAD in `push_intent` event order, including a repeated OID for a deliberate later retry, and `landed_head` is null until observation scans that array in reverse and selects the latest member proved contained by the destination. New attempts replace only the current fields and append exactly one head; immutable events retain every historical intent/result. Containment of the current `intended_head` (the last attempted-head member) establishes completion of the current operation only while that OID also equals the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD. Containment of only an older member sets `landed_head` as partial historical truth that forbids abort or blind retry but does not establish `pushed` for the newer unlanded generation. When partial historical truth has already parked a chain, the later epoch's fresh-attempt identity is the digest of its newly appended `epoch_intent`: that event follows the most recent `push_intent`, is unique to the bounded epoch, and remains the current `integration.epoch` until the epoch appends its own push intent or parks. While that exact event ordering holds, the preserved vector is historical evidence but cannot re-park or complete the not-yet-recorded attempt before its push intent is appended; the final observation instead applies the ordinary unchanged/moved/unavailable pre-intent rows to the current generation. This exception is keyed to the durable epoch identity, not OID inequality, so a legitimate no-op integration that again yields the last attempted OID may append one deliberate repeated head for the new epoch. `observed` is null before remote observation and otherwise has exactly `exists`, `oid`, `contains_intended_head`, `attempted_head_containment`, `observed_at`, `inflight_digest`, and `output_digest`. `attempted_head_containment` has one member per `push.attempted_heads` entry in the same order, each with exactly `head` and `contained`. An authoritative present destination uses `exists: true`, a full `oid`, and boolean current plus per-attempt containment; an authoritative absent destination uses `exists: false`, `oid: null`, current containment false, and false for every attempt; failed/unavailable observation uses null for `exists`, `oid`, current containment, and every per-attempt `contained`. `condition` is exactly one of `none`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `remote-moved`, `remote-churn`, `push-failed`, `non-fast-forward`, `push-outcome-unknown`, `lock-release-failed`, or `foreign-git-state`. Its retained state and admitted response are closed:

| `integration.condition` | Retained state | Admitted response beyond `status` |
|---|---|---|
| `none` | the FR-232 state | only the ordinary FR-232 rows |
| `fetch-failed` | `classifying` when start/refresh fetch failed; `authorized` when finalize/recover/final-tip resolution failed | From `classifying`, `merge refresh`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort; from `authorized`, `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `rebase-failed` | `revising` | `merge refresh` below the cap, or safe abort |
| `remote-moved` | `authorized` | `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `remote-churn` | `awaiting_approval` | exact operator `merge approve`, or safe abort |
| `push-failed` | `pushing` | observation-first bare `merge recover`, or FR-232's exact fresh-noncontainment/no-live-process safe abort whose first step is that same authoritative observation |
| `non-fast-forward` | `authorized` | `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `push-outcome-unknown` | `pushing` | observation-only bare `merge recover` until truth is known, or FR-232's exact fresh-noncontainment/no-live-process safe abort after that observation establishes not-landed truth |
| `lock-release-failed` | the durable primary state, including `closed` | bare `merge recover` may prove/retry release only; it never repeats the primary side effect |
| `foreign-git-state` | the state in which it was observed | bare `merge recover` may re-observe only; no mutation occurs until the state matches a different exact FR-236 predicate |

For `lock-release-failed`, `primary_condition` preserves the condition that existed before release failed; otherwise it is `none`. `cleanup.condition` is exactly `none` or `cleanup-failed`; the latter retains `cleanup_pending` and admits only `status` or `merge cleanup`. The v1-only `inactive-chain` and `frozen-chain` preconditions are never broadened for these merge conditions. The closed state enum remains the exact FR-232 enum. The chain file is retained after terminal cleanup and, when a run is open, is archived with its events and evidence under FR-237.

**DM-015**: History-mutation activation adds exactly one anchored line to the committed plugin-schema `.forge-manifest`:

```text
history_mutation_mode: legacy-v1
```

or:

```text
history_mutation_mode: forge-verbs-v1
```

The line's exact anchor is immediately after the single `init_completed: true|false` line and before every `region:` line. On a plugin-schema manifest, no such line means `legacy-v1`; exactly one correctly anchored line with either value is valid; duplicate, empty, misplaced, malformed, or unknown values are invalid and fail closed for raw `git commit` and raw `git push`. Upstream-schema and non-Forge repositories remain under their existing inert/halt-only law. The parser reads strict UTF-8 only from `git show HEAD:.forge-manifest` in each matched Git segment's invoking repository context; working-tree edits and staged deletion cannot change the result. A fresh init writes `legacy-v1`; re-init inserts `legacy-v1` at the exact anchor when the field is absent and preserves either valid existing value byte-for-byte. Init/re-init MUST NOT silently change `legacy-v1` to `forge-verbs-v1`. Activation changes both raw-commit and raw-push treatment atomically and is itself a reviewed, operator-approved control change after the phase-3/4 checks exist.

An invalid mode admits exactly one history-producing repair path: candidate-bound `forge commit finalize` after the normal control review and approval, and only when the staged path set is exactly `.forge-manifest`. Its candidate bytes MUST equal this canonical transform of `HEAD:.forge-manifest`: preserve strict-UTF-8 LF-terminated bytes for every line except remove every line whose first colon-delimited field, after ASCII space/tab trimming, equals `history_mutation_mode`, then insert exactly one valid DM-015 line at the anchor; every other byte and line order is identical. `forge merge finalize`, history-producing `forge push`, raw Git, and any finalize with another staged path or byte change remain denied with FR-239's invalid-mode literal. A strict-valid standalone singleton at exactly `recovery_required` with `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, null `lock_release`, and no live or unresolved fence/process admits this repair-only finalize as the sole exception to FR-238's nonterminal-singleton interlock: finalize revalidates that exact tuple under the commit lock, changes no singleton byte, and commits only the approved canonical repair; every adjacent singleton tuple remains blocked.

The invalid-mode hook also admits `forge push` when and only when `.forge/tmp/push-state.json` is a strict-valid nonterminal singleton, solely in **invalid-mode recovery-only** mode. That mode may prove recorded process-group death, finish exact ordered lock release, perform identity-bound observation/classification of an already-durable fetch/rebase/abort/push/remote-observation intent or result, perform the authoritative remote observation (including its bounded exact-ref object fetch) required to settle an existing push intent, and abort only an exact owned rebase to prove restoration. It MUST NOT start an integration-target fetch, rebase, continue, new attempt, retry, push intent, or push, and MUST NOT replace the singleton; every permitted child is bound to the existing attempt nonce/rebase/push/fence identity. If recovery proves a valid-mode pre-operation HEAD restored and no admitted Git rebase or abort child run by that invocation returned non-PASS, it persists that result and stops with exit-0 `ok` partial recovery and exact next `forge push`. A non-PASS owned-abort child retains the ordinary FR-238 result precedence even when restoration is later proved: exact remaining owned conflict retains and emits `rebase-conflict`; proven restoration records `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt` and emits `rebase-failed`; unmatched attribution records its observation-only condition but likewise emits `rebase-failed` for the actual failure. Recovery-only then stops without starting new work. If an invalid current HEAD remains after all already-started process/result/remote/lock truth is settled, it parks at unwrapped `recovery_required/activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, preserves every prior push/landing fact, clears `attempt_nonce`, stops with FR-239's exact invalid-mode diagnostic and byte-exact `state-precondition`, and enables only the canonical repair finalize above. Existing current-head landed truth may close without another push when locked HEAD is that current intended head; every unresolved condition retains its ordinary exact recovery reason/action. An absent, `closed`, malformed, unreadable, symlinked, identity-mismatched, or side-effect-requesting tuple receives the ordinary invalid-mode denial or exact `frozen-chain` as applicable before mutation. This recovery-only admission produces no history; non-history Forge inspection and chain-preparation verbs remain available but cannot produce history.

The referenced v1 cases retain their original four-field layout and expectations unchanged.

Revision-10 layout for `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json`: its schema is exactly `fr230-phase3-4-manifest/2`, and its top-level members are exactly `schema`, `generation`, `phase`, `previous_manifest_sha256`, `artifacts`, `v1_artifacts`, `subjects`, and `result_bindings`. Generation 1 has `phase: "phase-3"` and `previous_manifest_sha256: null`; generation 2 has `phase: "phase-4"` and `previous_manifest_sha256` equal to the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete generation-1 manifest bytes. `artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted six-member array for the other six DM-016 v2 inventory files, each object having exactly `path` and lowercase-hex `sha256`; the manifest never hashes itself. `v1_artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted three-member array for the reason corpus, hook corpus, and phase-0 manifest named by the recorded hashes below, again with exactly `path` and `sha256`. `subjects` has exactly `production` and `tests`, each a nonempty, unique, path-bytewise-sorted array of repository-relative implementation or test paths.

`result_bindings` is the slot-bytewise-sorted eight-member array for exactly `activated-denial`, `cleanup`, `merge-last-line`, `merge-transition`, `push`, `push-last-line`, `re-verification-matrix`, and `recovery`. Each object has exactly `slot`, `status`, `result_id`, and `result_sha256`. `status` is `PASS` or the exact normative PENDING marker `pending-phase-4`. `PASS` requires a nonempty result ID and the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete mechanically resolved result evidence; the manifest validator MUST resolve that ID, verify those bytes and their PASS outcome, and verify that the result binds the manifested subject candidate. `pending-phase-4` requires both result fields to be null and is permitted in a generation-1 phase-3 manifest only for `push`, `activated-denial`, and `push-last-line`; every other generation-1 slot is a resolved `PASS`. Recording a fictional PASS is forbidden. A pending marker is not PASS evidence and cannot satisfy phase-4 activation or any FR-242 predicate.

Generation 1 MAY ship the phase-3 surface with those three explicit pending slots. The phase-4 activation candidate MUST replace only this manifest with generation 2, preserve the other six v2 artifacts byte-for-byte, bind generation 1 through `previous_manifest_sha256`, replace all three pending slots with resolved `PASS` objects, and leave no pending slot. No other post-phase-3 replacement is authorized. A generation/phase mismatch, bad predecessor digest, missing/extra/duplicate slot, unresolved or digest-mismatched PASS, pending status in another slot, or pending status surviving generation 2 fails closed and authorizes neither phase-3 shipment nor phase-4 activation.

```text
1850257d7899a4c7199e9bcbe12ffd39b0905bb44e49d16348c10e438ea05db7  system/fr223/hook-argv-cases-v1.json
7741b877b1ed45047d680a077c5303b2314cd1f3ef0339821bd7105ac9acd5c9  .forge/evals/tasks/fr223-phase0-v1.manifest.json
```

**DM-017**: Standalone push chain state is the singleton `.forge/tmp/push-state.json`, schema `forge-push/1`; it is the standalone-push chain file for the byte-exact `frozen-chain` corruption precondition even though every envelope uses `chain_id: null`. Its top-level keys are exactly `schema`, `state`, `created_at`, `last_event_at`, `repository`, `remote`, `destination_ref`, `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip`, `rebased_head`, `push_intent`, `observed_remote`, and `recovery`. Its state is exactly one of `preparing`, `rebasing`, `pushing`, `closed`, or `recovery_required`. `repository` is the main-checkout realpath; remote is fixed to `origin`; destination is the committed manifest's full default-branch ref; OID fields are full OIDs when present or null only where their exact state-specific grammar permits, including authoritative destination absence.

`push_intent` is null before the first push and otherwise has exactly `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, `result`, `attempts`, and `landed_head`. The first five fields are the latest attempt. `result` is null until its push child is classified and otherwise has exactly `classification`, `exit`, `inflight_digest`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, and `recorded_at`; classification is exactly `success`, `non-fast-forward`, `known-failure`, or `outcome-unknown`, exit is an integer or null, digests are 64 lowercase hex, and the three flags are boolean. `attempts` is a nonempty append-only array in intent order; every member has exactly `attempt_nonce`, `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, and `result`. Its nonce is 32 lowercase hex and names exactly one DM-017 ledger member; the other five fields equal the corresponding top-level fields for the last member byte-for-byte. A push-intent append copies the then-active recovery nonce into the new member, atomically clears the active field, sets state `pushing`, and normalizes `recovery.condition/next_action` to `none/none`; each ledger nonce names at most one member. Every push-child authorization, including same-epoch reuse of an existing unlaunched intent, requires a durable `pushing` plus `none/none` state replacement first. Duplicated intended-head OIDs are permitted only when a later bounded epoch deliberately retries that head, but their member nonces remain distinct. Result classification atomically updates both the top-level copy and last member and no later observation overwrites either. `landed_head` is null until observation scans the array in reverse and selects the latest attempted intended HEAD proved contained by the destination. It is completion truth only when it equals the current top-level `intended_head` and that OID is also the fresh attempt's locked `rebased_head`; an older-only value is retained partial-landed truth that forbids abort or blind retry while a new attempt reconciles the current checkout. `push_intent` remains present across `start-new-attempt`; a later push intent appends rather than erases history. During any active attempt, the non-null recovery `attempt_nonce`, its last ledger member, and later its rebase intent are the durable identity; a recovery wrapper may replace the immediate condition without changing that identity. Until the identity joins `attempts` or is definitively abandoned, the preserved attempted-head vector contains only prior attempts and cannot close or re-park it. The identity, rather than OID inequality or immediate source condition, controls the exception: successful no-op integration may produce the same OID as the preserved latest member and still append exactly one deliberate repeated member for this new bounded attempt. `observed_remote` is null before observation and otherwise has exactly `exists`, `oid`, `contains_intended_head`, `attempted_head_containment`, `observed_at`, `inflight_digest`, and `output_digest`. `attempted_head_containment` has one member for each distinct attempted intended HEAD in first-appearance order, with exactly `head` and `contained`. An authoritative present destination uses `exists: true`, a full `oid`, and boolean current plus per-attempt containment; a successful query proving the destination ref absent uses `exists: false`, `oid: null`, current containment false, and false for every attempted head; failed/unavailable observation uses null for those three scalar result fields and every per-attempt `contained`. Thus absence is distinguishable from observation failure, a later observation cannot erase any attempt/result, current-attempt containment establishes close truth, and older-only containment takes precedence over retry or abort without falsely completing the newer attempt.

`recovery` has exactly `condition`, `next_action`, `primary_state`, `primary_condition`, `primary_outcome`, `attempt_nonce`, `attempt_history`, `rebase`, `process`, and `lock_release`. `condition` is exactly one of `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-conflict`, `rebase-failed`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved`, `push-failed`, `push-observation-pending`, `push-old-tip-retry`, `non-fast-forward`, `remote-moved`, `push-outcome-unknown`, `lock-release-failed`, or `foreign-process-state`. `next_action` is exactly one of `none`, `start-new-attempt`, `observe-rebase`, `abort-owned-rebase`, `observe-remote`, `retry-push`, `recover-lock-release`, or `operator-quiescence`.
