The ordinary condition/action pairs are respectively `none/none`, `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, `fetch-failed/start-new-attempt`, `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase`, `push-failed/observe-remote`, `push-observation-pending/observe-remote`, `push-old-tip-retry/retry-push`, `non-fast-forward/observe-remote|start-new-attempt`, `remote-moved/start-new-attempt`, `push-outcome-unknown/observe-remote`, `lock-release-failed/recover-lock-release`, and `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence`; every other pair is invalid. `rebase-lock-unavailable` is an envelope-only acquisition refusal: FR-238 preserves an absent or prior singleton byte-for-byte, so neither that spelling nor `acquire-lock` is ever valid in persisted DM-017 state; either on disk makes the singleton schema-invalid exact `frozen-chain`. `activation-policy-invalid` is the no-history terminal-for-this-attempt condition selected when the final intended HEAD fails DM-015 immediately before push intent, or when invalid-mode recovery-only has settled every already-started process/result/remote/lock fact and an invalid current HEAD remains with no live or unresolved child. In the latter case every prior push intent/result and any partial/current `landed_head` remain immutable; the condition authorizes no retry or push. After an exact approved repair changes HEAD, the next invocation starts a wholly new attempt and never reuses the old rebase/push intent. `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase` is the exact recoverable standalone conflict waypoint: the next `forge push` may run only the owned abort, prove restoration, and park for a later fresh attempt; an abort failure outside that recoverable state uses `rebase-failed`. `push-observation-pending` is the exact post-intent state for a retained successful result or no durable result before authoritative remote observation; a retained known failure, non-fast-forward, or outcome-unknown result instead uses its same-named observation action. No refusal is emitted for an internal pending waypoint; authoritative observation first selects the next condition/output. `push-old-tip-retry` is legal after any retained push-result classification or no result when an authoritative reconciliation observation has just proven the exact old tip still current and intended HEAD absent; the result is preserved. “This epoch made no push attempt” means start-pipe proof establishes that no child received authorization for the current durable push intent. That just-completed observation is also the mandatory immediate pre-push recheck; the existing unlaunched current intent authorizes the epoch's sole push after identity revalidation, and no duplicate attempt member or second intent is appended. Once a push child received authorization, the epoch counts as attempted even if no result survived; it MUST release the lock and defer any retry to exact next `forge push`, whose newly acquired epoch appends the deliberate duplicate attempt. A retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery.

`attempt_history` is an append-only array for the lifetime of this singleton transaction and is empty before its first attempt. Every member has exactly `nonce`, `source_condition`, `base_condition`, `started_at`, `prior_push_attempt_count`, and `prior_intended_head`; `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex, `source_condition` is exactly `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `push-old-tip-retry`, `non-fast-forward`, or `remote-moved`, `base_condition` is exactly `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, or `remote-moved`, and `started_at` is UTC ISO-8601. `source_condition` is the condition immediately before attempt admission, with `none` used for an absent/closed singleton or `none/none` recovery waypoint. `base_condition` is the admitted lineage condition after that transition: it is `activation-policy-invalid` for the originating exact approved-repair admission and every exact no-side-effect retry descended from that active ledger identity, `remote-moved` for the originating exact partial-historical admission and every exact no-side-effect retry descended from that active ledger identity, and `none` for every other admission. Its exact current pair is respectively `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, `remote-moved/start-new-attempt`, or `none/none`; a descended retry copies the predecessor member's special `base_condition` and cannot invent or change that lineage. `prior_push_attempt_count` is a nonnegative integer equal to zero when `push_intent` is null and otherwise equal to the then-current `push_intent.attempts` length; `prior_intended_head` is null exactly when that count is zero and otherwise equals the then-current top-level intended HEAD. Immediately before atomically materializing `preparing`, every new attempt draws a nonce absent from every member of this transaction's ledger, appends/fsyncs its complete member, and sets `attempt_nonce` to that nonce with the same state replacement; a later closed-singleton replacement begins a new transaction and a new ledger. The exact approved-activation-repair and partial-historical lineages retain the pair derived from `base_condition` through `preparing`, `rebasing`, and every exact no-side-effect retry as an audit waypoint, while every other new-attempt admission atomically normalizes the current pair to `none/none`; all paths normalize to `none/none` before push authorization. A later observation-only wrapper may replace the pair, but the immutable base condition remains the sole value restored when that wrapper is exactly resolved. This occurs before fetch intent/fence publication, so even a no-side-effect release failure has a durable attempt identity and an exact current condition. A later-epoch old-tip retry that will append a deliberate duplicate push-intent member instead appends/fsyncs a fresh ledger member with `source_condition: push-old-tip-retry` and `base_condition: none` in the same transaction that sets it active and appends that member; the same-epoch retry of an already-recorded unlaunched intent creates neither ledger nor intent duplicate. Every `push_intent.attempts` member names its originating ledger nonce, each ledger nonce binds at most one such member, and its ledger `prior_push_attempt_count` equals that member's zero-based index. On successful fetch, `rebase.operation_nonce` MUST equal the active nonce and its intent digest binds it normally.

`attempt_nonce` is non-null from `preparing` admission until the attempt either appends its push intent or is definitively abandoned, and transiently during the atomic later-epoch retry append defined above. While non-null, the last ledger member MUST match it, its `prior_push_attempt_count` MUST equal the current attempts length (zero when `push_intent` is null), and no existing attempts member may name it. It remains unchanged through `preparing`, `rebasing`, exact owned conflict, and any `foreign-process-state`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved`, or lock-release waypoint still resolving that fetch/rebase/abort identity; once a rebase intent exists for that same ledger identity, its operation nonce MUST also match while the attempt is active. The atomic push-only retry transaction exposes no intermediate on-disk active-nonce state and reuses, without rewriting, the earlier integration's rebase record. This complete active-attempt predicate—not `source_condition`, current condition, or OID inequality—is the historical-vector exception: every existing attempt belongs to an earlier identity, so its containment can be retained but cannot classify, close, or re-park the active attempt. Atomic push-intent append copies the nonce into the new attempts member and clears the active field. Exact fetch failure, final-prepush movement/failure, activation-policy-invalid sink, successful/no-side-effect restoration, or another exact row that abandons the attempt also clears it; the no-side-effect retry row appends a new ledger member and replaces it with that fresh nonce, so the source-independent predicate survives every retry. The ledger itself is never cleared or rewritten before closed-singleton replacement. A missing, duplicate-within-ledger, replaced, prematurely cleared, already-member-bound, or mismatched nonce/ledger/rebase binding is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`; release recovery restores the same active nonce and never creates another attempt.

`primary_state`, `primary_condition`, and `primary_outcome` are null unless `condition == lock-release-failed`. Otherwise they are derived from already-durable ordinary-row or child-result evidence, never independently selected, and are exactly one of these triples: `(preparing, none, no-side-effect)`; `(preparing, activation-policy-invalid|remote-moved, no-side-effect)` only with the exact non-null fresh `attempt_nonce`; `(rebasing, none, none|no-side-effect|rebased)` while a durable fetch/rebase result still awaits ordinary classification; `(rebasing, activation-policy-invalid|remote-moved, none|no-side-effect|rebased)` only with that same fresh `attempt_nonce` and, once rebase intent exists, matching `rebase.operation_nonce`; `(pushing, none, none|push-not-attempted|pushed)` while a durable push result still awaits observation, after a successful pre-push observation whose intent has not executed, or after current containment with a refused close tuple; `(recovery_required, activation-policy-invalid, no-side-effect|rebased|push-not-attempted|push-not-landed|pushed)`; `(recovery_required, fetch-failed, no-side-effect|rebased)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-conflict, none)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-failed, no-side-effect)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-attribution-unresolved, none)`; `(recovery_required, none, no-side-effect|pushed)` after proved owned-abort restoration or current containment with a refused close tuple; `(recovery_required, push-failed|push-observation-pending|push-outcome-unknown|foreign-process-state, none)`; `(recovery_required, push-old-tip-retry, push-not-attempted|push-not-landed)`; `(recovery_required, non-fast-forward, none|push-not-landed)`; `(recovery_required, remote-moved, no-side-effect|rebased|push-not-attempted|push-not-landed)`; or `(closed, none, closed)`. The three push-derived `activation-policy-invalid` outcomes are legal only when invalid-mode recovery-only has classified a pre-existing durable push intent without starting a new integration, rebase, continue, retry, push-intent, or push child; the exact identity-bound remote-observation child permitted by DM-015 is part of that classification and is expressly allowed. `push-not-attempted` binds no-exec proof, `push-not-landed` binds the authoritative all-false attempted-head vector after an attempt, and `pushed` binds authoritative current-head containment retained because the locked HEAD/clean close tuple was not admitted. Here `none` means a durable child result awaits classification or only observation truth is known, `no-side-effect` is an exact no-effect/restoration predicate, `rebased` is the rewritten integrated result, `push-not-attempted` requires durable push intent plus no-exec proof, `push-not-landed` requires authoritative current-head noncontainment after an attempt, `pushed` requires authoritative current-head containment, and `closed` is the completed close row. `rebase-lock-unavailable` and every unlisted triple are invalid; an unlisted on-disk tuple makes the singleton schema-invalid exact `frozen-chain`. Release recovery restores the exact state/condition, `attempt_nonce`, and append-only attempt ledger, clears all three primary fields, and applies only its ordinary classification/observation transition without creating another attempt, regenerating its nonce, or replaying the recorded child or classified primary side effect.

`lock_release` is null unless `condition == lock-release-failed`; otherwise it has exactly `pending_layers`, `commit_lock`, `common_lock`, and `recorded_at`. `pending_layers` is the nonempty ordered array `["commit-lock", "common-lock"]` or `["common-lock"]`. `commit_lock` has exactly `path`, `device`, `inode`, `digest`, `owner_pid`, and `released`; `common_lock` has exactly `intent_path`, `device`, `inode`, `digest`, and `released`. Paths are canonical absolute paths, device/inode/owner values are nonnegative integers, digests are lowercase SHA-256, and each `released` is boolean. The commit-lock object preserves the last validated acquired identity even after its release; `pending_layers` equals exactly the objects whose `released` value is false and preserves inner-before-outer order. A standalone push defines the acquired FR-219 commit lock as the inner layer of its complete rebase transaction lock: failure to release either layer therefore satisfies the unchanged v2 precondition “Rebase lock could not be explicitly released after the primary outcome was recorded.” Recovery retains the common portable owner while it uses FR-219's serialized ownership/stale-owner protocol to prove and release the exact commit-lock layer first, updates and fsyncs `lock_release`, and only then releases the common layer. A foreign/live/replaced identity is never removed; the record and outer owner remain fail-closed for a later recovery. When both releases are proven, recovery clears `lock_release` with the primary fields and restores the ordinary primary tuple. Outside `lock-release-failed`, any non-null `lock_release` is schema-invalid.

`rebase` is null before rebase intent and otherwise has exactly `operation_nonce`, `action_prefix`, `intent_digest`, `branch_reflog_baseline`, and `head_reflog_baseline`. Each baseline has exactly `path`, `exists`, `size`, and `digest`: `path` is the canonical contained location resolved by `git rev-parse --git-path logs/<full-ref-or-HEAD>`; when `exists` is true it MUST be a nonsymlink regular file, `size` is its nonnegative exact byte length, and `digest` is lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes; when false, `lstat` at that contained path MUST return absence, `size` is 0, and `digest` is null. The operation nonce permanently equals the originating ledger member's nonce and, only while that attempt remains active, also equals the current already-fsynced `attempt_nonce`; clearing or replacing the active field never rewrites an extant rebase record. The action prefix is exactly `forge-push-rebase:<operation-nonce>:<pre-operation-head>:<fetched-tip>`, and the intent digest is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for the exact mapping `{repository, destination_ref, pre_operation_head, fetched_tip, operation_nonce, branch_reflog_baseline, head_reflog_baseline}`.

`process` is null before any fenced child, provisional stale-lock recovery, abort attempt, current attribution, or retained attribution history exists and otherwise has exactly `lock_owner_digest`, `lock_owner_host`, `lock_owner_pid`, `lock_owner_dead_at`, `inflight_digest`, `inflight_operation`, `inflight_intent_digest`, `host`, `pgid`, `group_dead_at`, `results`, `abort_attempts`, `attribution_history`, `attribution`, and `quarantine`. The four `lock_owner_*` fields are all null except during FR-235 provisional recovery, when they bind the strict-valid stale physical owner and its proved death. `inflight_digest`, `inflight_operation`, `inflight_intent_digest`, `host`, and `pgid` are all null until a fence is selected and otherwise bind that exact fence: the operation is exactly `fetch`, `tip-resolution`, `remote-observation`, `rebase`, `abort`, `push`, or `attribution-observation`, the two digests are 64 lowercase hex, and `group_dead_at` remains null until that selected group is proved dead. These are the complete standalone operation literals; `gate`, `continue`, `containment`, `worktree-remove`, `branch-delete`, or any other operation in a standalone process/fence/result is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`. After a parent crash without a durable result, the exclusive recovery owner MUST persist and fsync all six selected-fence identity/death fields before unlinking the fence; they cannot be cleared or replaced until the exact operation-and-intent-specific immutable result or recovery condition is durable. `results` is the empty array before the first completed/failed child observation and otherwise is append-only in fence-publication order. Every member has exactly `operation`, `intent_digest`, `inflight_digest`, `exit`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, and `recorded_at`; `operation` is one of that exact seven-member standalone set, both digests are 64 lowercase hex, `exit` is an integer or null, the three flags are boolean, and `recorded_at` is UTC ISO-8601. The result is appended and fsynced before its fence may be removed, is never overwritten by a later result/observation, and is selected for recovery only by its exact operation, intent digest, and fence digest. Push classification is additionally copied into the immutable `push_intent.result` member required above.

`abort_attempts` is empty before the first owned abort and otherwise is append-only in attempted order. Every member has exactly `abort_nonce`, `attempt_nonce`, `rebase_intent_digest`, `intent_digest`, `started_at`, and `no_exec_at`. Both nonces are 32 lowercase hex; `abort_nonce` is fresh from 128 operating-system-RNG bits and unique in this singleton transaction, `attempt_nonce` names the originating DM-017 attempt-history member, `rebase_intent_digest` copies that attempt's then-durable rebase intent, `started_at` is UTC ISO-8601, and `no_exec_at` is null unless start-pipe/fence proof establishes that no child was authorized, when it is the UTC proof time and never changes again. For a non-null active `attempt_nonce`, its current abort member is exactly the last array member whose `attempt_nonce` equals that active nonce, or is absent when no such member exists. Only that member may control current abort classification, attribution selection, envelope output, or admission of a later abort; every earlier member, whether for the same or another attempt, is immutable history and MUST NOT control those decisions. Before every owned abort fence, including a retry after an exact remaining conflict, the CLI appends and fsyncs a fresh member whose `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-push-abort-intent/1", repository, destination_ref, attempt_nonce, rebase_intent_digest, abort_nonce}`; that digest alone authorizes the fence/result and the append makes this member current. A crash after this append but before fence publication is settled before any later child: under both complete locks, the next invocation MUST verify the canonical fence is absent, no matching result, selected-fence/history, or quarantine member exists, the singleton's selected-fence fields do not name this intent, and replay contains no sanctioned fence removal. FR-236's fsynced-publication-before-authorization invariant then proves that no start byte could have been sent; the invocation atomically sets and fsyncs this current member's `no_exec_at` before Git/reflog classification. If any part is ambiguous, this settlement is not admitted and ordinary fence/foreign-state recovery remains fail-closed. Another abort member is forbidden until the current member is settled by a unique matching immutable result, a unique selected-fence attribution-history member after proved group death/quarantine, or non-null `no_exec_at`, and no live/unresolved fence remains. Exact conflict may then admit a fresh nonce; nonce reuse, a result/fence bound to the wrong member, multiple results for one member, out-of-order authorization, or an unproved unresulted predecessor is exact `frozen-chain`.

`attribution_history` is empty before the first attribution wrapper and otherwise is an append-only array whose members have exactly `attempt_nonce`, `operation_nonce`, `operation`, `source`, `intent_digest`, and `inflight_digest`. Both nonces are 32 lowercase hex and bind immutable provenance: for `rebase`, both equal the originating attempt-history nonce and the intent digest is the rebase-intent snapshot validated when the member is first appended; for `abort`, `attempt_nonce` names that originating ledger member, `operation_nonce` equals its unique `abort_attempts.abort_nonce`, and the intent digest equals that abort member. The retained member remains authoritative after the replaceable singular `rebase` field is cleared; validation thereafter uses its immutable nonce/ledger or abort-attempt binding, not equality to a later current rebase record. Operation is exactly `rebase` or `abort`; source is exactly `selected-fence`, `result`, or `pre-exec`; selected-fence/result copies the intent and inflight digests byte-for-byte from that exact evidence and requires a non-null 64-lowercase-hex inflight digest. `pre-exec` requires null inflight digest plus durable no-publication/no-authorization proof: for rebase it binds the then-current rebase-intent snapshot, while for abort it binds the exact abort member and requires that member's non-null `no_exec_at`. Materialization is deterministic for the exact nonce-bound operation identity: a unique matching immutable result MUST select `result`; only its absence permits the exact selected original-operation fence to select `selected-fence`; only absence of both permits byte-for-byte reuse of the unique matching history member; and only absence of all three plus the applicable durable no-exec proof permits `pre-exec`. Any ambiguity, precedence violation, duplicate member, or conflicting member for one nonce-bound operation identity is exact `frozen-chain`. A newly selected object is appended/fsynced exactly once in the same state replacement that first creates its wrapper; reuse never appends a duplicate, and history is never cleared before closed-singleton replacement.

`attribution` is null outside the enumerated wrapper tuples below and otherwise MUST be non-null and byte-for-byte equal to the deterministic six-key history member. The mandatory tuples are `condition == rebase-attribution-unresolved`; `condition == foreign-process-state` with selected `inflight_operation == attribution-observation` and exact matching observation-intent digest; and `condition == lock-release-failed` with `primary_condition == rebase-attribution-unresolved`, or with `primary_condition == foreign-process-state` plus that exact selected attribution-observation identity. Current attribution remains unchanged while that later observation fence runs, through its foreign-process or exact release wrapper, and until an operation-context row resolves the restored wrapper; clearing it never clears history, so a later unmatched state deterministically rematerializes the same source identity. A later remote- or attribution-observation result therefore cannot erase a nonzero/timeout/launch result for standalone rebase or abort, and FR-238's success/conflict/failure predicates always inspect the immutable result bound to the operation intent plus the exact attribution identity.

`quarantine` is the empty array before operator-reserved recovery and otherwise is a nonempty append-only array in completed recovery-stage order. Every member has exactly `kind`, `manifest_digest`, `artifacts`, and `quarantined_at`; `kind` is `portable-owner`, `inflight-fence`, or `recovery-reservation`, `quarantined_at` is UTC ISO-8601, and `artifacts` is a nonempty source-path-sorted array whose members have exactly `source_path`, `quarantine_path`, `file_type`, `device`, `inode`, `mode`, `size`, and `content_digest`. Each member is one top-level node actually renamed; nested directory entries are bound by its directory digest rather than duplicated as artifacts. The paths are absolute and contained in the recorded common directory; `file_type` is `regular`, `directory`, or `symlink`; and device/inode/mode/size are nonnegative integers. For a regular file or symlink, `content_digest` is lowercase SHA-256 of file bytes or symlink-target bytes. For a directory it is SHA-256 of the DM-013-canonical source-name-sorted nonrecursive entry array whose members have exactly `name`, `file_type`, `device`, `inode`, `mode`, `size`, and `content_digest`; entry `file_type` is `regular`, `directory`, `symlink`, or `other`, and its content digest is the regular-byte/symlink-target digest or null for directory/other. `manifest_digest` avoids a self-referential quarantine name: it is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical `{kind, artifacts}`, where each artifacts member is projected to the same exact fields except `quarantine_path`; each actual quarantine path is exactly its `source_path` with `.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` appended to that source basename in the same directory. Distinct sources therefore cannot collide. Under engaged halt the operator re-proves repository-wide quiescence and revalidates source identity plus destination absence immediately before each same-directory rename; an existing destination or any source change stops, so the protocol does not depend on a nonportable no-replace directory-rename primitive. Each successfully fsynced stage appends one member without overwriting earlier owner, reservation, or fence manifests, so a standalone recovery can bind sequential multi-kind quarantine. A persisted owner/group-death, quarantine, or child-result observation binds the exact physical-owner and in-flight identities and retains the timeout/output-limit/launch disposition. Before each fetch, rebase, push, or recovery side effect the CLI atomically writes and fsyncs the corresponding intent state and its directory. A nonterminal singleton is recovered by observation before a new operation can start; a prior `closed` record may be atomically replaced only after the next invocation acquires the complete common lock, re-reads the singleton, and passes preflight again. Malformed, unreadable, symlinked, or schema-invalid singleton state is exact corrupt-chain-file `frozen-chain`, not `state-precondition`, and is never overwritten automatically.

Revision-13 amendment to **DM-016**: hook-argv authority gains a third additive generation with an explicit, bounded supersession channel. `system/fr223/hook-argv-cases-v3.json` has exactly the top-level members `schema`, `v2`, `supersedes`, `case_count`, and `cases`. `schema` is `fr223-hook-argv/3`. `v2` has exactly `path`, `schema`, `sha256`, and `case_count` with values `system/fr223/hook-argv-cases-v2.json`, `fr223-hook-argv/2`, `310bfda5efdbfe3c99a1d189c8ff336782f90a79af741c4098ada4ae579bde27`, and `18`; the 18 v2 cases and, through v2, the 112 v1 cases are consumed by reference and MUST NOT be copied into `cases`. `supersedes` is an id-bytewise-sorted array whose members each have exactly `id`, `generation`, `expect`, and `reason`: `id` names exactly one referenced v1 or v2 case, `generation` names that case's schema, `expect` is the replacement expectation, and `reason` is the exact emitted denial literal when the replacement is a denial. A supersession changes only the named case's expectation; the referenced artifact bytes, the case's command, and every other referenced case stay byte-identical, and a later generation MAY supersede a supersession only by naming the same id again. Revision 13's sole member supersedes `no-match-bare-assignment-without-env` (`fr223-hook-argv/1`) to `deny-approve` with the exact `commit approve` operator-verb literal. The additive `case_count` is exactly `12`, the effective count is 142, and every additive case has exactly `id`, `command`, `activation`, `expect`, and `reason` with the v2 field grammar, `expect` drawn from the union of the v1 and v2 enums, and ids that collide with no referenced case. The twelve ids are `deny-<verb>-bare-<prefix>` for `<verb>` in `approve`, `merge-approve`, `skip` and `<prefix>` in `assignment-then-env-assignment`, `multiple-assignments`, `quoted-assignment-space`, `single-assignment`. The v3 eval fixture `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-hook-argv-matcher-v3.md` plants an `env`-only prefix skip and its `.result` baseline BLOCK MUST be recorded from a fresh `review-cheap` execution with the oracle section withheld; `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-hook-argv-v3.manifest.json` (`fr223-hook-argv-manifest/3`, members exactly `schema`, `artifacts`, `clauses`) pins the corpus, fixture, and result digests under one `hook-argv-supersession-v3` clause. Tests that replay referenced cases MUST apply the supersession map before asserting; the guard consumes v3 through its tests and evals only, since the supersession restores a code-level literal already pinned by FR-221 and introduces no runtime corpus read.

Revision-9 amendment to **DM-016**: Revision-9 reason-code authority is another additive, immutable corpus generation; it does not edit or remint either v1 or v2. Its exact inventory is:

```text
system/fr223/reason-codes-v3.json
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v3.md
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v3.result
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-archive-fidelity-v3.manifest.json
```

The corpus schema is exactly `fr223-reason-codes/3`. It contains the complete sorted 53-member `forge-cli/2` union: every 41-member v2 `{code, exit_class, precondition}` row byte-identical and the twelve §8 Revision-9 additions. Tests consume, rather than copy, v1 and v2, prove their existing artifacts and rows unchanged, and then prove the v3 union. The v3 `.manifest.json` is only an immutable eval-corpus inventory under the existing DM-016 discipline; it is not a run-close, archive-authentication, or legacy-recovery manifest. Fixture-family immutability applies once these v3 baselines land; a future change requires another additive generation.

Revision-10 amendment to **DM-016**: the new `run-scope-exceeded` reason requires this exact additive v4 inventory; it edits or remints no v1, v2, or v3 artifact:

```text
system/fr223/reason-codes-v4.json
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.md
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.result
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.manifest.json
```

The corpus schema is exactly `fr223-reason-codes/4`, with exactly `schema` and `codes`; `codes` is the complete bytewise-code-sorted 54-member `forge-cli/2` union, every v3 row byte-identical plus only the §8 Revision-10 row. The manifest schema is exactly `fr223-reason-code-enum-manifest/4`, with exactly `schema`, `artifacts`, `predecessors`, and `result_bindings`. `artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted three-member array for the other three v4 files, each object having exactly `path` and lowercase-hex `sha256`; the manifest never hashes itself. `predecessors` is ordered by `reason_generation` 1, 2, 3, and each object has exactly `reason_generation`, `member_count`, `corpus_path`, `corpus_schema`, `corpus_sha256`, `manifest_path`, `manifest_schema`, and `manifest_sha256`. Generation 1 binds 25 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v1.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/1`, corpus hash `3646227d8437789e0407117dc09e00d6116edccb63e89354c746d4b9059c264b`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-phase0-v1.manifest.json`, `fr223-phase0-manifest/1`, and manifest hash `7741b877b1ed45047d680a077c5303b2314cd1f3ef0339821bd7105ac9acd5c9`. Generation 2 binds 41 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v2.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/2`, its hash from the generation-1 phase-3 manifest, that complete `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json` generation-1 digest, and manifest schema `fr230-phase3-4-manifest/2`. Generation 3 binds 53 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v3.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/3`, corpus hash `13256ac9c9003205510866bc1fb295d11906dd6747b2ea2b94fb9733d6f5864e`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-archive-fidelity-v3.manifest.json`, `fr223-archive-fidelity-manifest/3`, and manifest hash `6a659fbfcc36480782da00fb1b479fd64be779f51f3945f1aed9309ef77e8b20`.

`result_bindings` is a one-member array whose sole object has exactly `slot`, `status`, `result_id`, and `result_sha256`, whose values are `reason-code-enum-v4`, `BLOCK`, `fr223-reason-code-enum-v4`, and `09a6c264d3c4abdcb411b1659573ef239dad5df6f71760e7f44d8b36d73bf387`, the digest of complete `BLOCK\n` result bytes. Validation resolves the matching prompt/result, requires its `expected_verdict: BLOCK`, and checks the manifested corpus. Phase-3 shipment conjunctively requires a valid generation-1 v2 manifest and this v4 companion whose generation-2 predecessor manifest digest equals those complete v2 bytes; v4 cannot fill or replace a v2 result slot. Phase 4 preserves all four v4 files byte-for-byte, and the generation-2 v2 manifest's `previous_manifest_sha256` MUST equal that same v4-recorded generation-1 digest while satisfying the ordinary no-PENDING proof.

---

## 7. Functional Requirements

### Plugin packaging (FR-001..FR-006)

- **FR-001** (MUST): The plugin MUST ship `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` with `name: "forge"` and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` listing the plugin with `source: "./"`, so skills surface as `/forge:init`, `/forge:workflow`, `/forge:orchestrate`, `/forge:report`, `/forge:commit`, `/forge:worktree-merge`, `/forge:drift`, `/forge:learn`. The plugin MUST also ship `hooks/hooks.json` registering the PreToolUse commit guard, the PostToolUse invariant guard on Edit/Write, the Stop union of `scripts/forge/aggregate-telemetry.sh` plus `scripts/forge/drift-staleness.sh`, and SessionStart `scripts/forge/drift-staleness.sh`; a plugin-load smoke test MUST verify every registered hook is discovered.
- **FR-002** (MUST): The plugin MUST contain exactly eight skill directories under `skills/` (`init`, `workflow`, `orchestrate`, `report`, `commit`, `worktree-merge`, `drift`, `learn`), each a `SKILL.md` with `name` and `description` frontmatter, and MUST NOT contain a `commands/` directory.
- **FR-003** (MUST): The vendored engine MUST live at upstream-identical relative paths: `scripts/codex_orch_tools.py`, `scripts/codex_orchestrator/{__init__,cli,journal,events,monitor}.py`, `tests/`, `docs/orchestration-contract.md`. Every deliberate in-file change to a vendored file MUST carry an inline `# forge: modified from upstream — <reason>` (Python/shell) or `<!-- forge: modified from upstream — <reason> -->` (markdown) marker.
- **FR-004** (MUST): An `UPSTREAM` file at the plugin root MUST record, for each upstream (nixlim/forge, alexzh3/codex-orchestrator): repository URL, vendored commit SHA, vendoring date, and a list of deliberate deviations (one line each). Every FR in this spec that deviates from an upstream behavior MUST have a corresponding deviation line.
- **FR-005** (MUST): All runtime code MUST run on Python ≥ 3.10 with the standard library only; shell scripts MUST run under bash on macOS (BSD userland) and Linux. `pyproject.toml` remains a dev-tooling-only file (ruff config, no build-system, no dependencies).
- **FR-006** (MUST): Every skill, hook, and agent file MUST reference plugin files only via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`; no absolute paths may appear in any shipped file (upstream forge-init's hard-coded `/Users/...` paths are not carried over).

### Vendored engine behavior (FR-010..FR-019)

- **FR-010** (MUST): The journal schema MUST remain exactly the upstream seven entry types (`run_started`, `task`, `execution`, `execution_result`, `verification`, `decision`, `run_closed`) with upstream enums (`TERMINAL_TASK_STATUSES`, `TERMINAL_EXECUTION_STATUSES`, `VERIFICATION_RESULTS`, `judgment ∈ {passed, blocked}`). No entry type or enum value may be added or removed. FR-019 is an append-source validation control for newly written records, not a new journal dialect, type, enum, or historical-validation rule.
- **FR-011** (MUST): `validate <run_dir>` without `--gates` MUST preserve all 23 upstream checks, the `{ok, issues, warnings, non_passing_verifications}` payload (sorted keys, no `profile` key), and exit codes (0 when `ok`, 1 otherwise). A journal bearing an FR-016 declaration is the sole exception: FR-016 reclassifies its enumerated pre-declaration diagnostics to warnings in both plain and `--gates` validation. Historical scanning, `read_journal`, and `validate_run` MUST NOT invoke FR-019's proposed-record validator; sparse legacy records continue scanning and validating byte-identically, while FR-016 never relaxes a record newly submitted for append.

Revision-13 citation-root amendment to **FR-011** (bead forge-plugin-7t0): the file-existence checks that `validate` and the run-close validation apply to `execution.prompt`, `execution.events`, `execution.handoff`, `execution_result.handoff`, and `verification.evidence[<n>]` MUST resolve a relative citation against the run directory first (the upstream resolve-then-exist reading, unchanged) and, only when it is absent there, against the run's repository root through the same ordered resolve-then-contain predicate FR-017 applies at append time, so a gate verification drained by a run-bound chain that cites repository-relative `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/evidence/…` validates without a hand-made mirror under the run directory. The repository root is derived only from the fixed run layout `<repository>/.codex-orchestrator/runs/<run-id>` after resolving the run directory physically (a symlinked run directory therefore derives the repository that physically contains it); journal data never widens it, a citation whose resolved path escapes the repository root (symlink or `..`) never satisfies the repository leg, an absolute citation keeps its upstream run-only reading, and a citation absent from both roots reports the unchanged upstream diagnostic. The FR-016 missing-file legacy legs consult both roots before tolerating. A consumer that validates a mirrored journal outside the fixed layout MUST pass the real run's layout-derived repository root explicitly (`validate_run(…, repository=…)`); the archive's FR-024 pre-close recompute does, so a run that closes `passed` in layout re-validates identically in the archive's mirror. The repository leg is a named control (`VALIDATION_REPOSITORY_LEG`) with a disable-in-memory proof that restores run-relative-only resolution for both the layout root and the explicit override.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-011**: every journal reader takes the stable per-run batch sidecar lock in shared mode before inspecting the intent, opens `journal.jsonl` no-follow, records its device, inode, and size, reads and `fstat`s the same descriptor, and rechecks the intent plus identity and size before using the bytes. A pending intent, inode replacement, or size change discards the read and refuses exactly `forge: journal read refused — pending or changed batch transaction`; it never returns a partial snapshot. Readers MUST NOT append, repair, unlink, write a receipt, or perform recovery. This locked refusal is the only Revision-9 change to reading; the four-key plain payload and all historical semantics above remain unchanged when no transaction is pending.
- **FR-012** (MUST): The `state` and `monitor` subcommands MUST preserve upstream behavior: state statuses `idle|starting|active|complete|failed|unknown`, exit 2 on low parse confidence with the incompatibility message on stderr; monitor payload types `codex_agent_complete|codex_agent_failed|codex_agent_unknown|codex_agent_stale|monitor_error`, mtime-based staleness (default 600 s), selector exclusivity, and exit-code rules.
- **FR-013** (MUST): The vendored unittest suite MUST pass via `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` from the plugin root. Doc-contract assertions MUST be migrated, not deleted: each of the 24 upstream assertions is either retained (engine prose unchanged), updated to the merged skill/doc text, or removed with a line in `UPSTREAM` naming the assertion and why.
- **FR-014** (MUST): Before opening a run, the workflow skill MUST declare its nonempty intended repository file scope and use DM-011's locked two-phase reconciliation to compare it atomically with every open run and every derived scope-reserving retired leaf. An ordinary run is admitted only when its scope is disjoint from all such reservations. Each open-run overlap retains exactly `forge: new run refused — scope overlap between <new-run-id> and open run <open-run-id>`; each retired-leaf overlap refuses exactly `forge: new run refused — scope overlap between <new-run-id> and scope-reserving retired run <retired-run-id>`. All conflict lines form one list sorted bytewise by the named conflicting run ID. A user-designated successor MAY reuse scope only when its named immediate predecessor is the scope-reserving retired leaf whose reservation is atomically transferred: otherwise it MUST refuse exactly `forge: successor run refused — predecessor <predecessor-id> is not a scope-reserving retired run`, and a proposed successor scope that does not overlap that predecessor MUST refuse exactly `forge: successor run refused — scope of <new-run-id> does not overlap scope-reserving retired run <predecessor-id>`. A valid successor and its later readmissions are exempt from every retired ancestor in their own transitive lineage, but not from unrelated open or retired-leaf reservations; the immediate predecessor's existing foreign-live-owner refusal remains byte-identical. Historical forks, second-child refusal, passed/blocked release, rollback preservation, and dangling/cyclic fail-closed behavior are as derived by DM-011 and FR-192. Except for DM-011's one silent empty-placeholder class and its named nonempty-orphan refusal, missing, malformed, ambiguous, or inconsistent persisted lifecycle, identity, scope, journal, or registry state MUST fail closed with `forge: new run refused — run registry unavailable`. Every `task.files` entry MUST be contained by the admitted scope; widening requires fresh admission under the same lock before any append or mutation.
- **FR-015** (MUST): Run initialization MUST keep the upstream local-exclude protocol: append `/.codex-orchestrator/` to `git rev-parse --git-path info/exclude`, verify with `git check-ignore`, and never edit tracked `.gitignore` for the run root.
- **FR-016** (MUST): Legacy journal compatibility posture. A journal opts pre-existing records into dialect tolerance only through a `decision` entry whose `id` is exactly `journal-dialect-compat` and whose `resolution` is exactly `legacy-dialect-compat: <justification>` with a nonempty single-line justification (CR/LF forbidden). Tolerance applies solely to records on physical lines before the first such declaration; every record at or after it, including `run_closed`, validates fully strict, and a journal without a declaration validates byte-identically to FR-011/FR-020. Exactly ten diagnostics may be reclassified from issues to line-numbered warnings, each emitted per application plus one activation warning — except leg (7), which emits exactly one consolidated warning per tolerated duplicate ID naming every occurrence line: (1) `observation` entry types; (2) `verification` results of `pass`, or an absent `result` with `status: pass`, normalized to `passed` — a present-but-malformed `result` is never rescued; (3) nonempty string `evidence` normalized to a singleton list before the existence check; (4) `execution_result` statuses mapped exactly `handoff-ready`→`complete`, `pass`→`complete`, `block`→`blocked`; (5) execution/result task mismatches only when both records precede the declaration and both task IDs name known tasks, the execution's task being authoritative; (6) referenced prompt/events paths that do not exist (including the historical `(inline)` sentinel) — wrong types and existing non-file paths stay hard; (7) duplicate `verification` IDs only when every occurrence precedes the declaration; (8) executions with no terminal `execution_result`; (9) empty-string `events` references; (10) failed gate verifications lacking a later identical-criterion passing recheck. Leg (8) also governs FR-021's unterminated-mutation gate veto: a pre-declaration mutating execution with no terminal result does not veto gate verifications, a pre-declaration terminal result still anchors gate ordering (gates recorded before it stay vetoed), and a post-declaration unterminated mutating execution vetoes every gate. Nothing else is tolerated: JSON decodability, run lifecycle placement, `judgment` values, citation-correction grammar and targets, duplicate `decision` IDs (including a second declaration), task terminality, and every unlisted diagnostic stay hard for all records. Raw records remain authoritative for lifecycle and citation checks; validator-assigned line numbers are never taken from journal content; `non_passing_verifications` reporting is unchanged by tolerance. Each tolerance leg MUST be independently disableable in code with a focused test that fails when it is disabled, and extending the tolerated set is a control-class change requiring binding review and explicit operator approval. For a journal that is already closed — where no declaration is appendable — FR-018's operator-directed flag is the sole out-of-band keying for this posture.
- **FR-017** (MUST): Append-time citation-root enforcement. `run-open`, `journal-append`, and `run-close` MUST validate every path-like citation in the proposed record before writing anything: `execution.prompt`, `execution.events`, `execution.handoff`, `execution_result.handoff`, each `verification.evidence[]` entry, each path-like `decision.basis[]` entry, and each path token of `verification.observation` — the complete set of citation surfaces the commitment audit later checks; append-time coverage and audit-time coverage MUST remain exactly coextensive, and extending one without the other is a defect. Path-likeness MUST be decided by the same committed tokenizer the commitment audit uses (`scripts/forge/commitment_paths.py`, with its per-surface contexts) — one predicate, both enforcement points, never a second implementation — and the containment decision MUST be the audit's own predicate applied identically at both points: an absolute citation is refused outright (in-root content is always expressible relatively), and a relative citation is resolved with symlinks followed (resolve-then-contain) against the run directory first and the repository root second. A violating record MUST refuse with exactly `forge: journal append refused — record cites path outside run or repository: <field>: <path>`, where `<field>` is `execution.prompt`, `execution.events`, `execution.handoff`, `execution_result.handoff`, `verification.evidence[<n>]`, `decision.basis[<n>]`, or `verification.observation token <token>`, and write nothing, so the author corrects the citation at the only moment it is still correctable. Non-path basis and observation content (record IDs, prose) is untouched; the journal schema and upstream reader behavior remain unchanged under FR-010/FR-011. The enforcement MUST be independently disableable in code with a focused test that fails when it is disabled in memory.
- **FR-018** (MUST): Operator-directed closed-run dispensation. A closed journal cannot carry a new declaration or citation-correction (`run_closed` is final), so the only sanctioned relaxations for closed, otherwise-unarchivable runs are explicit operator-directed flags with always-visible output. (a) `validate --gates --closed-legacy-compat "<justification>"` — nonempty single-line justification, CR/LF forbidden, mirroring FR-016's grammar — MUST treat the closed journal as pre-declaration under FR-016's posture with the virtual declaration point immediately before `run_closed`: the identical ten legs with identical semantics for every earlier record, one activation warning naming the operator-supplied justification, and `run_closed` itself always fully strict, preserving FR-016's guarantee that no keying — in-journal or flag — can relax it. The flag MUST refuse on a journal without `run_closed` with exactly `forge: closed-legacy-compat refused — journal has no run_closed entry` (for open runs the in-journal declaration remains the only path), and without the flag validation MUST remain byte-identical to FR-011/FR-016. (b) The commitment audit and the archive renderer MUST accept repeatable `--dispense-citation` targets in exactly two forms mirroring FR-191's correction grammar — `<decision-id> basis[<n>]` and `<verification-id> observation: <token>` — plus one required `--dispense-reason "<text>"` whose grammar is identical to (a)'s justification: nonempty, single line, CR/LF forbidden. Target parsing is exact-match on those two shapes; a target whose record ID is ambiguous (duplicate IDs in the journal) MUST be refused as ambiguous rather than resolved by position. Exactly the named missing citations degrade from the fail(5) refusal to a `## Dispensed Citations` section listing each citation, its unresolved path, and the reason; every non-dispensed missing citation of either kind still fails(5); a dispensation naming a citation that resolves, a malformed target, or a target the journal does not contain MUST fail with a diagnostic rather than being ignored; the archive MUST render the section and record the exact dispensation flags under provenance, and FR-171's required-contents inventory includes that section and provenance record whenever dispensation was used. (c) Authority: both flag surfaces are operator-reserved — a skill MAY pass them only on explicit operator direction naming the run, that direction is recorded in the rendered archive, and use without direction is a control failure, not a convenience. (d) Every dispensation leg MUST be independently disableable in code with a focused test that fails when it is disabled in memory, and no-flag parity MUST be pinned by test. (e) This mechanism exists only for closed journals; it never weakens open-run validation, the FR-017 source control, or any non-citation audit refusal.

- **FR-019** (MUST): Every record proposed by `run-open`, `journal-append`, `run-readmit`, `run-retire`, or `run-close`, including engine-generated retirement and readmission decisions, MUST satisfy the open-key new-write schema below before it can mutate owner, journal, or registry bytes. A candidate that is not an object or whose `type` envelope is missing, malformed, or not one of FR-010's seven types retains exactly `forge: journal append refused — invalid journal record`. Every per-field failure MUST use exactly `forge: journal append refused — invalid journal record: <field-path> <requirement>`, report only the first failure in the table's type-and-field order with array indexes ascending, and never echo the rejected value. `<field-path>` is the record type plus dotted field name and, for a member, its zero-based bracketed index; `<requirement>` is the applicable normative phrase from this requirement, such as `is required`, `must match execution-NN`, or `must be one of passed, failed, inconclusive, skipped`. The activating implementation candidate MUST synchronize every plugin-owned record-writing surface with this schema in the same candidate — the workflow and orchestrate skills' documented record shapes, the scoped-mutation runner's verification appends, and every engine-generated retirement, readmission, and takeover record — so activation cannot orphan a documented writer; known current divergences (an `execution_result` written without `task`, historical `status: pending`) are part of that activation inventory, and a writer shape the schema would refuse is a defect of the activating candidate, not of the writer's history.

| Type | Required fields and constraints, in validation order |
|---|---|
| All | `type`; `recorded_at` as a valid UTC RFC-3339 timestamp ending in `Z`. A present `run_id` MUST be a valid run ID and match the target run. |
| `run_started` | `run_id`; nonempty `goal`; absolute `repo` matching the target repository; full Git object ID `repo_head`; string-array `repo_status`; nonempty `plugin_ref`. Canonical nonempty `scope` is engine-injected before pure validation; known optional `successor_of` MUST be a valid run ID. |
| `task` | Nonempty `id`; `status` equal to `active` or an existing terminal task status; effective nonempty `goal`; nonempty `acceptance[]`; nonempty, admitted-scope-contained `files[]`. A terminal update MAY inherit `goal`, `acceptance`, and `files` only from the preceding same-ID task. |
| `execution` | Nonempty `agent`, `task`, `provider`, `role`, `mode`, `model`, and `effort`; `execution` matching `execution-NN`; absolute `worktree`; full Git object ID `head`; nonempty `prompt`, `handoff`, and `event_source`; `events` required and nonempty when `event_source` is `exec`, optional otherwise. |
| `execution_result` | Nonempty `agent`, `task`, and `summary`; `execution` matching `execution-NN`; `status` equal to an existing terminal execution status; arrays `files_changed[]` and `caveats[]`; nonempty `handoff` when `status` is `complete`. |
| `verification` | Nonempty `id`, `task`, `criterion`, `method`, `check`, and `observation`; `result` equal to an existing verification result; optional `evidence[]`. |
| `decision` | Nonempty `id` and `resolution`; optional `task`, `finding`, `outcome`, and `risk` are strings; optional `basis` is an array. Existing retirement, readmission, compatibility, and citation-correction grammars remain stricter special cases. |
| `run_closed` | Existing `judgment`; nonempty `summary`; `validation` object containing Boolean `ok`, arrays `issues`, `warnings`, and `non_passing_verifications`, and `profile` exactly `gates`; arrays `risks` and `follow_ups`. |

All nine FR-120 array fields remain arrays. Every string-array member MUST be nonempty; an array MAY be empty unless the table explicitly makes it nonempty. Every known optional field MUST be type-checked, while unknown keys remain accepted for forward compatibility. This schema adds no entry type or enum, never validates a historical record during scan/read/validate, and never lets FR-016 relax a newly proposed record. The validator's ordering is exactly: (1) object/type envelope; (2) existing FR-017 citation-root and existing citation-correction checks; (3) existing run-identity plus live-foreign or missing/malformed-owner refusal precedence using a read-only owner classification; (4) pure candidate-schema validation; (5) stale-owner adoption or any other mutation; and (6) append plus transactional registry update. Owner inspection and takeover MUST therefore be separate phases; an invalid candidate changes no journal, owner, or registry byte. The deterministic forms include `forge: journal append refused — invalid journal record: task.goal is required`, `forge: journal append refused — invalid journal record: execution.execution must match execution-NN`, and `forge: journal append refused — invalid journal record: verification.result must be one of passed, failed, inconclusive, skipped`.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-016**: the effective compatibility set is fourteen legs. Existing leg (4)'s named `LEGACY_EXECUTION_STATUS_MAP` additionally contains exactly `"completed" -> "complete"`; this is normalization, not a new FR-010 enum. Four new pre-declaration-only legs follow: (11) a later `execution_result` with the same nonempty `agent` and `execution` as an earlier result is warned rather than issued only when every occurrence is before the declaration; the first record retains pairing and lifecycle authority, so tolerance is not last-write-wins; (12) duplicate non-declaration `decision` IDs are tolerated only when every occurrence is before the declaration, with one consolidated warning per ID naming all physical lines; (13) FR-023 unknown-gate diagnostics for a pre-declaration `verification` are warnings, but the criterion remains unknown and can neither satisfy FR-021 nor recheck a failed known gate; (14) a nonempty string path in a pre-declaration `verification.evidence[]`, including a singleton normalized by leg (3), may be missing because its worktree was removed or the file was never materialized. Leg (14) tolerates absence only: empty or wrong-typed values and an existing non-file remain hard.

The exact warning suffixes introduced by this amendment are `interpreted execution_result status 'completed' as status 'complete'`; `tolerated duplicate execution_result for <agent>/<execution>; first occurrence at line <n> remains authoritative`; `tolerated duplicate decision id <id>; occurrences at lines <comma-separated-lines>`; `tolerated unknown gate criterion: <criterion>`; and `tolerated missing evidence[<n>] file: <path>`. Each is prefixed by the existing line-numbered legacy-warning formatter, except the duplicate-decision warning, which is emitted exactly once for the duplicate set and anchored to its last pre-declaration occurrence. The earlier sentence saying duplicate decision IDs stay hard is superseded only for leg (12): a second `journal-dialect-compat` declaration, a duplicate set crossing the declaration, or any ambiguous correction/dispensation target remains hard. The `run_closed`-final rule, retirement ordering, every record at/after the declaration, and every FR-019 proposed append remain strict.

The committed pinning corpus is exactly `tests/fixtures/prose-and-position-journal.jsonl` (109 records, declaration at physical line 107, SHA-256 `dd0695b47a37506a10efa9f7889855ada36e7cf9d09fdfdae284c57b049eed86`) paired with `tests/fixtures/validate-gates-0.6.4-output.json` (SHA-256 `41e563086b48340bb03f35734b6469551d9aab26efe14f12d38f88aebda3aa60`). Its 18 strict issues partition exactly 4 completed statuses, 4 duplicate execution results, 1 duplicate decision ID, 1 unknown gate criterion, and 8 missing evidence paths. The fixture is unconditional test input, not an optional sibling-checkout probe.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-017**: the shared tokenizer, resolve-then-contain predicate, no-follow file-type checks, and audit inventory also govern `journal ingest-chain` inputs labeled `ingest.state_file`, `ingest.events_file`, and `ingest.outcome_map`; the resulting content-addressed capture destination labeled `ingest.captured_package`; the engine-derived batch paths labeled `batch.intent` and `batch.receipt`; and the archive candidate labeled `archive.candidate`. Ingest source paths MUST be repository-relative and contained in the repository; before any journal citation, exact bytes are copied no-follow into an owner-controlled direct child of the run's `captured/sha256/<digest>/` directory and the inserted citation names only that run-relative captured path. The batch and archive paths are engine-derived direct children, never caller spellings. A violation retains the exact FR-017 refusal shape with the applicable new field label, and append-time, capture-time, render-time, and audit-time inventories MUST be generated from one committed surface table. No binding member is a path, and `run_binding.repository` is a derived repository identity rather than a citable path.

Revision-11 amendment to **FR-017**: Ingest MUST capture every evidence citation into the run's `captured/sha256/<digest>/` store and cite only that run-relative path; identical retry MUST remain byte-identical.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-018**: every reference above to the "identical ten legs" means the complete fourteen-leg FR-016 posture after Revision 9. The virtual declaration remains immediately before `run_closed`, and `run_closed` itself remains strict. No closed-legacy flag authorizes the separate FR-172 recovered-HEAD exception; that exception requires its own per-instance operator decision.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-019**: `run_started.writer_contract` is a known optional string and, when present, MUST equal `forge-journal-binding/1`. An activated `verification.binding` and `decision.binding` are known objects with the exact DM-001 shape; a gate verification requires one, and a chain-derived decision additionally requires `outcome` equal to `chain-approval`, `chain-skip`, `chain-landing`, or (Revision 13) `chain-abort`. A pre-activation legacy `decision` field remains renderable evidence under DM-001 but is never a valid replacement for a new-write `resolution`. Activated terminal `task` records remain ordinary task records with inherited goal/acceptance/files; they carry no journal generation, supersession, or `binding_summary` field and MUST follow all same-task `chain-landing` and (Revision 13) `chain-abort` decisions. `run_closed` gains no manifest field.

All journal mutation uses the typed builder table in §8. Every mutating builder receives exactly one nonempty `--repo` and `--run-id`; rejects duplicate or empty singleton options before repository access; injects `recorded_at`, `run_id`, allocated IDs, canonical scope, writer contract, and other derived repository facts rather than accepting them as caller JSON; preserves repeated-flag order unless §8 requires canonical bytewise binding order; and applies FR-017 plus the complete projected FR-019 validation before mutation. Except for `journal batch-recover`, every directly invoked mutating builder also requires exactly one global `--idempotency-key <64-lowercase-hex>`; a missing, duplicate, empty, or malformed key refuses exactly `forge: journal batch refused — idempotency key must be 64 lowercase hex`. It is transaction metadata, never a journal-record field, and a retry MUST reuse it. A chain outbox accepts no caller key and uses its DM-012 source-event digest. `--record-json`, raw JSON stdin, and any equivalent bypass are forbidden for an activated writer.

Batch append uses direct children `.journal-batch.lock`, `.journal-batch.intent`, and `.journal-batch-receipts.jsonl` under the run directory. The lock is stable for the run and the journal inode is never replaced. The lock, journal, present intent, and present receipt ledger MUST each be an owner-controlled nonsymlink regular file with one link; every open is no-follow, descriptor identity is checked against `lstat`, and the lock/journal identities are revalidated immediately before each protected append, unlink, or receipt write. A replacement, hard link, nonregular node, or identity mismatch is divergence and is never repaired automatically. One outstanding intent is permitted. `request_sha256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{"schema":"forge-journal-builder-request/1","verb":<exact-verb>,"repository":<canonical-repository-identity>,"run_id":<run-id>,"inputs":<normalized-caller-input-object>}`. `verb` is exactly one §8 table literal. `inputs` is an object with that row's caller field names: absent optional scalars are null, repeated flags are arrays (possibly empty) in caller order, and every caller-controlled semantic value is present; it excludes the idempotency key, allocated IDs, timestamps, generated records, and derived repository facts. For ingest it additionally contains the exact content digests of the state, events, and outcome-map inputs. An ordinary receipt, including every intent-carried receipt, is one append-only canonical line with schema `forge-journal-batch-receipt/1` and exactly `schema`, `idempotency_key`, `request_sha256`, `base_size`, `batch_sha256`, `record_count`, `journal_size`, `journal_sha256`, and `recorded_at`. Each bounded repair receipt retains that schema and has exactly those nine members plus only `"repaired":true` and `"repair_reason":"reconstructed from canonical journal bytes and byte-exact following receipt"`.

After validation and allocation but before the intent write, the builder computes the exact final journal size/hash, allocates the receipt timestamp, snapshots the receipt ledger's current size/hash (an absent first-use ledger is the zero-byte base), and constructs the complete canonical LF-terminated receipt line. The intent schema is `forge-journal-batch-intent/1` with exactly `schema`, `idempotency_key`, `request_sha256`, `base_size`, `base_sha256`, `record_count`, `batch_bytes`, `batch_sha256`, `receipt_base_size`, `receipt_base_sha256`, and `receipt_bytes`; both byte fields are unpadded base64url, for the exact ordered LF-terminated canonical record bytes and exact canonical LF-terminated receipt line respectively. When first use requires creating the empty receipt ledger, its exclusive no-follow creation and directory fsync precede the intent; the empty file is inert if the operation then fails.

Under the exclusive sidecar lock and before allocating an ID/timestamp or mutating, a direct builder resolves the repository/run, constructs `request_sha256`, and looks up the idempotency key in the intent and receipts. The same key with a different request digest refuses exactly `forge: journal batch refused — idempotency key already names different content`. A matching intent recovers only its stored exact journal and receipt bytes. A unique matching receipt requires the current journal to be at least `journal_size` bytes, the lowercase SHA-256 of `[0,journal_size)` to equal `journal_sha256`, the lowercase SHA-256 of `[base_size,journal_size)` to equal `batch_sha256`, and that suffix slice to contain exactly `record_count` canonical lines; it then reconstructs and returns the original result without validation, allocation, append, or a new receipt. A mismatching or duplicate receipt is divergence. Only a key absent from both stores proceeds to validate every record, citation, relation, ID, and the projected journal; allocate derived values and the exact stored receipt; write and fsync the immutable intent; append to the guarded journal descriptor with `O_APPEND`; fsync and verify the exact suffix; append/fsync the stored receipt; then unlink the completed intent and fsync the directory.

Journal recovery admits only base-only, an exact prefix of the intended journal suffix, or the exact complete suffix and appends only missing exact bytes. Receipt recovery then requires the ledger to equal the intent's exact receipt base or that base plus one exact prefix (including the complete value) of `receipt_bytes`; it appends only the missing suffix and fsyncs, verifies that `[receipt_base_size,current-size)` is exactly the one stored canonical line, and never emits a second copy. Thus a crash or short write at any receipt byte and a crash after receipt fsync but before intent unlink are recovered from intent-carried bytes without truncating the append-only ledger or choosing a new timestamp. Every other journal or receipt base/prefix/inode shape freezes the run. A second outstanding intent refuses exactly `forge: journal batch refused — another intent is pending`. Any base/suffix/inode/receipt divergence refuses exactly `forge: journal batch recovery refused — journal diverged from intent` and leaves all bytes for operator inspection. Completed-intent unlink is transaction cleanup, not the deferred automatic-GC subsystem.

The validation/mutation order is exactly: (1) singleton-option syntax, canonical repository/run resolution, request digest, and pre-allocation idempotency lookup/recovery/return; (2) caller-input and object/type envelope; (3) FR-017 citation/capture checks; (4) target run lifecycle and read-only owner classification; (5) pure per-record schema including writer-contract conditionals; (6) complete batch relations, unique allocated IDs, binding structure, and projected-journal validation; (7) any required terminal-chain replay/capture proof with no journal mutation; (8) stale-owner adoption when permitted; (9) intent, same-inode append, suffix proof, receipt, and intent cleanup; (10) registry transaction where the existing operation requires it. A failure before its listed mutation boundary leaves every earlier persistent byte unchanged. Historical read/validate never invokes this candidate or batch validator.

Revision-11 amendment to **FR-014**, **FR-019**, and **FR-120**: Activated readmission MUST use a typed scope-change record bound to the transaction run and canonical scope; without `--replace`, the submitted scope MUST contain the complete current admitted set, and containment diagnostics MUST name offending pathspecs as escaped JSON string literals. For exactly those diagnostics, naming syntactically valid pathspecs takes precedence over FR-019's no-echo rule; values that fail field validation remain unnamed. `run-readmit` is a directly invoked typed builder with repeated `--scope`, optional `--replace`, and the mandatory global `--idempotency-key`. Its normalized inputs are exactly `{"scope":<accepted caller-supplied canonical UTF-8-bytewise-sorted duplicate-free scope array>,"replace":<boolean>}`. It emits the existing `scope-readmission: locked` decision and, while retaining both the batch-sidecar and registry locks, performs locked validation, intent publication, same-inode journal append, ordinary receipt publication, intent cleanup, and registry publication.

Revision-11 receipt-ledger amendment to **FR-019**: Before admitting any new mutation of an activated journal, outside the bounded repair below, the receipt-covered intervals interpreted in journal-offset order MUST begin at byte zero, be contiguous and non-overlapping, and end at the current journal byte length. `journal batch-recover` MAY append one repair receipt only when all complete persisted receipts authenticate against the unchanged journal; their journal-offset intervals contain exactly one interior gap; the gap bytes are exactly one canonical, fully valid record for that run; the physically final ordinary receipt immediately following the gap ends at journal EOF; and the exact stale intent binds that following receipt's already-landed batch bytes, canonical receipt line, and preceding ledger base size and hash. The repair receipt is the exact eleven-member `forge-journal-batch-receipt/1` form pinned above, and every member is deterministically re-derived on every load. Leading, trailing, multiple, multi-record, noncanonical, tampered, nonmatching-intent, forged-repair, or disabled-control cases MUST refuse exactly `forge: journal batch recovery refused — journal diverged from intent` without mutation.

Because standalone `journal batch-recover` has no durable proof of `--replace`, it MAY recover an unpublished readmission only when the intended canonical scope contains every exact currently admitted pathspec and all other locked-readmission predicates pass. Otherwise the shrinking intent remains pending until an identical caller-bound retry with the same idempotency key and request re-supplies `--replace` from that original normalized request and revalidates the transaction. An already authenticated registry publication remains recoverable from its bound durable state.

### Level B gate enforcement (FR-020..FR-025)

- **FR-020** (MUST): `validate` MUST accept a `--gates` flag. With it, the payload gains `"profile": "gates"` and the checks in FR-021..FR-023 run in addition to the 23 baseline checks. Without it, behavior is bit-identical to upstream (FR-011).
- **FR-021** (MUST): With `--gates`, a `run_closed` with `judgment: "passed"` requires, for **each** of the three gate prefixes, a `verification` with `result == "passed"` whose journal line number is greater than the line number of the terminal `execution_result` of every mutating execution (executions whose `role != "review"`): for Gate 1 and Gate 2, any `criterion` beginning `gate-1: ` / `gate-2: `; for Gate 3, `criterion` exactly `gate-3: review-final verdict`. Each missing gate produces its own issue: `run closed as passed without a passing 'gate-1' verification after the last mutating execution` (likewise `'gate-2'`), and `run closed as passed without a passing 'gate-3: review-final verdict' verification after the last mutating execution`. A journal with zero mutating executions is exempt from this check.
- **FR-022** (MUST): With `--gates`, every `verification` whose `criterion` starts with `gate-1: `, `gate-2: `, or `gate-3: ` and whose `result` is `failed` MUST produce the issue `failed gate verification '<id>' has no subsequent passing recheck` unless a later `verification` with the **identical `criterion` string** has `result == "passed"` (prefix-only matches do not clear a failure: a passing `gate-1: unit tests` never clears a failed `gate-1: blast radius`).
- **FR-023** (MUST): With `--gates`, a gate verification whose `criterion` matches `gate-` followed by anything other than `1: `, `2: `, or `3: ` MUST produce the issue `unknown gate criterion: <criterion>`.
- **FR-024** (MUST): Whenever the workflow skill invokes `validate` it MUST pass `--gates`, and its close sequence MUST read `validate --gates → run_closed → validate --gates → archive → report.md`: a pre-close pass (advisory — FR-021 cannot fire before closure exists), then `run_closed` whose `validation` field embeds the pre-close payload verbatim, then a post-close pass that MUST exit 0, then the committed archive required by FR-170..FR-174, then `report.md`. The report skill MUST refuse to write `report.md` while the post-close `validate --gates` reports issues or the archive precondition fails. The exact close-sequence string appears only in the workflow skill (doc-contract test updated accordingly). A close that skipped the gates profile is detectable by the absent `profile` key in `run_closed.validation`.
- **FR-025** (MUST): The orchestration contract doc MUST gain a "Gate Recording" section defining DM-001 and stating explicitly that the `--gates` profile is a deliberate forge deviation from the upstream stance that validation never decides acceptance.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-021**: in a run activated by `forge-journal-binding/1`, gate acceptance additionally uses journal fields only to correlate task, chain, candidate, verdict, landing, approval, and record order. Every passing Gate-1, Gate-2, and Gate-3 record counted for a passed close MUST have a valid DM-001 binding recorded after the terminal result of every earlier same-task mutating execution. For one landed chain, all counted gates and any `chain-approval`/`chain-skip` decision MUST match the `chain-landing` decision's `source_record.chain_id` and candidate exactly; Gate-3 result/verdict consistency is mandatory. Multiple chains may serve one task, but each requires its own landing decision and complete matching gate set, and the explicit terminal task update MUST follow every such landing decision. A later different candidate does not silently supersede or rebind an earlier journal tuple; the writer must emit a fresh complete set for the landed candidate. Validation reports exactly `activated gate verification '<id>' has no valid forge-gate-binding/1 binding`, `task '<task>' has inconsistent bound candidate across gate and landing records`, `binding '<binding-id>' precedes the last mutating execution for task '<task>'`, or `terminal task '<task>' precedes a bound chain landing decision` for the first applicable correlation failure. These checks never open or replay `.forge/chains/`; chain replay belongs to the builder, ingest, and archive renderer, preserving FR-222.

Revision-13 amendment to **FR-021**: a `chain-abort` decision retires every gate, approval, and skip record bound to its `source_record.chain_id` from the landing correlation above — those records were drained by a chain that never landed — and the explicit terminal task update MUST follow every such abort decision, reported as exactly `terminal task '<task>' precedes a bound chain abort decision`. A task whose landing decision cites a chain that also carries an abort decision fails with the existing inconsistent-candidate diagnostic. Gate records drained by a chain aborted without a carried abort decision (a commit chain aborted before Revision 13, or any merge chain abort, which carries none yet) are NOT retired by this rule: journal-only correlation cannot recognise them, and a `passed` close carrying them remains refused by `validate --gates` and the archive until a retrospective abort disposition path exists.

Revision-13 superseded-candidate amendment to **FR-021** (bead forge-plugin-2mu): a BLOCK-then-restage cycle drains a fresh complete gate set for every candidate a chain stages, and only the last of them lands. Within one task and one `source_record.chain_id`, a bound record is *superseded* when a later bound record of the same task and chain (any bound `verification` or `decision`) carries a different `candidate`; superseded gate, approval, and skip records are retired from the landing correlation exactly as an abort retires a whole chain; the landed candidate's complete gate-1/2/3 set MUST consist of records that are not superseded (a chain that restaged A, then B, then A again lands only on a fresh A set drained after the B records — the earlier A set is exempt from precedence and therefore cannot count toward the landing), any `chain-approval` or `chain-skip` decision that is not superseded must still match the landing exactly as before, and a superseded `chain-landing` decision (a chain that landed and then carried a different candidate) fails with the existing inconsistent-candidate diagnostic, as a landing beside an abort does. The precedence rule (`binding '<id>' precedes the last mutating execution for task '<task>'`) applies only to the landed candidate's evidence: records bound to a superseded candidate or to a chain carrying a `chain-abort` decision were drained for bytes that never landed and legitimately precede a later re-implementation of the same task, so they are exempt; a record bound to the landed candidate that precedes the task's last mutating result is still refused. For both the run-level gate requirement above and the per-task precedence rule, a terminal `execution_result` whose journal line follows every `chain-landing` decision of its task, for an `execution` record that precedes the task's last such landing, does not move the last-mutating boundary: such a result is completion bookkeeping appended after the landing whose replayed event already proved the landed bytes (for example, an execution the orchestrating session finished but never journaled before it died). A mutating execution whose own record follows the task's last landing is a later mutation and keeps every refusal, and a mutating execution whose result was never journaled remains refused as before. Records bound to a chain the journal cannot correlate — one that neither landed, nor carries an abort decision, nor was superseded on its own chain, such as a run-bound chain sealed by an operator tombstone — are not retired by this amendment and keep the inconsistent-candidate refusal. Both rules are named journal-only correlation controls (`superseded-candidate`, `post-landing-result`) with disable-in-memory proofs that restore the prior refusals exactly.

### Roles, routing, and Codex launches (FR-030..FR-039)

- **FR-030** (MUST): Role assignment MUST be: Claude main session = orchestrator/verifier (owns journal, worktrees, gate chain, all reintegration); Codex fresh session = implementer (`role: "implementation"`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `ultra`, sandbox `workspace-write`); Codex fresh session = first-pass reviewer (`role: "review"`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `high`, sandbox `read-only`); Claude subagent `review-final` = binding final reviewer. The journal `execution` entry's `model` and `effort` fields MUST record the values actually passed at launch. Changing any model/effort/sandbox value is a control-class change.
- **FR-031** (MUST): Implementer executions MUST run in a dedicated git worktree; the implementer MAY commit only inside that worktree (its prompt template states: "You may commit inside this worktree. You must NEVER push, never touch any branch other than your own, and never run destructive git commands."). The orchestrator performs all reintegration.
