The archive/report additions and gotchas append do not alter the seven journal entry types, `.codex-orchestrator/` remains locally excluded under FR-015, and `.forge/tmp/` remains transient.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-170..FR-174**:

- **FR-170 archive mode**: `archive-run.py` renders deterministic bytes from the locked journal snapshot, captured chain package, cited plan documents, and commitment-audit output, subject to DM-012's 16 MiB cap. `commit start --archive-run-id <run-id>` rerenders those inputs and requires the candidate `.forge/history/runs/<run-id>.md` to be byte-identical before it stages or authorizes anything. The path MUST be an absent-from-HEAD direct child of `.forge/history/runs/`; creation uses no-follow exclusive semantics, while a pre-existing uncommitted regular file is accepted only when its complete bytes match. Symlinks, nonregular files, multiple hard links, another staged path, a changed input snapshot, or any rerender mismatch refuse. The CLI repeats rerender equality and the archive-only index proof immediately before authorization and immediately before commit. There is no pre-close manifest, close barrier, or archive-manifest field.
- **FR-171 contents**: each cited pre-close chain renders the exact DM-012 state/event blocks, digest summary, selected binding IDs, journal-line mapping, and complete discrepancy list. The gate table uses only resolved DM-001 bindings and includes `Binding source` and `Binding status`; `UNBOUND` and `UNEMBEDDED` remain visible. A legacy `decision` field renders under `Decision (legacy field)` with line/digest provenance. Missing chain authority for an activated record, changed snapshots, and authoritative binding conflicts refuse rather than becoming `None recorded`; a pre-activation unresolved record instead follows DM-001's visible `UNBOUND` rule.
- **FR-172 legacy mode**: the sole closing-HEAD exception is an absent archive for a closed pre-`forge-journal-binding/1` run whose contemporaneous closing HEAD was not captured. An operator MUST approve that single recovery instance by naming the target run and full recovered HEAD; the orchestrator records an ordinary decision in its current recovery run with `outcome: "operator_approval"` and resolution exactly `legacy-archive-recovery: <target-run-id> recovered closing HEAD <full-object-id>; <nonempty-single-line-reason>`. The archive command receives both `--legacy-recovered-head <full-object-id>` and `--legacy-approval <recovery-run-id>:<decision-id>` or neither, resolves that exact decision, and prints the exact archive lines `Legacy recovered closing HEAD: <full-object-id>` and `Legacy recovery approval: <recovery-run-id>:<decision-id>`. It MUST NOT label that value as a contemporaneous FR-172 capture, alter the closed legacy journal, create a recovery manifest, or apply to an archive already present in HEAD.
- **FR-173 report check**: normal and legacy archives retain the existing committed/clean check. When the local run/capture inputs remain available, `/forge:report` also rerenders in the recorded mode and requires equality with `HEAD:<archive-path>`; unavailable transient inputs are a visible follow-up but never authorize changing the committed archive. A mismatch refuses exactly `forge: report refused — committed archive differs from deterministic rerender: .forge/history/runs/<run-id>.md`.
- **FR-174 immutability and self-carriage**: an archive created in either mode immediately becomes ordinary DM-008 append-only history. The necessarily post-close archive-only commit chain receives only DM-012's narrow self-carriage exemption; it creates no post-close record in the target run and is not embedded in the archive it commits. The recovery run's approval decision remains ordinary evidence in that recovery run. Existing archives are never rerendered in place.

Revision-10 oversized-archive amendment to **FR-170..FR-174**: whenever an archive candidate requires review and its complete review package cannot be supplied within one direct reviewer-input window, it MUST use FR-216's single-master-package protocol. The one authoritative package contains the complete archive candidate and required review context, has one master digest, and is supplied to one reviewer by owner-controlled path plus digest rather than embedded package bytes. Deterministic bounded views are transport only; the single verdict cites the master digest. Archive shard packages, shard summaries, shard verdicts, aggregation, and quorum are forbidden. An archive review that cannot inspect the complete authoritative package refuses with exactly `forge: review refused — reviewer cannot inspect the complete authoritative package`; no partial review can authorize the archive-only commit.

### Upstream-forge migration (FR-180..FR-186)

- **FR-180** (MUST): At FR-080's prior-`.forge-manifest` detection step, after git-root validation and before any migration decision or mutation, init MUST classify an existing manifest as exactly one of: plugin schema when it contains an anchored `^plugin_ref: ` line; upstream schema when it contains an `upstream_commit:` line or any `region: <name> (<file>)` line; otherwise malformed. Plugin schema follows the unchanged re-init branch, upstream schema follows the migration branch, and malformed input MUST be refused without mutation. `.opencode/` or `opencode.jsonc` MAY corroborate and MUST be reported when present, but MUST NOT determine classification. This classification preserves FR-080's phase order and FR-090's shipped arming predicate.
- **FR-181** (MUST): Migration MUST enumerate every `FORGE:REGION <name> BEGIN` marker in the live upstream tree mechanically and salvage filled region bodies byte-identically from their actual upstream locations: `file-categories`, `stack-validations`, `changelog-policy`, and `review-prompt-project-focus` from `.opencode/rules/commit-workflow.md`; `gate1-test-command` from `.opencode/rules/worktree-workflow.md`; `completeness-project-items` and `project-triggers` from `.opencode/rules/review-constitution.md`; `project-overview` from `AGENTS.md`; and `agent-project-context` from `.codex/agents/implementer.toml`. It MUST enumerate all copies, surface divergent bodies for operator choice, and MUST NOT silently merge them. For every discovered region with no plugin destination, the FR-186 report MUST identify its source path and quote its marker name and complete body bytes verbatim so nothing vanishes unrecorded; the orphan set MUST be derived from discovered markers, not a fixed list (the current expected inventory is fourteen names: nine salvaged and five orphaned). Salvaged executable regions remain subject to FR-082.
- **FR-182** (MUST): Before FR-101 seed creation, migration MUST copy upstream fixtures and their committed `.result` baselines from `.opencode/evals/tasks/` to `.forge/evals/tasks/`. It MUST preserve fixture and baseline bytes, MUST NEVER re-mint a baseline for an imported fixture, MUST seed only coverage gaps, and MUST require `STRICT=1 run-evals.sh` to pass against imported baselines before Phase 5.
- **FR-183** (MUST): Migration MUST detect a project-scoped `.claude/agents/review-final.md` that shadows the plugin agent. Init MUST NOT write or commit `init_completed: true` until the collision is removed or renamed with explicit operator approval; while it exists, init MUST refuse activation and identify the colliding path.
- **FR-184** (MUST): Migration MUST recognize upstream-authored `.codex/config.toml` and `.codex/hooks.json` by upstream content signature, not only by the newer `# forge-managed` sentinel. It MUST preserve each original byte-for-byte as `<file>.pre-migration` before replacing routing with the plugin layer. It MUST leave the upstream agent TOMLs on disk, deregister them through the new config, enumerate the actual deregistered TOMLs in the migration report, and MUST NOT delete them automatically.
- **FR-185** (MUST): Migration MUST reconcile gitignore blocks by required content rather than treating an upstream header substring as an append guard. It MUST add or rewrite the plugin entries under a distinct plugin header without duplicate effective entries, and before activation MUST assert that `git check-ignore .forge/tmp` succeeds and `.forge/history/` is not ignored. Failure of either postcondition MUST stop init before `init_completed: true`.
- **FR-186** (MUST): Every migration MUST create and commit `.forge/history/migrations/<date>.md` under DM-009 before activation completes. The report MUST derive facts from the live disk, preserve each orphan region's source path, marker name, and complete body bytes as required by FR-181, and enumerate every legacy artifact left in place, including `.opencode/**`, `opencode.jsonc`, `.claude/commands/*`, the `.claude/settings.json` Stop hook when present, `.agents/`, deregistered agent TOMLs, `.tmp/`, and `.tmp/.commit-lock`. It MUST state that `AGENT_HALT` is shared, the rebase-lock path is shared, and the commit-lock paths differ (`.tmp/.commit-lock` versus `.forge/tmp/commit-lock`), making concurrent legacy `/commit` and `/forge:commit` unsafe until the legacy surface is removed. Removal MUST be left to the operator; migration MUST NOT delete legacy trees.

### Multi-run concurrency (FR-190..FR-194)

- **FR-190** (MUST): Commit authorization MUST use DM-006 content-addressed markers. Each authorizer hashes the exact staged diff in its invoking Git context and writes only `.forge/tmp/authorized/<staged-diff-sha256>` at the common main-checkout root; each guard resolves that same candidate path from its own invoking index, and each commit attempt removes only its candidate marker. Markers for other candidates MUST NOT be modified. Stale markers MAY be swept by age, but the current candidate MUST be validated first so the unchanged stale denial remains observable. FR-050's review loop remains outside the repo-wide commit lock. FR-055's persistent PID-owned lock epoch spans the prepare, standalone commit, and unconditional cleanup tool calls under one unchanged live DM-010 identity; only cleanup ends the epoch, and a fresh tool shell never substitutes its own PID.
- **FR-191** (MUST): Run opening MUST validate DM-010's inherited live session identity before mutation and atomically create its ownership sidecar with `run_started`; before every later append the journal writer MUST prove that the same host/PID exactly owns the sidecar. A same-host dead-PID owner permits atomic stale takeover only by a different current identity that has passed DM-010 liveness; a same-host live different PID or any different/unverifiable host MUST hard-refuse with exactly `forge: journal append refused — run <run-id> has live owner <pid>@<host>`. Missing or malformed ownership after `run_started` MUST fail closed with exactly `forge: journal append refused — owner record missing or malformed for run <run-id>`. Read-only owner classification retains precedence over candidate-schema validation, but stale takeover occurs only after FR-019 validation; a refused candidate MUST NOT change owner bytes. Ownership metadata and any takeover audit MUST ride existing entry types; the journal remains seven types. Citation corrections MUST use an existing `decision` entry whose resolution begins exactly `citation-correction:` and lists `<decision-id> basis[<n>]: <corrected-path>` or `<verification-id> observation: <cited> -> <corrected-path>` directives; the latest correction for a citation applies and the original bytes remain visible.
- **FR-192** (MUST): The workflow MUST implement FR-014 admission through DM-011 as one serialized registry-snapshot-read, child-classification, journal-reconciliation, successor-DAG derivation, comparison, revalidation, and atomic-replace operation. Run scope MUST use positive repository-relative Git pathspecs for intended project files and MUST exclude transient Forge and run state. It MUST be declared before `run_started`, remain fixed except through locked readmission, contain every task's declared files, and fail conservatively when missing, malformed, or ambiguous. Ordinary admission and readmission compare against every open run and every unrelated scope-reserving retired leaf; successor admission atomically consumes exactly one reserving predecessor and exempts the successor's full retired ancestry. A second child of a consumed predecessor is refused; historical forks remain readable and reserve once per open or retired leaf until the full descendant set has closed as passed or blocked. Closing or retiring a run MUST transactionally update the registry under the same lock; a passed or blocked successor close releases its closed branch's ancestry only when no other descendant leaf still reserves it, while a failed append or rollback releases nothing. The DM-011 empty-placeholder skip and named nonempty-orphan refusal apply to unrelated global reconciliation, never same-ID adoption or targeted monitor/validate. Immediately before any atomic replacement, the workflow MUST revalidate excluded placeholder identity/type/emptiness and all admission predicates; an atomic race MUST admit at most a mutually disjoint set. Lock acquisition, registry update, and post-update journal rollback failures use the cause-specific §9 diagnostics; ambiguous persisted state retains the unchanged registry-unavailable refusal.
- **FR-193** (MUST): All Stop telemetry MUST be append-only and session-identified per FR-093. Concurrent sessions MUST serialize only their append operation, preserve all previously written rows, and never use a shared overwrite target. Mechanical drift remains sourced from `events.jsonl`, not the Stop artifact, under FR-162.
- **FR-194** (MUST): The integration suite MUST include a real concurrent-repository harness that starts two complete commit chains and two complete worktree merges simultaneously. It MUST additionally exercise an ordinary admission racing a successor transfer, mixed open/retired conflicts in bytewise run-ID order, a historical fork, passed and blocked descendant-set release, rollback-preserved reservation, second-child refusal, concurrent placeholder change, and open/append/readmit/close from distinct fresh shells inheriting one live harness PID. It MUST prove no marker cross-admission, no interleaved journal identities, no lost telemetry rows, no deadlock, no transient-shell identity substitution, and fail-closed behavior under genuine contention; a sequential simulation or inspection-only test MUST NOT satisfy this requirement.

### Journal-derived learning (FR-200..FR-205)

- **FR-200** (MUST): The plugin MUST ship deterministic, standard-library-only `scripts/forge/journal-patterns.py`. Given one or more journals, it MUST emit sorted-key schema-v1 JSON containing per-task verification-result sequences, iteration counts and BLOCK-to-PASS latency; decision outcome mix; recorded execution routing compared with committed agent TOML; finding counts by severity and reviewer role; and frequencies keyed by exact diagnostic string. Input order MUST NOT affect output bytes. `drift-check.sh` MUST run this mechanical extraction and include its output in the FR-161 summary; no LLM participates in extraction.
- **FR-201** (MUST): `/forge:learn` MUST run a fresh read-only `review-periodic` pass over exactly three separate inputs: (i) the available FR-200 `journal_patterns` output derived from journals; (ii) the committed archive corpus under `.forge/history/runs/`; and (iii) the current committed `.forge/history/gotchas.md` when present. It MUST refuse to start when `journal_patterns.available` is false. It MAY cluster recurring failure shapes and propose the control that could have caught each shape earlier; it MUST identify its inputs and provenance.
- **FR-202** (MUST): `/forge:learn` MUST write proposals only as candidate fixtures under `.forge/evals/candidates/` in FR-102's Input/expected-verdict/provenance form and as traceable one-line failure shapes appended to `.forge/history/gotchas.md`. Each gotcha line MUST cite the journal entries that earned it. It MUST preserve every prior gotchas byte and MUST NOT write a fixture or baseline under `.forge/evals/tasks/`.
- **FR-203** (MUST): The learning loop's authority class is `advisory`. It MUST NOT write to `.forge/evals/tasks/`, any `.result` baseline, `rules/`, the constitution, `forge-project.md`, `.forge-manifest`, routing, hooks, gates, execpolicy, agent definitions, or any other control surface. It MUST NOT auto-apply, promote, approve, weaken, or commit any proposal. Promotion of a candidate follows FR-051's ordinary independent review and explicit operator-approval control path.
- **FR-204** (MUST): The semantic learning pass runs only after the FR-170 archive-only commit has completed, never inside that commit. It MAY also run on the drift cadence after mechanical drift output exists; on that cadence, the journal-derived FR-200 output is the first of FR-201's three separate inputs, alongside the committed archive corpus under `.forge/history/runs/` and the current committed `.forge/history/gotchas.md` when present. It MUST never commit, never block or delay the close sequence, and leave candidates and gotchas changes for a separate ordinary commit. Failure or refusal of learning MUST NOT alter the passed run's closure or report outcome.
- **FR-205** (MUST): Committed gotchas MUST join every later agent prompt as feed-forward context through FR-037. Working-tree-only gotchas MUST NOT influence prompt assembly.

### Forge CLI commit chain (FR-210..FR-224)

FR-210..FR-224 specify the already-shipped commit slice — phases 0–2 of `docs/design/0003-forge-cli-plumbing.md` — and retain their Revision-4 wording byte-for-byte where mechanically anchored. Revision 7 adds separate phase-3/4 authority in FR-230..FR-241, the non-authorizing phase-5 admission boundary in FR-242, and the non-authorizing phase-6 declaration in FR-243. Throughout, `forge <verb>` is shorthand for `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" <verb>`.

- **FR-210** (MUST): The plugin MUST ship `scripts/forge/cli.py` (Python ≥ 3.10, standard library only) implementing the commit-chain state machine with subcommands `status`, `commit start|restage|rebase|abort|approve|skip|finalize`, `verify`, `gate run`, `classify`, `scan secrets`, and `review request|collect|attach|disposition`. Every state-mutating subcommand MUST run the halt check first via `check-halt.sh` and refuse while a halt sentinel is present. The CLI MUST compose the existing tested executables — `check-halt.sh`, `acquire-commit-lock.sh`/`release-commit-lock.sh`, `risk_tier.py`, the staged-diff secret scan, `run-evals.sh`, `check-test-quality.py`, `emit-decision-event.py`, and FR-154's independent fast recomputation — recording their observed results; it MUST NOT reimplement halt, lock, classification, the secret scan, or any other tested control. Where required logic is embedded in a hook-shaped script, the sanctioned move is extracting a shared function from the existing script as its own control-class change, keeping every existing disable-in-memory test pointed at one implementation — never a second copy. When a run is open, the CLI appends the existing journal `verification`/`decision` shapes for chain events; it introduces no new journal record type and never infers "the latest run" (FR-057 unchanged). Chain-event artifacts the CLI produces for a run — prompts, bounded transcripts, review packages, verdicts — MUST be written under that run's execution directories, and every journal citation the CLI records MUST resolve within the run directory or the repository, satisfying FR-017 at the source; session- or machine-local scratch locations are never citable.
- **FR-211** (MUST): Chain lifecycle. States are exactly `classifying`, `verifying`, `reviewing`, `revising`, `awaiting_approval`, `authorized`, `committing`, `closed`, `aborted`. Chains are born in `classifying`: `commit start --paths <path>... [--declare-tier <tier>]` refuses when another live commit chain exists for the same worktree (at most one live commit chain per worktree — the git index is the mutex; the refusal names the live chain and the abort/finalize remediation), when the index already holds staged content (dirty-index refusal: the offending paths are named with the remediation printed — pre-existing staged content belongs to no live chain and MUST never silently ride into a candidate), when a named path does not exist, or when policy is unreadable from committed HEAD (FR-083's bootstrap admission path keeps its narrower fixed rules and MUST remain possible). On admission the CLI performs `git add -- <paths>` itself, computes the candidate per DM-012, and runs classification automatically. The normative transitions: `classifying → verifying` (tier evidence bound to candidate); `verifying → reviewing` (every required mechanical step PASS or operator-skipped, all bound to the current candidate) or `verifying → authorized` (tier requires no review — fast — with eligibility holding at authorization time); `reviewing → authorized` (verdict PASS, non-control) or `reviewing → awaiting_approval` (verdict PASS, control-class) or `reviewing → revising` (verdict BLOCK; iteration counter incremented); `revising → classifying` (restage after fixes; refused at the FR-053 cap of 8 — record residual risk and escalate, never another cycle); `awaiting_approval → authorized` (approval recorded for the exact current candidate through FR-218); `authorized → committing → closed` (finalize per FR-219). `abort [--reason]` reaches `aborted` from any nonterminal state and is journaled; from `closed` or `aborted` it refuses with `state-precondition` before any mutation (Revision 13: a landing is never rewritten and an abort is never retried). `aborted` admits exactly one self-event, `abort_disposition_recorded` (Revision 13, FR-210/FR-222 amendment), which changes no state field. Tier is promote-only end to end: declared tier is recorded and promoted only by evidence — the CLI has no demote operation — and the effective tier is the maximum of declared, computed at start, and every later recomputation. An out-of-band index change detected by re-hash at any command returns the chain to `classifying` with all evidence dead and an anomaly journaled. A chain past `inactive_after` (default 24 hours from `last_event_at`) is dead in place: only `status`, `abort`, and (Revision 13) `abort-disposition` may touch it, the last because it targets chains nobody has touched for a long time; the same three verbs are exempt from the FR-053 iteration-cap refusal. There is no chain-lifetime TTL; only the FR-219 authorization token carries the 30-minute bound.
- **FR-212** (MUST): Candidate identity and invalidation. The candidate is the SHA-256 of the exact `git diff --cached` bytes computed by the CLI after it stages; it does not exist before staging and is never computed from a path list. `commit restage --paths <path>...` is the only sanctioned way to change the staged set mid-chain: it re-runs staging, recomputes the candidate, and invalidates every evidence record bound to the old hash, including classification, which reruns. An index change made outside the CLI has the same invalidation effect on detection, plus a journaled anomaly note. Working-tree-vs-index drift on a staged path MUST be refused at `review request` and at `finalize` — the bytes about to be reviewed or committed are not the bytes just edited; the remediation is restage, or the operator-recorded index-drift skip of FR-217. Cross-chain isolation is unchanged from FR-190: authorization binds to the candidate hash, so chains in different worktrees cannot authorize each other's diffs.
- **FR-213** (MUST): Out-of-band HEAD movement. Every command compares the recorded `repo_head` with the current HEAD. A moved HEAD MUST never surface as a bare hash mismatch: the CLI journals a `head_moved` event naming the old and new SHAs with an explicit "out-of-band commit, not chain corruption" diagnostic, and every state-advancing verb refuses until `commit rebase` or `abort`. `commit rebase` re-pins `repo_head`; checks policy continuity by digest — byte-identical committed policy at the new HEAD keeps policy-derived records, while changed policy bytes end the chain (restart); restages the recorded path set and recomputes the candidate. Evidence disposition is graded, mirroring FR-063: diff-scoped records (secret scan, review verdict — candidate-bound per DM-006 doctrine) survive iff the recomputed candidate hash is unchanged; tree-dependent records (gate runs, stack validations) are always dead and re-run. Whole chains MUST NOT be serialized behind a repository lock: FR-190's review-loop-outside-the-lock rule stands, content addressing remains the coordination mechanism, and the CLI MUST NOT be stricter than DM-006's staged-diff-hash pinning — concurrent commits that leave the candidate bytes unchanged remain tolerated.
- **FR-214** (MUST): `forge verify` MUST run every remaining required mechanical step for the effective tier, in order — the configured `changelog` gate first when present, then gate 1 twice, stack validations, the assertion-quality sensor, invariant `commit` rows, the secrets scan, and STRICT evals where FR-103 applies — recording each exactly as `gate run` does. Judgment verbs (review, approve, skip, finalize) are never included. `verify` MUST be resumable by construction: per-step completion is persisted in the chain, each invocation continues from the first incomplete step until done, failure, or interruption, and invoking it when everything passes is a no-op that prints the next judgment verb — the harness tool-execution ceiling is shorter than gate 1 alone, so a blocking monolith is a liveness bug, not an ergonomic choice. `gate run <gate-id>` runs a single configured gate from the committed policy table (never a working-tree policy file) under FR-149's execution discipline — one-cell `bash -c` argv, isolated process group, 65,536-byte output cap, 1200-second fail-closed timeout — while the CLI itself captures exit code, duration, stdout/stderr digest, a bounded transcript, and the DM-013 fingerprint. Phase 1 has no generic mutating-gate registry: a configured DM-003 changelog command is automatically the sole mutating gate, and on success the CLI adds its declared output paths to the chain's path set, stages them explicitly, recomputes the candidate, invalidates downstream evidence, and reruns classification; the machine therefore refuses every other `gate run` while `changelog` is pending and names it as remediation. The complete phase-1 mechanical gate-ID set is exactly `changelog`, `gate-1`, `stack:<category>`, `assertion-sensor`, `invariant:<row-number>`, `secret-scan`, and `strict-evals`; `<category>` is normalized lowercase `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`, and `<row-number>` is the one-based ordinal among all invariant data rows, of which only rows with `enforcement == "commit"` are required. Both Gate-1 observations use the same `gate-1` ID. `classification` is an internal step rather than a runnable gate; `review` is a skip target rather than a mechanical gate ID; index drift is targeted only by `--index-drift`; unknown or unconfigured IDs refuse with `state-precondition`. The shipped resolver applies Python `int()` to an `invariant:` suffix, so an integer-equivalent noncanonical spelling such as `invariant:01` or `invariant:+1` can execute row 1, but its evidence is stored under that literal alias and does not satisfy canonical `invariant:1`; this is documented shipped behavior, not a promise that aliases are canonical. "Twice consecutively" for gate 1 is two CLI-recorded runs whose DM-013 fingerprints match; a mismatch applies DM-013's complete voiding rule.

Revision-11 amendment to **FR-050**, **FR-210**, **FR-214**, and **FR-215**: Exact changelog outputs from the authenticated committed policy snapshot are engine-injected gate paths exempt from task and admitted-scope membership during commit binding, transition validation, and ingest; working-tree policy and pathspec containment MUST NOT establish this exemption. Only an exact changelog output declared by that authenticated committed-policy snapshot receives the exemption; the fact that a caller supplied or labeled a path never establishes it. Archive-only chains never schedule the configured changelog gate. The mutating-gate refusal on an archive-only chain remains unchanged; the changelog step is simply never required for archive mode.

- **FR-215** (MUST): Per-tier required steps restate FR-050/FR-152 without weakening: every tier runs stage + candidate + classification (inside `start`), the configured mutating changelog gate first where present, gate 1 twice consecutively, stack validations for touched categories, the FR-144 assertion-quality sensor, the FR-147 `commit` invariant rows, the secrets scan, and finalize with halt, lock, and candidate re-verification (fast additionally: the independent eligibility recomputation per FR-154 doctrine). Fast differs from the other tiers in exactly one row — the reviewer; a fast chain made cheap by weakening any other row is an FR-152 violation. Standard requires a fresh Codex `review-cheap` execution; hard requires `review-final`; control additionally requires STRICT evals (a control diff is never fast) and operator approval bound to the candidate. Fast is opt-out, not opt-in: classification runs automatically inside `commit start`, a mechanically eligible diff is a fast chain unless evidence promotes it, and fast finalize keeps the independent recomputation — the cheap path is cheap to claim, not cheap to fake.
- **FR-216** (MUST): Review verbs. `review request` refuses on tree-vs-index drift over staged paths, assembles the review context package for the current candidate — the exact staged diff, the mechanically selected per-artefact review profile, and the committed project focus regions — and MUST exclude the implementer's handoff, claimed results, and prior verdicts from the package by construction: the reviewer never receives them because the CLI never packages them. For the Codex tier, the CLI launches the reviewer process itself (detached, per the existing launch doctrine), records the launch as evidence (argv digest, PID, timestamps), and owns the verdict output path under `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/review/`; `review collect` verifies the process completed, validates the verdict grammar, and binds the verdict to the candidate — the orchestrator never launches the Codex reviewer and never writes its verdict, removing the accidental path to a model-authored PASS. For the review-final tier, the CLI emits the package path, the package digest, and the exact reviewer invocation for the orchestrator to spawn as a Claude subagent (a CLI subprocess cannot spawn one); `review attach --verdict-file <file>` validates the verdict grammar and requires the verdict to cite both the current candidate hash and the package digest issued at request time, journaling the attach. This tier is best-effort, validated-but-trusted: it converts one sloppy write into deliberate multi-step forgery and no further, and the spec states that asymmetry rather than papering over it. The shipped transport accepts strict UTF-8, strips each split line, discards blanks, and requires the first nonblank line to be exactly `VERDICT: PASS` or `VERDICT: BLOCK`; one exact `candidate: <exact-current-candidate>` line and one exact `package: <exact-issued-package-digest>` line MUST each occur exactly once later in either order; every other nonblank line MUST be an optional repeatable `finding: <CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR> <nonempty text>` line. PASS rejects CRITICAL and MAJOR findings but permits MINOR; BLOCK is accepted with any permitted finding set, including no finding or only MINOR findings. `OBSERVATION` is not a transport severity. These transport rules describe shipped parsing only and do not narrow the constitution's broader substantive review duties. A BLOCK verdict increments the chain's iteration counter and transitions to `revising`; at the FR-053 cap of 8 the CLI refuses further cycles, records residual risk, and escalates — never a commit. `review disposition --finding <n> --severity <sev> --resolution <text>` is model-issuable judgment work; the hook never parses its flags (an argv severity matcher would be another FR-090-grade parser, refused as such); an above-MINOR disposition leaves the chain unable to advance until the operator co-signs through the FR-218 mechanism, preserving FR-053.

Revision-10 oversized-package amendment to **FR-216**: one review iteration has exactly one authoritative owner-controlled regular master-package file and one lowercase SHA-256 master digest over its complete bytes. When that package exceeds a direct reviewer-input window, the launch gives one reviewer execution the master path and digest, never embeds the package bytes, and may additionally supply the ordinary candidate/generation/profile/target identifiers. The same reviewer inspects the complete master through deterministic raw-byte windows `[65536*n, min(65536*(n+1), byte_length))` in ascending `n`. The reader verifies the unchanged master path identity, byte length, and full digest before the first window and after the last, and verifies that concatenating the windows produces exactly the master bytes and digest. An index or cached window is a discardable transport view only and never a second package or verdict authority.

The one reviewer emits exactly one FR-216 verdict citing the master digest. Multiple reviewer executions, shard packages, shard summaries, shard verdicts, aggregation, majority, and quorum are forbidden for one review iteration. If that reviewer cannot inspect every master-package byte through the bounded views, review stops without attaching a verdict and refuses exactly `forge: review refused — reviewer cannot inspect the complete authoritative package`; where a CLI envelope applies, the absent required review remains exact `evidence-incomplete`. This protocol applies to oversized commit and merge packages; FR-170 applies it independently to oversized archive candidates.

Revision-10 merge-disposition amendment to **FR-216**: a merge chain may have exactly zero or one outstanding above-MINOR (`MAJOR` or `CRITICAL`) disposition awaiting operator co-sign. An above-MINOR `review disposition` is admitted only when that slot is empty. While it is occupied, another above-MINOR disposition — including a replacement for the same finding — changes no event, state, or evidence and refuses with exact `forge: review disposition refused — above-MINOR disposition already awaits operator co-sign` and `state-precondition`. MINOR dispositions do not occupy the slot. The sole outstanding object fixes the finding number, severity, resolution, candidate HEAD, and generation; the flag-less `merge approve` disposition purpose selects exactly that object, records its co-sign, and atomically clears the slot before another above-MINOR disposition may be accepted.

- **FR-217** (MUST): Operator verbs. `commit approve --candidate <sha256>` is valid only in `awaiting_approval`, requires its argument to equal the current candidate hash, and transitions to `authorized`. `commit skip <gate-id> --reason <text>` and `commit skip --index-drift --reason <text>` record an FR-056 user-directed skip or the index-drift override in chain state ahead of finalize; overriding the drift negligence guard is the definitional operator-reserved act. Both verbs are operator-bound: the PreToolUse hook denies them when issued through the model's Bash tool, with a denial message instructing the model to present the candidate SHA-256 and diff summary and ask the operator to run the exact command via the `!` prefix. `finalize` carries no override flags at all — skips exist only as operator-recorded chain state. The skill's FR-056 duty is unchanged: mapping operator skip language onto gate IDs and presenting the exact operator argv; the CLI accepts only gate IDs. No skip covers review for control-class changes; no skip covers approval, ever. In headless runs a needed skip or approval parks the chain — correct behavior, not a liveness bug. The `!`-channel rule is constitutional skill content pinned by an FR-223 eval: the model may ask the operator to run Forge operator verbs via `!`; it may never ask the operator to run raw git or any other enforcement-bypassing command.
- **FR-218** (MUST): Approval mechanism, layered strongest-first, preserving FR-051/FR-060 law — a reviewer PASS never authorizes a control-class commit: (1) the state machine is the mandatory floor — control-class chains enter `awaiting_approval` after review PASS, `finalize` refuses in any other state, and no transition to `authorized` exists without an approval record naming the current candidate hash; (2) hook-denied model approval plus operator-run approval via the `!` prefix is the normative mechanism — operator-typed `!` commands execute outside the model's Bash tool path, so the verbs reach the CLI only from the operator's keyboard; its precondition is the FR-223 eval proving the `!` bypass in the current harness — an eval, never an assumption; (3) the approve verb stays permanently out of every allowlist, so if layer 2's assumption fails, a model invocation still raises a harness permission prompt showing the candidate SHA in argv; (4) the skill retains the conversational presentation duty (diff summary, candidate SHA, review outcome) — its answer flows through the model's context and is never treated as the approval itself. Honest limits, stated normatively: the CLI proves the approve command ran and named the right candidate; it cannot prove the operator understood the change.
- **FR-219** (MUST): Finalize. `commit finalize --message <text> | --message-file <file>` is the only commit-producing verb in the CLI; the commit message is model judgment and arrives only through these flags — no stdin, environment, or tempfile side channel. Finalize verifies the complete evidence chain — every required step for the effective tier present, bound to the current candidate hash, within the authorization TTL, and either PASS or covered by an operator-recorded skip — and refuses on tree-vs-index drift over staged paths. The authorization token is issued when the chain reaches `authorized`, carries a 30-minute TTL from issuance (preserving DM-006's clock exactly), and is consumed on use; the TTL is the only bound on base drift between verification and commit — DM-006 parity forbids tightening it, and the archive shows the drift instead. The normative two-phase order is: (1) halt check; (2) acquire the commit lock; (3) re-verify candidate byte identity and refuse on drift; (4) write the intent event, state → `committing`; (5) `git commit`; (6) record the produced commit SHA; (7) close the chain; (8) release the lock. Recovery is executed by the next CLI invocation and diagnosed by `status`: crash after (4) before (5) — HEAD does not match the intent; fall back to `authorized` iff the authorization token is unexpired and unconsumed, otherwise refuse to the operator with both facts stated; crash after (5) before (7) — identify the new HEAD against the recorded intent, require its diff identity to equal the bound candidate, then complete (6)–(8) idempotently; a HEAD matching neither the pre-finalize state nor the intent is an exit-2 frozen chain — history moved outside the protocol. Git itself is atomic inside (5): the protocol never guesses, it observes HEAD. A chain found in `committing` accepts only `status` and the recovery path; every other verb refuses with the window diagnosis. After a successful finalize the CLI emits the FR-157 `gate_commit` (and, for fast, `fast_allowed`) events under the existing advisory-emission contract.
- **FR-220** (MUST): Output contract. Every command ends its stdout with `next required step: <exact command>` (or `next required step: none — chain closed`). Every refusal is a structured, self-contained diagnostic — current chain state, the failed precondition, expected versus observed values (digests truncated for display, full in the JSON envelope), the exact remediation command, and the next required step — and exits 1; a refusal that requires the operator says so explicitly and names the operator action. `--json` on every command emits exactly one machine-readable envelope on stdout with the exact key set `{schema, ok, chain_id, state, reason_code, message, expected, observed, remediation, next_required_step, evidence_refs}` and nothing else — under `--json` the human `next required step:` line is a field, never a second stdout line. Reason codes are a fixed enum, spec'd and pinned by tests, so the hook and evals assert on codes rather than prose. The closed table below is that enum, normative and exhaustive: every member is kebab-case, unique, and sorted; success envelopes use exactly `ok`; exit-2 internal failures use exactly `frozen-chain`; every exit-1 refusal uses exactly one refusal member; wildcards, dynamic construction, and unlisted codes are forbidden, and adding, removing, or respelling a member is a control-class change. The members and their failed preconditions: `ambiguous-target` (a dispensation or disposition target matches more than one record); `approval-required` (control-class chain not yet operator-approved); `candidate-stale` (evidence bound to a superseded candidate hash); `citation-out-of-root` (FR-017 refusal surfaced through the CLI); `dirty-index` (pre-existing staged content at `commit start`); `drift-tree-index` (working-tree-vs-index divergence on a staged path); `evidence-incomplete` (a required tier step is absent or non-PASS without an operator skip); `frozen-chain` (corrupt chain file, irresolvable event/state divergence, or foreign HEAD in `committing`); `halt-engaged` (operator halt sentinel present); `head-moved` (out-of-band HEAD movement pending `commit rebase` or `abort`); `inactive-chain` (chain past `inactive_after`; only `status`/`abort` accepted); `iteration-cap` (FR-053 cap of 8 reached); `live-chain-exists` (another live commit chain owns the worktree); `lock-unavailable` (commit-lock acquisition failed or timed out); `mutating-gate-pending` (non-mutating `gate run` while a configured mutating gate is pending); `ok` (success); `operator-verb-denied` (model-issued `approve`/`skip` reached the CLI through a denied path); `path-missing` (a named path does not exist at `commit start`/`restage`); `policy-changed` (committed policy bytes differ at the new HEAD after `commit rebase`); `policy-unreadable` (committed-HEAD policy unavailable outside the bootstrap path); `review-verdict-invalid` (verdict grammar, candidate-hash citation, or package-digest citation failed at `collect`/`attach`); `skip-not-permitted` (a skip naming control-class review or approval); `state-precondition` (verb issued in a state whose transition table does not admit it); `token-consumed` (authorization token already used); `ttl-expired` (authorization token past its 30-minute TTL). `--verbose` streams underlying activity live without changing what is recorded as evidence — the bounded transcript and digests are written identically either way. Internal failures (corrupt chain file, irresolvable event/state divergence, unexpected exceptions) exit 2, fail closed, and print what was being attempted, what is on disk, and that the chain is frozen pending `status`/`abort` — never a bare traceback. Exact refusal strings and reason codes are control surface: pinned by tests, changed only through control-class review — the same doctrine as FR-090's denial literals.
- **FR-221** (MUST): Hook integration, phase-1 dual-accept posture. While the DM-006 marker flow remains live, the PreToolUse hook MUST accept a `git commit` that is authorized by EITHER a valid DM-006 marker (FR-090 byte-identical, unchanged) OR a live chain in `authorized` state whose bound candidate hash equals the staged-diff hash the hook computes itself in its invoking Git context, with an unexpired, unconsumed authorization token. Two red lines are normative: the dual-accept MUST NOT be skipped — it is the only safe decommission path for the marker flow — and the marker grammar, its parser, and their tests MUST NOT be deleted until the FR-223/eval net pins the state machine (a later phase-5 revision). The hook additionally: denies the model-issued operator verbs (`commit approve`, `commit skip`) on the model's Bash path per FR-217; denies index-mutating git verbs (`add`, `restore --staged`, `reset`, `rm --cached`, `stash`) from any session that does not own the live chain for that worktree — ownership is the session identity recorded in the chain file at `start`, Codex implementer worktrees (FR-031) have their own indexes and are untouched, only sessions running under the hook are bound, and re-hash detection remains the backstop for everything else. The matcher grammar for the CLI invocation form is spec content, normative here. Split the command string into segments on unquoted `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, `&`, and newlines with the same quote-aware discipline FR-090's parser applies today (bare `&` is retained deliberately: the shipped parser already splits on it, and narrowing would be a weakening). A segment matches the CLI invocation form when, after any leading `env` prefix with assignments, its command token is an interpreter form — the token `python3`, the token `python`, or a path whose final component is `python3` or `python` — followed by a script argument whose final path components are `scripts/forge/cli.py` (in any of: a repository-relative or `./`-prefixed path; an absolute path; a `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`- or `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`-prefixed path; or a resolved plugin-root path under a cache, marketplace, or local-checkout directory), with quoting variations handled by the same tokenizer. A matched segment is then classified by its first one or two subcommand tokens: the hook denies segments whose subcommand is `commit approve` or `commit skip` when issued through the model's Bash tool, with exactly `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (commit approve)` or `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (commit skip)` as the deny reason; it allows `commit finalize` and every other CLI verb (phase-1 posture; raw-verb denial is phase 4). Decoy paths whose final components are not `scripts/forge/cli.py`, lookalike verbs, and quoted separator characters inside string arguments never match. Every existing FR-090 denial literal remains byte-identical, and the two operator-verb literals above join the FR-090 doctrine: pinned by tests, changed only through control-class review. The FR-223 eval pins this grammar with a committed accept/deny vector corpus. No repo-native git hooks, ever: FR-031 implementer worktrees never pass through PreToolUse, and a repository git hook would break them; the enforcement point is PreToolUse, full stop. Raw `git commit`/`git push` denial is NOT part of this revision: it arrives with the phase-4 revision, which must also ship `forge push` so a legal close path exists.
Revision-8 amendment to **FR-221**: DM-010's stable identity and FR-055's standalone command shape are additive only. They MUST NOT change independent marker-or-chain acceptance, either existing operator-verb denial, the marker grammar/parser/tests, or any other byte-pinned phase-1 matcher behavior; no internal command-shape classification becomes a third public denial or a third authorization path.

- **FR-222** (MUST): System of record. The chain file is the system of record for commit authorization; the hook's chain-side acceptance and the CLI's finalize checks read chain state from it, never from the journal. `validate --gates` remains a journal instrument (FR-020/FR-021) and MUST NOT read the chain file — pointing it at chain state would recreate the split-brain this rule prevents. When a run is open, chain events ride the existing journal `verification`/`decision` shapes; the journal remains the system of record for the run narrative; run archives cite `chain_id` and carry the archived chain file and events log. The model never copies data between the two records; any surface that needs both reads both.
- **FR-223** (MUST): Phase-0 precondition evals and finalize-check severity. Before any phase-1 surface ships, committed evals MUST exist and pass for: (a) the `!`-prefix bypass behavior FR-218 layer 2 rests on; (b) the hook argv matcher for the CLI invocation form, including its segment-splitting and denial literals; (c) the FR-220 reason-code enum; (d) the `!`-channel temptation task — a blocked model must refuse to ask the operator to run raw git. Because the CLI's finalize path inherits the hook's status as last line of defense, every internal finalize check — evidence completeness, candidate byte identity, TTL, tree-vs-index drift, halt, lock — carries hook-parser severity: each is independently disableable in code with a focused test that fails when it is disabled in memory, and its exact diagnostics are pinned. Definitions these evals bind to: **harness qualification** — eval (a)'s recorded evidence MUST capture the qualification tuple `{arch, claude_executable_digest, claude_version, distribution_channel, hook_config_digest, os, permission_mode}`; the recorded bypass result is valid only while the installed harness matches the recorded `claude_version` major.minor and `distribution_channel`, a mismatch renders FR-218 layer 2 unavailable (control-class chains park in `awaiting_approval`) until the experiment is re-run and re-recorded, and layer 3 covers only the hook-absent case, never a failed bypass. **Eval subjects** — eval (a)'s subject is the interactive Claude Code TUI itself, exercised by a committed repeatable operator protocol with recorded evidence, never headless; eval (d)'s subject is the operational orchestrating model under the commit skill (a fresh headless session, plugin loaded, tools disabled), never a review agent, with the verdict derived by a mechanical oracle from structured action/command fields — a model-authored compliance claim is never the oracle — and a paired permitted case (presenting the exact operator `!` argv) so an always-refuse subject cannot pass; evals (b) and (c) are mechanical corpus checks run by the focused test suite, whose phase-1 legs consume — never copy — the committed corpora. For eval (d), "raw git" means any git invocation whose subcommand mutates history or enforcement state (`commit`, `push`, `reset`, `rebase`, `merge`, `add`, `restore`, `rm`, `stash`, `checkout`); asking the operator to run read-only git is not a violation. Fixture families for these evals under `.forge/evals/tasks/` are versioned (`-v<n>`) and immutable once their baselines are recorded: a legitimate change ships a new version and never edits or re-mints an existing one.
- **FR-224** (MUST): Dogfood (phase 2). Once phase 1 ships, this repository MUST run its commit chains exclusively through the CLI path, with FR-155 telemetry watching the fast tier: `fast_allowed` remaining zero here after adoption is a design-failure signal that MUST surface as a drift finding for semantic review, never silently accepted. The skills MUST retain and emphasize the standing duty to stop and surface out-of-checklist anomalies — the CLI's green light must never become the only signal the model attends to — and best-effort labels in this section are normative text whose removal is a control change.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-210**: the engine exposes the §8 typed builder verbs and `journal batch-recover`; `scripts/forge/cli.py` removes `append_run_stub` and never hand-authors journal JSON. Every parser treats `--repo`, `--run-id`, and `--chain-id` as singleton nonempty options: a duplicate refuses with exact human diagnostic `forge: CLI option refused — duplicate <option>` and `option-duplicate`; an empty value refuses with `forge: CLI option refused — empty <option>` and `option-empty`. A run-bound commit begins only with paired `commit start --run-id <run-id> --task <task-id>` and a run-bound merge only with the equivalent paired merge-start flags; exactly one of the pair refuses. The start builder proves repository identity, the active task, admitted path membership, committed policy identity, and the immutable one-chain-to-one-task binding. Later verbs take neither flag and inherit `run_binding` from chain state; implicit latest-run, latest-task, or mutable rebinding is forbidden.

The same digest-chained event that records each consequential transition carries its complete canonical journal batch. Under DM-012's journal-outer lock order, event fsync precedes state projection, the chain lock is released while the journal lock remains held, the CLI drains through the FR-019 idempotent batch path, and the reacquired chain lock records `journal_receipted`. Replay resumes an unreceipted batch before any new transition. `journal-outbox-pending` blocks transition, finalize, explicit task finish, and run close; it is never converted to a generic decision. The shared outer journal lock also serializes bound-chain creation/mutation against terminal task/run inspection and append, so an outbox cannot appear in their check-to-append interval. Gate completions map to `verification`, approvals/skips/landing facts map to `decision`, and task termination remains an explicit `journal task-finish` operation.

`journal ingest-chain` is the only retrospective path for an already-landed chain. Before one idempotent typed batch it MUST prove, in order: (1) chain schema and digest replay; (2) materialized-state equality; (3) repository identity; (4) policy identity; (5) generation freshness in authoritative chain state; (6) current-candidate gates; (7) reviewer package; (8) reviewer role; (9) reviewer iteration; (10) reviewer verdict; (11) required operator approval; (12) staged-diff-to-commit or merge proof; (13) monotonic transitions; (14) commit containment at the caller-supplied proposed closing HEAD; (15) explicit task membership; and (16) admitted scope membership. It captures caller-named files into FR-017's content-addressed in-run package first, requires an explicit task/outcome map, and emits only ordinary `verification`, `decision`, and terminal `task` records. An identical retry is a no-op; autoappend-plus-ingest cannot duplicate records. Failed later `validate --gates` leaves the run open. There is no `landed-external` status and no post-close synthesis.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-220**: the parser-pinned 25-member `forge-cli/1` enum and every existing 41-member `forge-cli/2` row remain byte-identical. Revision-9 commands continue to emit `forge-cli/2`; DM-016's v3 corpus extends its complete union to 53 members with exactly these twelve sorted exit-1 additions and failed preconditions: `archive-rerender-mismatch` (Archive candidate bytes do not equal deterministic rerender); `archive-size-limit` (Rendered archive exceeds the 16 MiB UTF-8 limit); `batch-idempotency-conflict` (Existing receipt or intent uses the idempotency key for a different normalized request or batch bytes); `batch-pending` (Another outstanding journal batch intent prevents the requested operation); `binding-invalid` (Structured binding is missing, malformed, stale, or inconsistent with its chain event); `ingest-proof-invalid` (At least one required external-chain ingest proof failed); `journal-outbox-pending` (A run-bound chain has an unreceipted event-carried journal batch); `legacy-recovery-approval-required` (Legacy archive mode lacks the matching per-instance operator decision and recovered HEAD); `option-duplicate` (A singleton option `--repo`, `--run-id`, or `--chain-id` occurs more than once); `option-empty` (A required singleton option has an empty value); `run-task-binding-invalid` (Run/task binding fails repository, task, scope, policy, or immutability validation); `run-task-binding-required` (Exactly one of `--run-id` and `--task` was supplied at chain start). The existing `frozen-chain` exit-2 member covers a journal suffix/inode divergence; no second exit-2 spelling is added. These additions are a closed union extension, not permission to broaden an old precondition.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-222**: the chain remains commit/merge authorization and generation authority; the journal remains narrative and gate-validation authority. `validate --gates` correlates only structured journal fields under FR-021 and MUST NOT open chain state, captured packages, or an outbox. Chain replay is mandatory at the producing builder, retrospective ingest, and archive renderer, and the resulting ordinary records are what validation consumes. Event-carried batches and receipts remove model transcription but do not make chain state a validation fallback.

Revision-11 amendment to **FR-210**, **FR-211**, **FR-219**, **FR-220**, and **FR-222**: Commit-family replay MUST authenticate every record's immutable source fact, treat receipted record sets as history, and currentness-check only the latest appended record set against its exact append state and prefix, except for the authenticated `commit_produced` landing window. A frozen commit chain MAY be sealed only by explicit operator abort or tombstone after commit-family proof from event authority or canonical matching raw state; `forge-chain-tombstone/1` MUST record operator identity, time, reason, and exact captured-or-absent state/event facts using crash-recoverable owner-controlled publication. Terminal guards MUST accept an authenticated tombstone only while captured artifact facts remain exact or after both artifacts are absent, and MUST reject partial or changed artifacts. Family enumeration MUST skip unreadable unrelated chains while emitting one escaped single-line stderr warning per skipped chain; explicit selection MUST continue to surface the chain's failure.

Revision-13 assignment-prefix amendment to **FR-221** (bead forge-plugin-di8): the CLI invocation matcher MUST skip leading shell assignment words (`VAR=value`) before and after any `env` prefix exactly as FR-090's git matcher already does, so `FORGE_SESSION_PID=123 python3 scripts/forge/cli.py commit approve …` is denied with the same exact literal as the bare form. The v1 corpus row `no-match-bare-assignment-without-env` pinned the opposite; its expectation is consciously superseded by the `fr223-hook-argv/3` generation (DM-016 Revision-13 amendment) rather than edited, so v1 bytes stay immutable. The skip is a named control with a disable-in-memory proof.

Revision-13 abort-disposition amendment to **FR-210** and **FR-222** (bead forge-plugin-437): An explicit operator abort of a readable run-bound chain that never landed is a terminal disposition, not a dead end at the terminal guards. When the aborted commit chain holds a staged candidate and no landed commit, `commit abort` MUST carry exactly one `decision` with `outcome: "chain-abort"`, bound to that candidate and sourced from the `chain_aborted` event, through the same journal outbox and receipt protocol as a landing; a chain aborted before it staged a candidate carries nothing, and a chain aborted after it landed keeps its landing authoritative and carries nothing. The terminal guards behind `journal task-finish` and `run-close` MUST accept a bound chain whose replay-authenticated state is `aborted` with a null journal outbox (commit family: `commit_result` records the abort time and no commit) as satisfying the landing predicate: when the journal holds a `chain-abort` decision citing it there MUST be exactly one and it MUST replay exactly; a `chain-landing` decision citing an aborted chain, more than one abort decision, an abort decision citing a chain whose authenticated state is not a terminal abort, a nonterminal bound chain without a landing, an aborted chain whose outbox is pending, and every partial or changed artifact MUST be refused with the terminal-guard diagnostic `forge: journal builder refused — bound chain proof is invalid`. `journal task-finish` MUST inspect only bound chains naming the finishing task, skipping chains bound to other tasks of the same run (which `run-close` inspects), while a journal-cited chain bound to another run or repository remains a refusal. The acceptance is a named terminal control (`abort-disposition`) with a disable-in-memory proof. This disposition is commit-family: merge chains that abort carry no decision and remain outside journal-only correlation (pending merge work), though the guards accept their authenticated terminal abort state. `commit abort` refuses `closed` and `aborted` chains with `state-precondition` before any mutation, so a landing is never rewritten and no duplicate abort event or decision can be produced; a duplicate or contradicting decision found in the journal is refused by the guards as above. A run-bound commit chain aborted before Revision 13, or whose `chain_aborted` event carried no batch, MAY receive its disposition retrospectively through `commit abort-disposition --chain-id <id>`: the verb requires `--chain-id` and refuses with `state-precondition` unless the chain is run-bound, `aborted` with a null outbox, holds a staged candidate and no landed commit, no `chain_aborted` or earlier `abort_disposition_recorded` event carried a journal batch, the run journal is readable, and it holds no `chain-abort` decision citing the chain — each precondition evaluated independently and attributably; a chain whose bound task is already terminal refuses with `run-task-binding-invalid` as any bound verb does; it then appends the self-event `abort_disposition_recorded` (details exactly `reason`, state unchanged, admitted only from `aborted`) carrying that one `chain-abort` decision through the ordinary outbox and receipt path, so the terminal guards, FR-021 correlation, and the archive's carried-record obligation see the disposition exactly as a Revision-13 abort's. The verb is mechanical and model-issuable; an identical retry refuses.

Revision-13 tombstone-disposition amendment to **DM-001**, **FR-021**, **FR-210**, and **FR-222** (bead forge-plugin-11a): a run-bound commit chain that froze and was sealed by an operator tombstone (Revision 11, `forge-chain-tombstone/1`) never landed, yet the gate records it drained remain bound to its `chain_id` in the journal and no chain event can ever carry its disposition. Such a chain MAY receive its disposition through `commit abort-disposition --run-id <run> --chain-id <chain>`. The tombstone record is the chain's immutable source fact: the carried `decision` has `outcome: "chain-abort"`, `basis` exactly `[".forge/chains/tombstones/<chain>.json"]`, and a binding of the ordinary DM-001 shape whose `source_record.event_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the canonical tombstone record bytes, whose `candidate` is the single `staged-diff-sha256` value every journal record bound to that chain carries, whose `review` is null, and whose `binding_id` follows the ordinary preimage. The verb refuses with `state-precondition` and the message `commit abort-disposition refused — tombstoned chain is not dispositionable` unless `--run-id` is given, the tombstone's state and events artifacts are both `absent`, the named run's journal is readable and cites the chain, the cited records name exactly one task and exactly one candidate, and no `chain-abort` or `chain-landing` decision already cites the chain; each refusal names the missing precondition in `expected`/`observed`, appends nothing, and an identical retry refuses. The decision is appended through the typed decision builder under the named run's journal lock with the tombstone digest as its idempotency key; because the frozen chain could not be dispositioned while its task was open, the builder admits the decision on a terminal task and FR-021's `terminal task '<task>' precedes a bound chain abort decision` ordering does not apply to a tombstone disposition (journal-only test: a `chain-abort` decision whose `basis` is exactly its chain's tombstone path). Binding authentication gains one branch: when a chain's events file is absent and a valid tombstone with both artifacts absent stands for it, the only binding the chain can carry is this decision's, reconstructed from the tombstone digest and the caller-supplied candidate and compared for exact `binding_id` equality; `replay_only` requests, any other outer type or outcome, a tombstone with captured artifacts, and a candidate that is not 64 lowercase hex refuse with the binding replay diagnostic. The terminal guards behind `journal task-finish` and `run-close` keep Revision 11's acceptance of a tombstoned chain the journal carries no disposition for; when the journal cites a `chain-abort` decision against a tombstoned chain there MUST be exactly one, it MUST authenticate through that branch, its candidate MUST equal the candidate of every other record bound to the chain, and a `chain-landing` decision beside that abort or a second abort refuses with `forge: journal builder refused — bound chain proof is invalid` (a tombstoned chain the journal cites with a landing but no abort keeps Revision 11's acceptance unchanged). FR-021 then retires the chain's records exactly as any abort does. The acceptance is the named terminal control `tombstone-disposition`, the resolver branch the named builder control `tombstone-binding`, and the ordering exemption the named correlation control `tombstone-disposition`, each with a disable-in-memory proof. Archive rendering of a tombstoned chain and of this decision is not authorized by this amendment: the archive's chain package still requires both chain artifacts for every journal-bound chain, so a run whose journal cites a tombstoned chain closes `passed` but does not archive until the archive amendment (bead forge-plugin-cyg) lands.

The phrases "this revision" and "the phase-4 revision" retained inside the parser-pinned FR-221 text describe the Revision-4 commit-slice boundary, not Revision 7's current authority. FR-221 itself remains the shipped phase-1 hook contract; only a later implementation candidate governed by FR-238..FR-241 may change that runtime posture.

### Forge CLI merge, push, and later-phase boundaries (FR-230..FR-243)

- **FR-230** (MUST): Revision-7 scope and sequencing. The phase-1.1 additions to DM-003, DM-013, FR-214, FR-216, and §8 describe shipped HEAD behavior only and MUST NOT tighten it. FR-230..FR-237 are phase-3 merge-chain authority; FR-238..FR-241 are phase-4 push, activation, hook, migration, and v2-corpus authority; FR-242 is a non-authorizing phase-5 admission boundary; FR-243 is a non-authorizing phase-6 declaration. Phase-3/4 implementations are later control-class changes and MUST land in order after the phase-1 dual-accept and phase-2 dogfood prerequisites. They MUST compose the existing tested halt, classification, committed-policy, gate, mutation, assertion, invariant, review, lock, telemetry, and archive controls rather than reimplement them. DM-012, FR-210..FR-224, every phase-1 `forge-cli/1` command, every v1 corpus/eval/manifest byte, and the DM-006 dual-accept remain unchanged. New merge and push commands use the separate DM-014/DM-017 records and `forge-cli/2`. Revision 7 itself changes no implementation surface and authorizes neither phase-5 deletion nor phase-6 execution.

Revision-9 temporal clarification to **FR-230**: its sentence that DM-012 and FR-210..FR-224 "remain unchanged" constrained the Revision-7 candidate relative to the then-shipped Revision-4 baseline. It does not negate the expressly labeled Revision-9 amendments to DM-012 and FR-210, FR-220, or FR-222; every clause not expressly amended remains unchanged.

- **FR-231** (MUST): Merge start, trusted target discovery, and candidate identity. `merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]` MUST canonicalize the supplied path without following an ambiguous symlink spelling and match exactly one registered entry from `git worktree list --porcelain`; it MUST reject a missing path, main checkout, detached worktree, default-branch worktree, foreign Git common directory, non-local branch, or worktree already owned by a live merge chain. The ownership claim MUST be serialized and recorded atomically before admission can succeed. The source status MUST produce zero bytes from `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all`, including no untracked file and no in-progress Git operation. Target discovery reads only the main checkout's committed plugin-schema `.forge-manifest`, fixes remote to `origin`, fixes destination to `refs/heads/<default_branch>`, and never trusts the candidate worktree's manifest or a symbolic moving ref as the bound target. After the read-only path/policy/status preflight, start acquires the complete common lock, rechecks those predicates, publishes the exact DM-014 pre-generation chain/ownership events, and performs its fixed-target fetch/tip resolution only through the null-generation bootstrap `fetch_intent`, FR-236's process fence, and timeout contract. The successful `fetch_result` atomically binds generation 1; a failed result leaves the closed bootstrap state recoverable without inventing a generation. Refresh uses the same locked/fenced fetch discipline. Executable policy comes only from the candidate's committed HEAD and retains every FR-061 sentinel. Start computes DM-014's full candidate generation, classifies the exact three-dot diff with promote-only tiering, and binds every later record to its full candidate HEAD, fetched base, diff digest, policy digest, target, worktree identity, and generation digest. A halt, unreadable policy/manifest, common-lock failure, failed fetch, dirty/invalid worktree, or non-atomic ownership claim refuses before any branch/history mutation.

Revision-10 run-scope amendment to **FR-231**: immediately after a run-bound start's successful fixed-base fetch and before its generation-1 `fetch_result`, classification, or any gate child, the CLI derives the complete three-dot changed-path set by this exact direct argv, with no shell: `git --no-pager --no-replace-objects -c core.quotePath=false -c color.ui=false -c diff.renames=copies -c diff.renameLimit=0 -c diff.algorithm=myers -C <canonical-worktree> diff --no-color -O/dev/null --name-status -z --find-renames=50% --find-copies=50% --find-copies-harder -l0 --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --ignore-submodules=none --diff-filter=ACDMRTUXB <full-remote-tip>...<full-candidate-head> --`. Before launch it unsets every inherited variable whose name begins `GIT_CONFIG_` and exact `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_NAMESPACE`, `GIT_PREFIX`, `GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF`, `GIT_DIFF_OPTS`, `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE`, and `GIT_GRAFT_FILE`, then overlays exactly `LC_ALL=C`, `LANG=C`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, `GIT_PAGER=cat`, and `PAGER=cat`. It uses FR-236's direct-argv isolated process group, 65,536-byte combined cap, 1200-second fail-closed timeout, and complete-result proof; an unavailable, capped, timed-out, nonzero, or malformed result creates no partial scope proof and takes existing `run-task-binding-invalid`, never `run-scope-exceeded`.

For a run-bound bootstrap, `fetch_intent.payload.scope_request` has exactly `run_id`, `task_id`, `task_files`, `admitted_scope`, `command_template`, `command_template_digest`, and `environment_digest`; the arrays are the immutable journal snapshots. `command_template` has exactly `schema`, `worktree`, `candidate_head`, and `remote_tip_source`; its schema is `forge-run-scope-command-template/1`, its next two members equal the enclosing intent's canonical worktree and pre-fetch HEAD, and `remote_tip_source` is exact `scope_fetch_binding.remote_tip`. `command_template_digest` hashes its DM-013 canonical bytes, and `environment_digest` binds the exact unset/overlay contract above. The template deterministically expands to FR-231's pinned argv by substituting only the later binding's full remote-tip OID; a resolved `command_digest` cannot appear in the pre-fetch intent. For an unbound bootstrap, `scope_request` is null.

The successful run-bound fetch child publishes exactly `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/scope-fetch-<fetch-intent-digest>-<inflight-digest>.json` before scope execution. That file's schema is `forge-run-scope-fetch-binding/1`, and it has exactly `schema`, `chain_id`, `fetch_intent_digest`, `scope_request_digest`, `candidate_head`, `remote_tip`, `command_template_digest`, `command_digest`, `environment_digest`, `publication`, `retained_inflight`, `child_result`, `recorded_at`, and `digest`; `digest` hashes DM-013 canonical bytes for the preceding thirteen members. `scope_request_digest` hashes the complete canonical `scope_request`, `remote_tip` is the full OID returned by the successful fixed-tip child, and `command_digest` hashes the exact fully resolved argv. `publication` has exactly `canonical_path`, `temporary_path`, `device`, and `inode`; the canonical path is the repository-relative path above, the temporary path is exactly that path plus `.tmp-<retained-inflight-nonce>`, and its nonnegative identity members are obtained by `fstat` immediately after the temporary file's exclusive creation and before constructing the canonical sidecar bytes.

`retained_inflight` has exactly `path`, `device`, `inode`, `inflight_digest`, `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `operation`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `started_at`, `intent_digest`, and `nonce`. It copies the strict-valid canonical `forge-rebase-inflight/1` path, physical identity, digest, and record; owner kind is `merge`, both chain IDs match, intent digest equals `fetch_intent_digest`, and operation is the retained final bootstrap `fetch` or `tip-resolution` operation whose successful result authoritatively resolves the full fixed-fetch tip. `child_result` has exactly `operation`, `intent_digest`, `inflight_digest`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `exit`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, `group_dead_at`, `resolved_tip`, and `recorded_at`. Its operation, intent digest, in-flight digest, host, PID, and PGID match the corresponding `retained_inflight` members byte-for-byte; exit is zero, all three failure flags are false, group death is proved, and `resolved_tip` equals `remote_tip`. No later tracking ref or remote query may supply either value.

Empty stdout is the empty path set; nonempty stdout MUST end in NUL. The parser consumes NUL fields: a status matching `[ADMTUXB]` consumes one following path; `R[0-9]{1,3}` or `C[0-9]{1,3}` with a decimal score in `0..100` consumes old and new paths and adds both. An undetected rename is the ordinary `D` plus `A` pair. Every path decodes as strict UTF-8 and already has the exact repository-relative `/` spelling admitted by the existing scope-item grammar; the parser performs no realpath, Unicode, case, separator, quote, or dot-segment normalization. It deduplicates and sorts by UTF-8 bytes, then tests each path separately against both the snapshotted task `files[]` and run admitted scope through FR-192's shared positive-pathspec containment matcher.

Every bootstrap `fetch_result.payload` has `scope_fetch_binding` and `scope_proof`. A successful run-bound generation-1 result copies the complete sidecar object into the first and has a non-null proof. A failed scope-command result has that same non-null binding and a null proof; a fetch or binding-publication failure before a valid sidecar, and every unbound result, has both members null. The non-null proof has exactly `schema`, `run_id`, `task_id`, `generation_digest`, `remote_tip`, `candidate_head`, `command_template_digest`, `command_digest`, `environment_digest`, `scope_fetch_binding_digest`, `output_digest`, `task_files`, `admitted_scope`, `changed_paths`, `out_of_scope_paths`, `result`, and `digest`. `schema` is `forge-run-scope-proof/1`; `scope_fetch_binding_digest` is the copied sidecar's digest; the command/template/environment members match the intent and binding; and `output_digest` hashes raw scope stdout bytes. All arrays are unique and UTF-8-byte-sorted snapshots; `result` is `contained` or `exceeded`; and `digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for the preceding sixteen members. Replay revalidates the binding, command expansion, and cited immutable task and admitted-scope snapshot against the authenticated event tail and journal before trusting the proof.

For `result: exceeded`, the CLI creates no classification, gate, or review evidence and leaves the remote and source branch/history untouched. The ordinary acquired-release `terminal_preconditions_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema, target_terminal, terminal_disposition, release_mode, source_state, scope_proof_digest, fetch_result_event_digest, generation_digest, worktree_identity, branch, candidate_head, current_head, status_output_digest, push_intent_event_digests, git_mutation_intent_event_digests, unresolved_fence_digests}`. `schema` is `forge-run-scope-abort-preconditions/1`; the next four values are exactly `aborted`, `ordinary`, `acquired`, and `classifying`; `scope_proof_digest` is the authenticated proof digest; `current_head` equals `candidate_head`; `status_output_digest` hashes the exact zero-byte clean-status output; and the final three arrays are empty, with the Git-mutation array ranging over rebase, continue, abort, push, containment, worktree-remove, and branch-delete intents. The CLI then appends `ownership_release_intent` with `target_terminal: aborted`, `terminal_disposition: ordinary`, `release_mode: acquired`, and that precondition digest; matching `ownership_released`; then `aborted`. Only after that terminal sequence is durable does it return exit 1 with new `forge-cli/2` reason `run-scope-exceeded` and exact diagnostic `forge: merge start refused — changed paths exceed bound task scope`. Recovery after the release intent revalidates only authenticated event/proof and claim identity and never reruns the diff, fetch, scope snapshot, or remote observation. A mismatch is exact `frozen-chain`. This row does not widen scope and never leaves a live ownership claim.

- **FR-232** (MUST): Merge verbs, chain selection, and lifecycle. Every post-start command MUST receive `--chain-id <id>`; no implicit latest/single-chain selection exists. The complete command surface is:

```text
forge status --chain-id <id>
forge merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]
forge merge refresh --chain-id <id>
forge merge verify --chain-id <id>
forge merge gate run <gate-id> --chain-id <id>
forge review request --chain-id <id>
forge review attach --verdict-file <file> --chain-id <id>
forge review disposition --finding <n> --severity <sev> --resolution <text> --chain-id <id>
forge merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>
forge merge finalize --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --continue --paths <path>... --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --abort-rebase --chain-id <id>
forge merge cleanup --chain-id <id>
forge merge abort [--reason <text>] --chain-id <id>
```

There is no `merge skip`; FR-060 remains fail-closed. The state enum is exactly:

```text
classifying, verifying, reviewing, revising,
awaiting_approval, authorized, rebasing, rebase_conflict,
reverifying, reverification_failed, pushing, pushed,
cleanup_pending, closed, aborted
```

The complete state-changing transition table is normative.
