For this table, a merge chain's transition state is the tuple of its scalar state, `integration.condition`, `cleanup.condition`, inactive/active classification, review iteration, pending finding-disposition/co-sign status, pending ownership-release intent/result/terminal target, `worktree.claim.status`, current per-chain-lease ownership, and the final intended HEAD's DM-015 parse result whenever a row could create push intent. Every state-changing row below implicitly requires an internally consistent event/state record, the row-appropriate claim status, and a lease owned by the invoking process; the start row requires an absent ownership slot. A live, foreign, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or mismatched lease or an otherwise unresolved ownership slot therefore has no admitting row, rather than being a transition event of its own. Commands not admitted by a row refuse with the byte-exact `state-precondition` meaning and do not change state; malformed or mismatched chain/event data, including a claim that diverges from its authoritative ownership events, instead satisfies the byte-exact `frozen-chain` corruption or irresolvable-divergence meaning and exits 2. The first two pending-release rows have absolute precedence: their fsynced release intent is the remote/cleanup observation cutoff, so neither a later external remote change nor another result row may retarget the selected terminal event. With no pending release and while the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals the last attempted-head member, current/latest attempted-head containment precedes older-only containment, and both precede every retry, movement, inactivity, or abort row; older-only containment blocks abort/blind retry but is not current-generation pushed truth. The sole fresh-attempt exception is an active later epoch after partial historical truth whose current `epoch_intent` event follows the most recent `push_intent` and has not yet produced a later push intent. Its preserved vector cannot classify the not-yet-recorded epoch, so the final pre-intent observation takes the ordinary unchanged/moved/unavailable row and only the unchanged row appends the new push intent. OID equality does not cancel this digest/sequence-bound exception; a no-op epoch may deliberately append the same intended HEAD again. The next four inactivity rows precede every remaining scalar-state row; they classify and persist truth from work already admitted while active or from an allowed observation, but delegate neither admission nor automatic execution of any later integration step. The next post-start-absence row then precedes every remaining scalar-state row so exact v2 reason preconditions are never broadened:

| From / event | To | Normative result |
|---|---|---|
| any nonterminal state with an authoritative `ownership_release_intent`, `worktree.claim.status == releasing`, and matching immutable terminal-precondition/claim identities; bare `merge recover` (or `merge cleanup` for target `closed`) | payload-selected `aborted` or `closed` | Treat the fsynced intent's bound remote/cleanup observation as the transaction cutoff; revalidate only event/digest continuity, the selected target, and local claim identity, append/materialize `ownership_released`, then append only that terminal event. Do not perform or require a fresh remote observation and do not repeat Git, remote, cleanup, or push effects |
| any nonterminal state with authoritative `ownership_released`, `worktree.claim.status == released`, and its selected terminal event absent; bare `merge recover` (or `merge cleanup` for target `closed`) | payload-selected `aborted` or `closed` | Revalidate the release-intent/result digests and local projection, then append only the cutoff-selected terminal event; no later remote change retargets a transaction after logical ownership is gone |
| any nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least one push intent, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; authoritative present-destination observation proves that last attempted member contained | `pushed` | Set `landed_head` to that current head, persist the complete vector and current-generation pushed truth, normalize `integration.condition` and `primary_condition` to `none` while retaining immutable result evidence, and perform no new push; this applies despite inactivity or an earlier condition |
| active nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least two distinct push attempts, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; authoritative observation proves that head uncontained but one or more older attempted heads contained | `authorized` with `remote-moved` | Set `landed_head` to the latest older member proved contained, persist the complete vector, reset the movement counter, and perform no push or abort. Integrate the current generation against that remote truth in a new bounded epoch; never call the newer unlanded generation `pushed` |
| inactive nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least two distinct push attempts, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; a fresh observation proves that head uncontained but one or more older attempted heads contained, while the current worktree/branch remain exact clean/unchanged and no fence/process is live or unresolved | `aborted` with terminal disposition `historical-landed-superseded` | Set `landed_head` to the latest contained older member, bind the full vector plus every attempt/result digest and current unlanded HEAD/worktree/branch/no-fence proof into the release cutoff, release logical ownership, retain the worktree/branch without cleanup or deletion, and append the tagged `aborted` event. Return exit-0 `ok` partial with exact next `forge merge start --worktree <same-absolute-path>`; this abandons only the current generation and never claims the older landing absent or the newer head pushed |
| a tuple becomes inactive after a child or side effect was admitted while active; its result/observation is not yet durable | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Persist and classify that already-started result, including fetched, conflict, integrated, failed, pushed, or cleanup truth, but stop before every otherwise automatic next child; this result write is not new authorization |
| inactive `rebasing` or `rebase_conflict`; observation-only bare `merge recover` | `authorized`, `rebase_conflict`, `reverifying`, `revising`, or same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | Select respectively only exact no-side-effect, owned-conflict, integrated-result, proven-restoration-after-failure, or unmatched-state truth; persist it and return partial recovery with exact next `status` or safe abort, but do not run gates, continue conflicts, retry, refresh, review, or finalize |
| any other inactive nonterminal tuple; `status`, observation-only bare `merge recover`, owned rebase abort/restoration, cleanup after pushed truth, pending ownership-release completion, the exact proven-not-landed safe abort, or the exact historical-landed-superseded release above | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Permit only the named inspection, observation, safety restoration, cleanup, release, or abort action through its exact row; no new integration-target fetch, gate, conflict continuation, rebase, push, retry, or churn acknowledgement occurs |
| any inactive nonterminal tuple; every other verb or continuation | unchanged refusal | Return exact `state-precondition`; inactivity is part of the complete tuple and cannot be cleared or re-armed |
| any pre-push nonterminal chain after start; the recorded worktree path is absent | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | The v2 `worktree-missing` code is start-only and `worktree-invalid` requires an existing path, so persist the unclassifiable Git-state truth and return exact `state-precondition` for mutation; status and observation-only recovery remain, and no path is reconstructed or silently rebound |
| run-bound bootstrap `classifying` after successful generation-1 fetch; complete three-dot changed paths exceed the bound task's admitted scope | `aborted` | Before classification or a gate child, bind the sorted scope proof, execute the ordinary acquired-ownership release sequence, emit exact `forge: merge start refused — changed paths exceed bound task scope`, and return `run-scope-exceeded`; no widening, branch/history mutation, or live claim remains |
| no chain; `merge start` | `classifying → verifying` | Validate the source, acquire the complete common lock, atomically claim the worktree in DM-014's null-generation bootstrap, fetch under the bootstrap intent and FR-236 fence, bind generation 1 on successful result, and classify |
| active `classifying` after an interrupted start/refresh; bare `merge recover` or `merge refresh`, all admission/classification predicates PASS | `verifying` | Reconcile the claim/events, bind the current generation, and persist classification |
| `classifying`; admission/classification predicate non-PASS | `classifying` | Return its exact `worktree-invalid`, `dirty-worktree`, `fetch-failed`, or `policy-unreadable` refusal; no history mutation |
| `verifying`; `merge gate run` or `merge verify`, required evidence still incomplete | `verifying` | Record each current result; a non-PASS returns `merge-gate-failed` and is retryable without advancing |
| `verifying`; initial Gates 1–2 complete | `reviewing` | All required mechanical evidence is current; scoped mutation evidence is advisory |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8; `review request` or `review attach` | `reviewing` | Persist the exact package or verdict before applying its PASS/BLOCK successor row |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8 with one outstanding above-MINOR disposition; another above-MINOR `review disposition` | unchanged refusal | Append no event and change no evidence; emit exact `forge: review disposition refused — above-MINOR disposition already awaits operator co-sign` and return `state-precondition` |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8; `review disposition` | same source state | Persist the exact disposition; an above-MINOR disposition sets a pending co-sign and cannot advance |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8; exact operator `merge approve` for a pending above-MINOR disposition | same source state | Bind a finding-disposition co-sign to chain, finding, full HEAD, and generation; it is not Gate-4 approval |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review PASS, non-control | `authorized` | Record Gate 3 plus Gate 4 summary |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review PASS, control | `awaiting_approval` | Record Gate 3/Gate 4 and present the exact full HEAD |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review BLOCK | `revising` | Increment iteration; on reaching 8, persist residual risk and refuse further review cycles with `iteration-cap` |
| any active mutable pre-push state except an active rebase, with iteration below 8 and no pending above-MINOR disposition co-sign; `merge refresh` | `classifying → verifying` | Acquire the complete common lock for any target fetch, re-observe current source HEAD, invalidate stale-generation evidence, regenerate, and reclassify; in `revising` this also permits a deliberate unchanged-HEAD restart after a safe rebase abort |
| `reviewing` or `revising` at iteration 8; request, attach, disposition, co-sign, refresh, or another review successor | unchanged refusal | Return `iteration-cap`; only `status` or safe abort remains |
| active `awaiting_approval` without `remote-churn`; exact operator approval | `authorized` | Bind Gate-4 approval to the current chain, full HEAD, and generation digest |
| active `awaiting_approval` with `remote-churn`; exact operator approval | `authorized` | Bind the acknowledgement to chain/HEAD/generation, reset `remote_movement_count` to zero, clear the condition, and re-arm one later epoch |
| active `authorized`; `merge finalize` | `rebasing` | Halt, acquire locks, recheck tuple, persist fetch/rebase intent, clear only a retryable `fetch-failed`/`remote-moved`/`non-fast-forward` condition, and start one epoch |
| active `rebasing`; fetch fails before rebase | `authorized` | Remote untouched; persist `fetch-failed`, release locks, and permit a later finalize/recover |
| active `authorized` with `fetch-failed`, `remote-moved`, or `non-fast-forward`; bare `merge recover` | `rebasing` | Acquire the common lock, re-read state and remote, persist a fresh intent, clear that retryable condition, and start one bounded epoch under the same preconditions as `merge finalize` |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` observes no rebase side effect, no durable rebase result reports launch failure, nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach, and the exact original tuple remains | `authorized` | Clear only the incomplete intent/condition; a later finalize may retry. A durable failed result instead has precedence and takes the proven-restoration `rebase-failed` row |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` observes the exact owned conflict predicate | `rebase_conflict` | Materialize the recorded conflict inventory; remote remains untouched |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` proves the exact integrated-result predicate | `reverifying` | Materialize the integrated generation and run the complete matrix-selected mechanical pair |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` proves restoration after failure | `revising` | Persist `rebase-failed`; refresh/re-review or safe abort is required |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` matches no exact predicate | `rebasing` | Persist `foreign-git-state`; observation only, no mutation or guessed transition |
| active `rebasing`; recoverable conflict | `rebase_conflict` | Remote untouched; accept only status, recovery, or safe abort surfaces |
| active `rebasing`; non-conflict rebase failure with proven restoration | `revising` | Persist `rebase-failed`; source is restored and must be refreshed/re-reviewed or aborted |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; the integrated/reverified result is durable and final intended HEAD has duplicate/malformed/unknown DM-015 immediately before push intent | `revising` | Persist the completed-result plus invalid-mode classification as an admitted internal transition and create no push intent; this row itself emits no CLI result |
| `revising` with that exact invalid-final-mode classification; the same invocation attempts to continue toward push | unchanged refusal | The push continuation has no admitting row: release locks, emit exact `forge: history mutation mode invalid — repair committed .forge-manifest through Forge CLI`, return a `forge-cli/2` refusal with byte-exact `state-precondition`, and require exact approved repair plus `merge refresh` |
| active `rebasing`; pure unchanged fast-forward with no earlier park and final DM-015 valid | `pushing` | Recheck current authorization and persist push intent; FR-235's pure-fast-forward row applies |
| active `rebasing`; integration requires mechanical reruns | `reverifying` | Persist integrated generation, reload policy, and run the matrix-selected checks |
| active `rebase_conflict`; successful `recover --continue` | `rebase_conflict` or `reverifying` | Persist the next conflict inventory, or enter integrated re-verification when rebase completes |
| `rebase_conflict`; proven `recover --abort-rebase` | `revising` | Restore pre-rebase HEAD and exact clean state; remote remains untouched |
| `rebasing` or `rebase_conflict`; `recover --abort-rebase` or active-rebase `merge abort` returns non-PASS but exact restoration is proven | `revising` | Persist exact `rebase-failed`, retain logical ownership, and permit a later safe ordinary abort or refresh; no terminal event is invented |
| `rebasing` or `rebase_conflict`; the same abort surface cannot prove restoration | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | Return exact `rebase-failed`, retain ownership, and admit only observation until an exact recovery predicate matches |
| `reverifying`; `merge gate run` or `merge verify`, required integrated evidence still incomplete | `reverifying` | Record current generation-bound results; remain parked until the complete pair passes or a later row applies |
| `reverifying`; Gate 1 or Gate 2 non-PASS | `reverification_failed` | Remote untouched; a retry reruns both integrated gates |
| active `reverification_failed`; bare `merge recover` with unchanged tuple | `reverifying` | Clear only the retryable gate condition and rerun the complete required integrated pair |
| `reverifying`; review-relevant HEAD/diff/policy change | `reviewing` | Persist integrated generation, park, release locks, and obtain fresh Gate 3/Gate 4/approval as required |
| active `reverifying`; matrix permits carry-forward, final DM-015 is valid, and all final checks PASS | `pushing` | Persist and fsync push intent before invoking Git |
| `rebasing` or `reverifying`; chain becomes inactive before push intent | same scalar state | Persist inactivity, release locks, return exact `state-precondition`, and admit only status or safe abort; no observation, counter, or approval can re-arm integration |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds a present remote-only tip movement, defeats epoch 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist `remote-moved`, the new remote-only generation/carry-forward, and incremented counter; release locks and return exit 0 with `ok: true`, reason `ok`, and the exact next `merge finalize --chain-id <id>`; this is safe partial progress and no push occurs |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds the destination authoritatively absent, defeats epoch 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist `remote-moved` and the incremented counter on the current generation, release locks, and return exit-0 `ok` partial progress; no successor generation or push is created until a later fetch yields a full tip |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds the eighth consecutive present-tip movement or authoritative absence | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`; include a successor carry-forward only for a present tip, release locks, and require exact operator `merge approve` acknowledgement before another epoch, even for non-control; no push occurs |
| `pushing`; push result or crash-before-result fact is durable but no authoritative remote observation is durable; in-epoch continuation or bare `merge recover` | `pushing` | Preserve the exact result/no-result fact and perform authoritative observation before any terminal claim, refusal classification, or retry; no result code is inferred from process exit alone |
| inactive `pushing`; authoritative present or absent destination proves every attempted intended HEAD not contained | `pushing` | Preserve observation and every historical result/no-result fact, reset the counter, release locks, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next `merge abort --chain-id <id>`; no retry, re-integration, churn increment, or acknowledgement is admitted |
| active `pushing`; authoritative present-destination observation shows the expected old tip and proves every attempted intended HEAD absent, regardless of retained current push-result classification or no result | `pushing` | Preserve the exact result/no-result fact and reset the remote-only counter because movement did not defeat this epoch. If start-pipe proof shows this lock epoch authorized no push child, the just-completed observation is its immediate recheck and the existing unlaunched intent authorizes its sole attempt without another history member. If a child was authorized, release with exact next `merge recover --chain-id <id>` for a new epoch and append a deliberate duplicate only there: retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push authoritative tip cannot be resolved | `authorized` | Persist exact `fetch-failed`, release locks, and refuse without creating push intent or invoking push; a later epoch repeats the final mechanical checks |
| active `pushing`; recorded ordinary update was rejected non-fast-forward and authoritative destination is absent or differs from expected old tip while every attempted head is uncontained, defeat 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist exact `non-fast-forward`, increment the remote-movement counter, and require a later recover/finalize to re-enter the matrix; never blindly push |
| active `pushing`; the same recorded non-fast-forward rejection is the eighth consecutive remote-only defeat | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`, clear `non-fast-forward`, and require the exact operator acknowledgement before another epoch |
| active `pushing`; observation-only destination movement or absence without a retained non-fast-forward result and with every attempted head uncontained, and durable evidence is a successful push result or start-pipe proof that no push executed, defeat 1–7 | `authorized` | Preserve the result/proof, persist `remote-moved`, increment the counter, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next finalize; remote movement alone defeated the epoch |
| active `pushing`; the same independently proven remote-only movement is the eighth consecutive defeat | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`; require the exact operator acknowledgement before another epoch |
| active `pushing`; destination movement or absence without retained non-fast-forward and with every attempted head uncontained, but retained `known-failure`/`outcome-unknown` or no-result evidence cannot prove that movement alone defeated the epoch | `authorized` | Preserve that result/no-result fact, persist `remote-moved`, reset the consecutive counter to zero, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next finalize; never claim non-fast-forward or churn |
| `pushing`; remote cannot be observed authoritatively | `pushing` | Persist `push-outcome-unknown`, reset the remote-only counter, and admit only status/recover; retry is forbidden until observation succeeds |
| any nonterminal scalar state; a fenced child cannot be proved terminated after timeout/output-limit/launch handling | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | This row has precedence over every operation-specific scalar successor: retain the exact fence/process/intent evidence, return the operation-specific FR-236 failure code, and forbid every later child or state mutation except death-proof plus observation recovery |
| `pushed`; cleanup succeeds | `closed` | Remove worktree/branch only under FR-236 containment rules, durably release logical ownership, then record terminal state |
| `pushed`; cleanup fails | `cleanup_pending` | Preserve pushed truth; cleanup remains independently retryable |
| `cleanup_pending`; `merge cleanup` | `closed` or `cleanup_pending` | Resume only incomplete cleanup steps; never re-run push |
| any durable primary state, including `closed`; explicit lock release fails | same primary state | Persist `lock-release-failed`; only status or bare recover to prove/retry release is admitted |
| any state with `lock-release-failed`; bare `merge recover` proves release | same primary state | Clear the release condition and restore its `primary_condition`; never repeat the primary side effect |
| any state with `foreign-git-state`, or any nonterminal state whose exact current intent/owner/reservation/fence identities match a newly completed FR-235/FR-236 operator quarantine; bare `merge recover` | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Under the acquired lease and complete lock, first append the exact ordered quarantine history and persist `foreign-git-state` when a parent crashed before that condition; then re-observe the operation-specific Git/remote truth and transition solely when one exact FR-236 predicate is satisfied. The identity-bound ingress admits no new side effect before observation |
| `pushing`; `merge abort` under complete common lock and chain lease, with one fresh authoritative present-or-absent destination observation proving every `attempted_heads` member uncontained and no live or unresolved fence/process | `aborted` | Bind the full attempted-head array and all-false containment vector, preserve every historical result, perform no retry, durably release logical ownership, and append `aborted`; this edge remains legal after inactivity |
| any scalar state before `pushing` with no unresolved Git/abort failure; ordinary `merge abort` | `aborted` | When Git rebase state may exist, acquire the complete common lock and chain lease and first prove its owned abort/restoration. If no push intent/attempt exists, prove remote untouched; otherwise use one fresh observation to prove every historical attempted intended HEAD uncontained and no fence/process remains, preserving every historical push result. Current-head containment takes the higher-priority `pushed` row; older-only containment takes the higher-priority active-reconciliation or inactive tagged-terminal row and forbids this ordinary abort. Only no attempts or an all-false vector permits ordinary logical ownership release and terminal state |
| bootstrap pre-push state with authoritative `ownership_intent`, `worktree.claim.status == unpublished`, and the canonical claim path proven absent under the acquired chain lease; `merge abort` or bare `merge recover` selecting abort | `aborted` | Revalidate absence immediately before each protected write, append release intent/result in `never-published` mode, materialize `released` with null inode, then append `aborted`; no pathname is removed and no ownership was acquired |
| `pushing` without the exact proven-not-landed abort predicate, `pushed`, or `cleanup_pending`; ordinary abort | unchanged refusal | Recovery MUST establish remote truth first; no other ordinary abort is legal |
| `closed` or `aborted`; `status` | unchanged success | Terminal inspection is idempotent |

A chain past `inactive_after` accepts only `status`, observation-only or safety-restoring `recover`, cleanup when push truth is already established, and a safe terminal release before current-generation pushed truth — either FR-232's exact fresh authoritative all-false/no-live-process abort after a push attempt or its exact `historical-landed-superseded` tagged abort when only an older attempt landed. The latter retains the newer clean worktree/branch for a wholly fresh chain and performs no cleanup/deletion. Inactivity invalidates authorization before any future integration; no retry, counter-driven acknowledgement, or observation result can re-arm it, and it never makes pushed/cleanup truth expire. Merge commands never emit the byte-pinned v1 `inactive-chain` code because that code's exact precondition permits only status/abort; a disallowed merge verb instead returns `state-precondition` under this table. The tuple definition above does not broaden that reason: the attempted verb literally has no admitting row for the observed transition state. `closed` and both tagged forms of `aborted` are terminal and status-idempotent except that either admits the exact common-lock-release recovery row above, and none can be materialized before its ownership-release result is durable.

- **FR-233** (MUST): Merge gates, ordering, evidence, and invalidation. Merge mechanical gate IDs are exactly `gate-1`, `stack:<category>`, `assertion-sensor`, and `invariant:<row-number>`; the invariant number is its one-based ordinal among all committed invariant data rows and only `enforcement == "merge"` rows participate. `merge verify` and `merge gate run` MUST preserve this initial order: (1) Gate 1 once; (2) scoped mutation immediately after Gate 1, always advisory and never a gate ID; (3) stack validations for touched categories, merge invariants, and the assertion sensor as Gate 2; (4) unconditional fresh `review-final` Gate 3, including fast constituent commits; (5) Gate 4 summary and exact candidate-HEAD approval for control. Every executable uses committed policy, one complete shell cell as one `bash -c` argv element, an isolated process group, a 65,536-byte combined-output cap, FR-149's 1200-second fail-closed gate timeout or the narrower configured control timeout, and CLI-captured exit, duration, digest, bounded transcript, and generation. After every executable, the CLI MUST recompute full HEAD, exact three-dot diff digest, policy digest, target tuple, and exact clean status. Any unexpected mutation or tuple change invalidates downstream evidence and parks through reclassification; a scoped mutation runner may not silently change candidate bytes. Merge Gate-1/Gate-2 failures use `merge-gate-failed` and leave the remote untouched. Merge approval imports no DM-006 30-minute TTL: it remains current until generation invalidation or chain inactivity.

- **FR-234** (MUST): Merge review, revision, approval, and no-skip law. `review request` packages the exact current three-dot diff, full candidate HEAD, generation digest, committed policy focus/profile, and target; it excludes handoffs, claimed results, and earlier verdicts. For merge review, FR-216's `candidate:` value is the full current candidate HEAD and `package:` is the exact issued package digest; `review attach` applies FR-216's shipped transport grammar to a verdict in a regular owner-controlled file no larger than 65,536 bytes. `review disposition` is instead the exact flag-only FR-232 command, validates the named finding/severity against the attached verdict, and stores its nonempty resolution. An above-MINOR disposition remains in its source `reviewing` or `revising` state until the operator runs the same exact `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>` surface; that record is explicitly `purpose: finding-disposition`, binds the finding number/resolution/HEAD/generation, and cannot satisfy later Gate-4 approval. Review BLOCK enters `revising`, fixes require `merge refresh` plus affected re-verification, and iteration 8 records residual risk and stops; at the cap `merge refresh` cannot reopen the loop. Every HEAD rewrite, changed diff, changed policy, or conflict resolution requires fresh Gate 3 and Gate 4; control requires renewed approval naming the integrated full HEAD. The sole retention exception is DM-014's immutable remote-only carry-forward record under FR-235. Gate 4 records `{candidate_head, generation_digest, diff_summary, control_paths, review_verdict, recorded_at}`; for control, `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha>` is operator-bound, valid only for the exact current tuple, and stores chain ID plus generation digest with `purpose: gate-4`. The PreToolUse hook denies model-issued merge approval with exactly `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (merge approve)`. No skip covers merge review, Gate 4, or approval; no approval TTL is imported from the commit chain.

Revision-10 clarification to **FR-234**: the disposition-purpose `merge approve` is unambiguous only because FR-216 permits one outstanding above-MINOR object. It co-signs and clears that sole object atomically. A persisted set with more than one outstanding object is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`; a second command can never create that state. The complete master-package and single-reviewer rules in FR-216 apply to every oversized merge package, including the exact whole three-dot diff.

- **FR-235** (MUST): Bounded locked finalize and re-verification. This is a substantive amendment to FR-062/FR-063 for CLI merge chains, not a phase-1.1 editorial clarification. Until phase-3 implementation lands, `/forge:worktree-merge` retains FR-062/FR-063's continuously held lock contract unchanged. Revision 13 supersedes that retention: the FR-062 portable-arbiter cutover amendment moves `/forge:worktree-merge` onto `common-lock hold` ahead of the remaining phase-3 surfaces. Before any phase-3 CLI surface is enabled, that implementation MUST update every sanctioned common-lock entrant — `/forge:worktree-merge`, CLI merge/cleanup/abort, standalone push, recovery, and the phase-5 scanner — to acquire the same mandatory Git-common-dir portable arbiter. No process ever selects an independent backend by host capability.

The mandatory arbiter is the pair `agent-rebase.lock.intent` / `agent-rebase.lockdir` on macOS and Linux. Its crash-safe acquisition is the following exact protocol rather than an unowned bare `mkdir`: create and fsync a mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file containing one DM-013-canonical record with exactly `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `host`, `pid`, `nonce`, `operation`, and `started_at`; `schema` is `forge-rebase-lock/1`, `owner_kind` is `merge`, `push`, or `phase5`, `chain_id` is the selected chain ID for merge and null otherwise, `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 operating-system-RNG bits, and `operation` is exactly `start`, `refresh`, `finalize`, `recover`, `cleanup`, `abort`, `push`, or `phase5-scan`. Publish that complete inode by a no-replace hard link at `agent-rebase.lock.intent`, fsync the common directory, unlink the temporary name, create mode-0700 `agent-rebase.lockdir`, hard-link the same inode at `agent-rebase.lockdir/owner.json`, and fsync both directories. Only that matching intent owner may create/materialize the directory, and portable acquisition succeeds only when intent and owner names are nonsymlink regular files with the recorded identical inode and digest.

Where `flock` is available, the portable-arbiter owner then opens the owner-controlled regular file `agent-rebase.lock`, polls for an exclusive kernel lock within the same single 300-second deadline, and after acquisition rewrites/fsyncs that file with the same canonical owner record; the complete common lock requires both layers. Where `flock` is unavailable, the portable arbiter alone is complete. The order is always portable arbiter then optional `flock`, and release is optional `flock` then portable arbiter; no entrant holds `flock` while waiting for the portable namespace or ever treats `flock` alone as complete. Thus a Linux entrant and macOS entrant contend on the same no-replace inode namespace, while `flock` remains a secondary compatibility layer rather than a separate lock backend. A non-crash failure before complete acquisition releases only optional kernel ownership and portable names proven to be that invocation's inode, fsyncs each affected directory, and retries within the one deadline; an unmatched name is never removed.

After every fence/result is resolved, complete-lock release explicitly unlocks/closes the optional `flock` layer while retaining the portable owner; unlock failure retains that owner and records `lock-release-failed`. Portable release then unlinks the inner owner, fsyncs and removes the lock directory, fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the outer intent, and fsyncs the common directory again. The parent fsync between `rmdir` and outer-intent unlink is mandatory: a crash cannot durably remove the identifying intent while leaving an unattributable empty directory. A crash at any portable acquisition or release boundary therefore leaves either no published owner or the complete outer-intent inode that identifies the attempt. The only automatically recoverable published topologies are: (a) outer intent plus lockdir/inner owner all strict-valid and the two names share that inode/digest; (b) a strict-valid outer intent with the lockdir absent; or (c) a strict-valid outer intent plus an owner-controlled nonsymlink mode-0700 lockdir containing no entries. Topologies (b) and (c) are the exact before-mkdir/owner-link acquisition or after-inner-unlink/rmdir release windows; any other missing, extra, symlinked, mismatched, or nonempty topology is unprovable. Reclaim is allowed only when the outer record is strict-valid, its host is local, its PID is proven dead, and any FR-236 in-flight record is absent or separately proven dead. A contender never runs release directly: it MUST first win the recovery reservation below in `fallback-owner` or `fallback-owner-and-fence` mode. A live, foreign-host, PID-reused, malformed, mismatched, or otherwise unprovable owner is never stolen.

All stale-portable-owner and dead-fence recovery uses the separate immutable reservation `<git-common-dir>/agent-rebase.recover`: a proven-dead portable owner with no fence, a proven-dead portable owner plus dead-but-published FR-236 fence, and a current mandatory-portable owner that also holds exclusive Linux `flock` but finds a legacy/anomalous dead fence preventing complete admission. After read-only proof of the applicable same-host PID/PGID absence, a contender creates a complete mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file containing one DM-013-canonical record with exactly `schema`, `recovery_kind`, `host`, `pid`, `nonce`, `started_at`, `stale_owner_inode`, `stale_owner_digest`, `stale_owner_host`, `stale_owner_pid`, `stale_owner_kind`, `stale_owner_chain_id`, `inflight_inode`, `inflight_digest`, `inflight_host`, `inflight_pgid`, `inflight_owner_kind`, `inflight_chain_id`, `owner_dead_at`, and `group_dead_at`. It writes and fsyncs the file, publishes its inode by a no-replace hard link at the reservation path, fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the temporary name, and fsyncs again. `schema` is `forge-rebase-recovery/1`, `recovery_kind` is exactly `fallback-owner`, `fallback-owner-and-fence`, or `flock-held-dead-fence` (the labels remain schema literals although the portable owner is now universal), and the nonce has the same 128-bit grammar. In either `fallback-*` mode `stale_owner_inode`, digest, host, PID, kind, and `owner_dead_at` are non-null; `stale_owner_chain_id` is non-null exactly when that kind is `merge` and is null for `push` or `phase5`. In `flock-held-dead-fence` mode every stale-owner field plus `owner_dead_at` is null because the contender owns the current portable and kernel layers. Every in-flight field plus `group_dead_at` is null in `fallback-owner` mode. In either fence mode `inflight_inode`, digest, host, PGID, kind, and `group_dead_at` are non-null; `inflight_chain_id` is non-null exactly for kind `merge` and null for kind `push`.

The no-replace winner is the sole provisional common-lock recovery owner and every ordinary entrant treats the reservation as unavailable. It may acquire the named chain lease or exclusively re-read/CAS the push singleton, persist every recorded owner/fence identity and death proof in a merge `condition_recorded` event or DM-017 `process` when a named transaction record exists, and clear only pathnames whose inode/digest it revalidates immediately before unlink. In either fallback mode it first revalidates the outer intent against the reservation's stale inode/digest. For topology (b) it creates the mode-0700 lockdir; for (c) it revalidates that exact empty directory; it then hard-links the validated outer inode at the inner owner name and fsyncs both directories. Topology (a) is already complete. Only after the complete matching pair is revalidated does it run the ordinary release sequence, checking the recorded inode/digest immediately before every unlink; any mismatch or failed completion abandons automatic removal. It then releases the chain lease before waiting for another lock form. Either fallback-mode winner competes through ordinary fallback acquisition next; a flock-held winner retains the physical lock it already owns. Once the physical lock is held and any recorded fence is absent with proofs durable, the winner immediately verifies that the canonical reservation still names its own inode/digest, unlinks it, fsyncs the common directory, and only then reacquires the chain lease/re-reads state for ordinary observation recovery. It never holds the chain lease while acquiring the physical lock.

If a reservation claimant crashes, automation never unlinks, replaces, or auto-reclaims the canonical reservation, even after proving its PID dead: portable pathname APIs provide no atomic compare-and-unlink, so recursive automatic takeover would admit an ABA race. Every contender preserves it, exhausts the shared deadline, and returns `rebase-lock-unavailable` with reservation path/inode/digest/owner plus every recorded stale-owner/fence identity. Quarantine is an operator-reserved recovery only after the operator proves repository-wide process quiescence and the claimant/recorded owner/recorded PGID deaths, engages `AGENT_HALT`, and reconciles every named chain/singleton with Git and remote observations. Under that halt the operator constructs DM-017's exact `recovery-reservation` manifest entry for the canonical reservation as its sole artifact, revalidates the reservation inode/content and absent destination immediately before moving exactly that inode to same-directory `agent-rebase.recover.quarantine-<manifest-digest>`, and fsyncs the common directory; a mismatch aborts without removal. The operator then re-proves quiescence, deaths, and state reconciliation, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may a new contender publish a fresh no-replace reservation. Before any new side effect, the first later complete-lock holder appends that entry to the affected singleton's DM-017 quarantine history or to a merge `condition_recorded` payload with the same exact ordered-history shape, then performs operation-specific observation; a later owner/fence quarantine appends rather than replaces it. The CLI never performs or recommends an unconditional move. Thus concurrent automated successors cannot ABA-remove one another's reservation and at most one provisional recovery writer exists. Linux dead-fence recovery and every fallback recovery require the next ordinary holder to observation-reconcile any named merge chain or push singleton before a new side effect; recovery never treats operation outcome as known. The single 300-second budget covers physical acquisition, reservation publication, stale-owner proof, the in-flight check when present, and FR-236's exact pre-intent admission recheck; timeout, an unprovable owner, a stale reservation, or absence of either portable mechanism returns `rebase-lock-unavailable` with path/inode/digest/owner evidence. Commands needing both locks otherwise acquire the complete common rebase lock before the per-chain lease and re-read state after both; this reservation is the sole bounded exception and counts as the common-lock predecessor only for proof persistence and stale-name clearing, never for a new Git/remote side effect. One lock epoch performs at most one integration-target fetch that selects `fetched_tip`, one rebase/integration attempt, one matrix-selected mechanical suite, and one push attempt. In addition it may perform at most one separately fenced pre-attempt authoritative observation — ordinary final-pre-push observation or recovery reconciliation of a prior intent — and, if it makes its sole push, one separately fenced post-attempt outcome observation. An old-tip reconciliation in a no-push-yet recovery epoch doubles as the immediate pre-push check; it is never followed by a redundant third observation before push. Either bounded observation may fetch the exact observed ref object needed for identity/ancestry proof but cannot start another integration, suite, or push. A present movement found by the pre-attempt observation may materialize only the corresponding remote-only successor generation from that observation. The epoch never loops indefinitely or holds the lock across external review/approval.

A live, foreign-host, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or unprovable portable owner/topology is permanently unavailable to automation; the CLI neither steals it nor prints an immediately executable removal. Revision 7 admits only this operator-reserved recovery: the operator proves repository-wide and all-relevant-host quiescence/death of every possible owner, engages `AGENT_HALT`, proves no recovery reservation exists, and reconciles every nonterminal merge chain and push singleton against Git and the remote. The operator records and then immediately revalidates one DM-017 `portable-owner` manifest entry whose source-sorted artifacts are the extant canonical outer intent and lockdir top-level nodes without following a symlink; the lockdir directory digest binds its inner owner and every other entry. Still under the halt, each exact artifact, lockdir first and outer intent second, is renamed within the common directory to its distinct nonexisting `<source-basename>.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` destination and the common directory is fsynced after each rename; any identity, content, entry-set, or target-existence mismatch stops without another move. This is quarantine, never deletion. The operator then re-proves quiescence and reconciles every affected transaction and lock-release fact, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may an ordinary entrant acquire fresh names. Before a new side effect, that first complete-lock holder appends the one manifest entry to the exact ordered DM-017 `quarantine` history in every affected push singleton or persists a merge `condition_recorded` payload with exactly `condition`, `quarantine`, and `observation_digest`, where `quarantine` has that same ordered-history shape; it then performs the operation-specific observation. Earlier reservation/owner/fence entries are never overwritten. A topology that cannot be completely manifested/revalidated remains permanently blocked in Revision 7.

If rebase changes the candidate and external review/approval is required, the epoch runs integrated Gate 1, scoped mutation, and Gate 2; persists the integrated generation; parks; and releases the lock. Gate 3, Gate 4, and approval occur outside the lock. A later finalize reacquires, proves tuple identity and the expected remote tip, and reruns Gate 1, scoped mutation, and Gate 2 in that final epoch before push. Immediately before push it performs a fresh authoritative remote-tip read. A change detected by that read parks without pushing. A present-tip movement after the successful read is left to ordinary non-fast-forward rejection; a deletion in that unavoidable final window can instead let the ordinary non-force refspec recreate the destination, and that exact limitation is classified only from push result plus post-attempt observation under FR-236. Rows are applied from most specific to least specific — conflict resolution, then policy/diff/HEAD change, then remote-only movement, then the unchanged row — using this normative matrix:

| Observation under lock | Required before push |
|---|---|
| Bound candidate HEAD changed | Invalidate hash-bound evidence and return `candidate-stale`; reclassify only through `merge refresh` |
| Recorded pre-push worktree path is absent after start | Persist `foreign-git-state` and return exact `state-precondition`; neither start-only `worktree-missing` nor existing-path `worktree-invalid` is broadened |
| Existing worktree identity becomes invalid, or checkout/remote/branch/destination is no longer the fixed target | Return the byte-exact `worktree-invalid` or `push-target-invalid` condition respectively; never silently retarget |
| Final intended HEAD's committed DM-015 mode is duplicate, malformed, or unknown | First persist the completed-result/invalid-mode classification and enter `revising` as an admitted internal transition without an envelope; create no push intent. The attempted push continuation from that parked tuple is not admitted, changes no further state, emits the exact invalid-mode diagnostic, and returns byte-exact `state-precondition`; approved repair plus refresh is required |
| Remote unchanged; HEAD/diff/policy unchanged; no earlier park | Recheck authorization and push; current pure-fast-forward treatment retains initial gates |
| Remote advanced but final HEAD, diff, and policy remain identical | Reload/reclassify; rerun Gate 1, mutation, and Gate 2; retain Gate 3/Gate 4/approval only through DM-014's explicit carry-forward record |
| Candidate HEAD rewritten, even with identical diff | Rerun Gate 1/mutation/Gate 2, then fresh Gate 3 and Gate 4; renew control approval |
| Diff digest changed | Reclassify and repeat Gates 1–4; renew control approval |
| Policy digest changed | Reclassify and repeat Gates 1–4 because commands and review context changed |
| Conflicts were resolved | Repeat Gates 1–4 unconditionally; approval names integrated HEAD |
| Any in-lock gate is non-PASS and the tuple is unchanged | Remote untouched; enter `reverification_failed` |
| Any in-lock gate or scoped mutation changes HEAD, diff, or policy | Remote untouched; persist the new generation, release the lock, enter `classifying → verifying`, and then apply the specific HEAD/diff/policy row before judgment |
| Tuple changes after re-verification | Refuse before push and park through the applicable row |
| Final authoritative remote-tip resolution fails before push intent | Persist `fetch-failed`, return to `authorized`, release the lock, and perform no push; the next epoch reruns the final mechanical checks |
| Final authoritative query succeeds but proves the destination ref absent | Persist `remote-moved` on the current generation, increment the remote-only counter, release the lock, and perform no push or ref creation; no successor generation exists until a later query yields a full tip |
| Remote changes during the final pre-push window | A detected pre-invocation change or absence parks; a later present-tip race is ordinary non-fast-forward, while a deletion after the read may let the ordinary non-force refspec recreate the destination; persist push intent/result and classify only by post-attempt observation |
| Push succeeds | Record and fsync `pushed` before attempting cleanup |

The consecutive-remote-movement counter is persisted in `integration`, increments only when remote movement or authoritative destination absence alone defeats an epoch, resets after an epoch ends for any other reason or after push, and reaches `remote-churn` on the eighth such defeat. After push intent, “alone” requires either the retained actual non-fast-forward rejection, a successful push result followed by an authoritative later movement/absence, or start-pipe proof that no push executed; a known independent failure, unknown outcome, or ambiguous missing result never increments the counter. Defeats one through seven park in `authorized`; the eighth parks in `awaiting_approval`, and FR-232 defines both transitions and the sole resume verbs. Another epoch then requires the operator-bound acknowledgement in FR-232. Liveness is claimed only under eventual remote quiescence. Explicit lock release occurs after the primary state/outcome is durable; failure preserves that primary truth, records `lock-release-failed`, and admits only status/recovery until release can be proved.

Revision-12 reservation-held fence-clearing amendment to **FR-235** and **FR-236**: Superseding only any earlier recovery reading that permits an ordinary common-lock contender to act on a surviving physical fence, FR-235's preliminary read-only identity/death proof may be used solely to construct the immutable recovery reservation and authorizes neither lifecycle classification nor clearing; after reservation publication, a surviving physical fence may be inspected, classified, or cleared only while holding that reservation, and common-lock acquisition MUST NOT clear it before reservation-held lifecycle recovery has classified it.

- **FR-236** (MUST): Rebase, push, cleanup, and crash recovery. Before fetch/rebase, conflict continue/abort, push, worktree removal, or branch deletion, the CLI MUST persist and fsync the applicable merge event or standalone singleton intent before the side effect and MUST forbid it if intent persistence fails. Before a merge chain has generation 1, that merge event is exactly DM-014's `fetch_intent` with `generation_digest: null`; its event digest is the in-flight fence's `intent_digest`, and only a successful `fetch_result` may bind the first non-null generation. Every later merge side-effect intent is generation-bound normally. Recovery observes actual Git metadata, HEAD, the worktree/index, and a freshly fetched/queried remote; event replay or a stale tracking ref alone never proves an external side effect.

The sole pre-side-effect-intent subprocess exception is the repeated admission recheck and deterministic reflog-path resolution after physical common-lock ownership but before the corresponding merge chain, push singleton, or DM-017 rebase intent can legally contain that result. Its closed direct-argv set is `git worktree list --porcelain`, `git rev-parse --git-dir --git-common-dir --show-toplevel HEAD`, `git rev-parse --git-path logs/HEAD`, `git rev-parse --git-path logs/<fixed-full-branch-ref>`, `git symbolic-ref -q HEAD`, `git cat-file blob <fixed-commit>:<fixed-manifest-or-policy-path>`, and `git -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.untrackedCache=false --no-optional-locks status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all --ignore-submodules=none`, with fixed `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`. The implementation MUST first qualify that its Git honors `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`; otherwise this mechanism is unavailable and fails closed. These commands read only object/ref/worktree/reflog-path identity, disable optional index refresh, configured fsmonitor/untracked-cache helpers, and promisor-object lazy fetch, invoke no hook, filter, pager, textconv, credential, network, or user shell, and cannot mutate Git or repository bytes. Reflog bytes are then `lstat`-validated and hashed in-process, not through another command. Every exception result is revalidated under the physical lock immediately before its first durable state use. The commands still use a direct argv, isolated process group, 65,536-byte combined cap, TERM/0.25-second/KILL escalation, and the one remaining 300-second common-lock acquisition deadline. A syntactically complete observation maps to the ordinary dirty/identity/policy or reflog-baseline result; launch, cap, timeout, unsupported no-lazy-fetch semantics, or unproved completion is retried only within that deadline and then returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable` because the complete common lock was never admitted. No state or history side effect follows such failure. An unkillable member of this exact no-write set cannot race a later mutation, and this exception MUST NOT admit any other Git verb, option, config key, helper, or subprocess.

Every other subprocess executed while the common lock is held — matrix gates, fetch/tip resolution, authoritative remote or attribution observation, rebase/continue/abort, push, containment checks, worktree removal, and branch deletion — MUST have a durable intent, use FR-236's in-flight fence, and run as a direct argv in an isolated process group with a 65,536-byte combined stdout/stderr cap. Git/remote/cleanup operations have a fixed 1200-second timeout; matrix gates retain FR-233's configured narrower timeout under the same 1200-second outer maximum. Timeout or output-cap breach sends SIGTERM to the group, waits 0.25 seconds, then sends SIGKILL; launch failure follows the same fail-closed outcome, and an unkillable survivor or unprovable group exit sets merge `foreign-git-state` or standalone-push `recovery_required` before any later side effect. After any termination, observation precedes retry. Matrix-gate failures use `merge-gate-failed`; fetch/tip failures use `fetch-failed`; an exact owned recoverable conflict has precedence and uses `rebase-conflict`, while every rebase/continue/abort failure outside that predicate uses `rebase-failed`; a timed-out or output-capped push remains outcome-unknown until observation and uses `push-outcome-unknown`; attribution-observation failure preserves its observation-only wrapper, after which the same invocation's attempted classification continuation has no admitting row and returns exact `state-precondition`; and containment/removal/deletion failures use `cleanup-failed`. Bounded output is preserved in evidence with its digest and launch/timeout/output-limit flags.

Revision-10 post-fetch scope amendment to **FR-236**: FR-231's one exact run-bound scope derivation is an additional closed no-side-effect subprocess exception and is expressly excluded from the preceding “every other subprocess” rule. After the single fenced bootstrap child returns success and group death is proved, the complete common lock and chain lease remain held and the exact original fence remains present. The parent derives FR-231's canonical and temporary paths and requires both absent, creates the temporary path as a mode-0600 `O_EXCL`/no-follow regular file, obtains its descriptor identity, constructs the binding as DM-013 canonical bytes with no trailing LF, writes and fsyncs it, and revalidates the fence path/device/inode/bytes. It publishes the temporary inode at the canonical path by no-replace hard link, fsyncs the directory, revalidates both names as the sidecar-recorded same inode/content with link count two, unlinks only the recorded temporary name after another immediate no-follow same-inode/content check, fsyncs the directory, then reopens the canonical name under a 16,384-byte cap and requires the recorded inode/content with link count one and the temporary name absent. Exact mode, bytes, self-digest, publication, intent, scope-request, fence, template, environment, candidate, resolved-command, and child-result bindings are mandatory. Only that strict-valid final file authorizes the unfenced scope start.

Recovery classifies the two deterministic names under both locks before scope: both absent is the expected pre-publication window and takes the null-binding ordinary-abort path; canonical absent plus a strict-valid one-link recorded temporary inode resumes only the no-replace link/fsync/revalidate/unlink/fsync sequence; both names present as the strict-valid recorded same inode/content with link count two resumes only temporary-name unlink/fsync and final validation; canonical present as the strict-valid recorded one-link inode with temporary absent is complete. A crash after unlink but before directory fsync resolves to one of the latter two states and is idempotent. Every other topology, link count, identity, path, mode, content, missing retained fence before `fetch_result`, wrong intent, or conflict is exact `frozen-chain`; no unrelated name or inode is removed. A completed canonical sidecar is reusable only when byte-identical.

Before admitting this exception, the implementation MUST qualify that its Git honors `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`; otherwise scope derivation fails closed. The exact tree-versus-tree argv reads only the binding's two fixed object IDs, disables replacements, optional locks, lazy fetch, external diff, textconv, paging, system/global config, and inherited repository/config injection, and invokes no hook, filter helper, credential helper, network, user shell, index refresh, ref update, worktree write, or object write. It retains the exact isolated process group, cap, timeout, and TERM/0.25-second/KILL escalation. Launch failure, nonzero exit, cap, timeout, malformed bytes, failed no-lazy-fetch qualification, or unproved scope-group exit creates no proof: the CLI appends and fsyncs the original intent's failed null-generation `fetch_result` with the complete non-null `scope_fetch_binding` and `scope_proof: null`, then removes the original fetch fence, performs the ordinary acquired-ownership abort release, and returns exit 1 `run-task-binding-invalid`. Failure to publish any canonical sidecar before scope starts instead records both result members null and takes that same abort path; a valid but divergent sidecar takes `frozen-chain`. This exception takes precedence over §9's general locked-child outcome row and never produces `fetch-failed`, `foreign-git-state`, or `run-scope-exceeded`.

A parent crash after valid sidecar publication but before `fetch_result` leaves that immutable binding and the original fetch fence. Recovery accepts only the authenticated intent plus exact surviving fence plus a binding completed by the four-state classifier, reconstructs and verifies the resolved argv from those bytes, discards every partial or orphaned scope-output byte, and reruns only that fixed-object scope derivation—never fetch, tip resolution, a tracking-ref read, or a remote query. A crash before publication cannot authorize scope; after proving the fetch group dead, recovery records no invented tip and takes the null-binding `run-task-binding-invalid` ordinary-abort path. An unproved orphan scope group cannot mutate Git, repository, worktree, or remote state under the qualified closed argv/environment; its bytes are never trusted. The successful or failed `fetch_result` copies the complete binding and, when successful, the complete proof before the original fence is removed. Thereafter the event copy is authoritative and replay never reruns scope. The canonical sidecar remains immutable chain/archive evidence and automation never overwrites or unlinks it; the deterministic temporary name may be linked or removed only in the exact recorded-inode recovery states above.

Revision-12 composite-bootstrap amendment to **FR-231** and **FR-236**: This supersedes only Revision-10's post-fetch unfenced scope-child sequencing; its scope-only crash rerun; the fetch-only meaning of the sidecar's `child_result`; the `forge-run-scope-fetch-binding/1` schema/member-count arithmetic; the run-bound-only trigger in `The successful run-bound fetch child publishes exactly .forge/chains/<chain-id>/scope-fetch-<fetch-intent-digest>-<inflight-digest>.json before scope execution`; the sentence `A failed scope-command result has that same non-null binding and a null proof`; and the clause `a fetch or binding-publication failure before a valid sidecar, and every unbound result, has both members null` insofar as it covers every unbound result. The pinned name-status argv/environment/parser, two-name no-replace publication topology, scope proof, failure/abort reason and terminal outcomes, and archive law remain unchanged except as explicitly rebound below. A run-bound null-generation bootstrap publishes one fence and creates one isolated process group whose single composite child performs, in order and under that fence, the epoch's sole integration-target fetch, the existing pinned read-only name-status subprocess over the resolved fixed range, and one read-only full-patch subprocess over the identical `<full-remote-tip>...<full-candidate-head>` range; only PASS results from all three constituents may produce the complete sidecar and successful `fetch_result`. An unbound null-generation bootstrap runs the same fenced composite child in the same isolated process group, but performs exactly the epoch's sole integration-target fetch followed by the same full-patch subprocess and launches no name-status or other scope constituent; only PASS results from both constituents may produce the same `/2` sidecar and a successful `fetch_result`. The full-patch subprocess's exact direct argv is `git --no-pager --no-replace-objects -c core.quotePath=false -c color.ui=false -c diff.renames=copies -c diff.renameLimit=0 -c diff.algorithm=myers -C <canonical-worktree> diff --patch --no-color -O/dev/null --find-renames=50% --find-copies=50% --find-copies-harder -l0 --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --ignore-submodules=none --diff-filter=ACDMRTUXB <full-remote-tip>...<full-candidate-head> --`. In either mode it uses byte-for-byte the same inherited-variable unset set and exact environment overlay, direct-argv discipline, isolated-process-group discipline, 1200-second fail-closed timeout, TERM/0.25-second/KILL termination escalation, and complete-result proof as FR-231's pinned name-status subprocess; ONLY the output-cap leg is replaced by this digest-streaming law: the full-patch constituent's stdout is streamed unbounded into the SHA-256 digest computation, never buffered whole, stored, parsed, or echoed. No transcript of the patch bytes is retained anywhere; `full_patch_output_digest` is their only residue. Any non-PASS constituent makes the composite child non-PASS.

Every Revision-12 scope-fetch sidecar uses schema `forge-run-scope-fetch-binding/2` and has exactly sixteen members: `schema`, `chain_id`, `fetch_intent_digest`, `scope_request_digest`, `candidate_head`, `remote_tip`, `command_template_digest`, `command_digest`, `full_patch_command_digest`, `full_patch_output_digest`, `environment_digest`, `publication`, `retained_inflight`, `child_result`, `recorded_at`, and `digest`; `digest` hashes the DM-013 canonical mapping of the preceding fifteen members, so the superseded Revision-10 arithmetic is exactly fourteen to sixteen members and thirteen to fifteen digest-input members. An unbound `/2` sidecar sets exactly the three scope-specific members `scope_request_digest`, `command_template_digest`, and `command_digest` to null and populates every other member identically; the sixteen-member count and digest over all fifteen preceding members, including those three nulls, are unchanged. `full_patch_command_digest` hashes the exact full-patch argv above, and in both run-bound and unbound sidecars `full_patch_output_digest` hashes the complete streamed stdout bytes and MUST equal the materialized DM-014 `diff_sha256`. For schema `/2`, `child_result` is the result envelope for the whole composite child rather than a fetch-only child, and `resolved_tip` records the successful fetch result. For a run-bound `/2` sidecar, its existing `output_digest` is the SHA-256 of the exact name-status stdout bytes and MUST equal `scope_proof.output_digest`. For an unbound `/2` sidecar, which launches no name-status constituent, the populated `child_result.output_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the exact zero-byte name-status-output slot; it is distinct from `full_patch_output_digest`, and no scope output is invented.

Under schema `/2`, Revision-10's sentence `A failed scope-command result has that same non-null binding and a null proof` is rebound solely to the run-bound post-sidecar-crash recovery leg: because a complete sidecar exists only after all three constituents PASS, a parent crash after its publication but before the authenticated successful `fetch_result` copies both the binding and non-null proof cannot be called a scope-command failure. Recovery copies that binding into a failed null-generation `fetch_result` with `scope_proof: null`, takes the existing `run-task-binding-invalid` ordinary-abort path, and reruns none of the three constituents. A successful unbound `fetch_result`, including one recovered after a complete valid sidecar survives a parent crash, instead copies the non-null binding with `scope_proof: null`, binds the complete generation, enters the existing `verifying` successor, and reruns neither constituent. Failure before a valid sidecar retains both scope members null and the existing mode-specific outcome: `run-task-binding-invalid` ordinary abort when run-bound, or `fetch-failed` with `classifying` retained when unbound. The one-fetch epoch budget and single-fence uniqueness law are UNCHANGED: no second fence or bootstrap child is admitted, and a dead composite child is exactly a dead fetch child in every crash topology.

Revision-10 fenced-gate amendment to **FR-236**: the durable intent for operation `gate` is the current sealed DM-014 gate-plan position. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-merge-gate-intent/1", chain_id, epoch_intent_digest, seal_event_digest, generation_digest, policy_digest, suite_digest, cursor, kind, id, authorizing_event_digest}`. `epoch_intent_digest` is the current `integration.epoch.intent_digest`; the next five identity members are the exact current sealed-plan values; `kind` and `id` are its cursor-selected suite member; and `authorizing_event_digest` equals `seal_event_digest` at cursor zero or the immediately preceding `gate_recorded` event digest that durably advanced to a later cursor. If a retry `epoch_intent` directly sealed the plan, the epoch and seal digests are equal. Those values MUST be re-derived from the authenticated event tail and materialized state before fence publication and again before the start byte. A `gate_recorded` result copies that intent digest and fence digest, carries DM-014's exact position members, and advances the cursor as DM-014 requires.

If the parent crashes after a gate start byte and before `gate_recorded`, the surviving fence plus unchanged sealed cursor identify the exact suite position; a live/unprovable child blocks all later positions, and proven death permits only result recovery or a rerun of that same position under a fresh fence after tuple observation. Recovery never advances from absence of a result. An unsealed or empty-complete plan cannot authorize a gate. A mismatched seal, plan, authorizing event, generation, policy, cursor, operation, intent digest, or result is exact `frozen-chain`, not an unattributed retry or generic gate failure; an authenticated matrix successor or gate-caused tuple change instead takes DM-014's ordinary seal or park law.

The `remote-observation` operation has two exact intent phases so recovery never conflates a failed final read with an unknown push outcome. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-remote-observation-intent/1", transaction, chain_id, attempt_identity, phase, push_intent_digest}`. `transaction` is `merge` or `push`; `chain_id` is the selected merge chain or null for standalone; and `phase` is exactly `final-prepush` or `post-push`. Merge `attempt_identity` is the current `epoch_intent` event digest. Standalone `attempt_identity` is the active recovery `attempt_nonce` for `final-prepush` and the current last `push_intent.attempts` member's nonce for `post-push`; thus a later push-only retry is distinct even though it reuses an earlier integration's immutable rebase record. For merge, `push_intent_digest` is null in `final-prepush` and is the current `push_intent` event digest in `post-push`. For standalone, it is null in `final-prepush` and in `post-push` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{attempt_number, attempt_nonce, expected_old_tip, intended_head, destination_ref, intended_at}`, using the current one-based `push_intent.attempts` index and its immutable intent fields. Historical push intents may remain present during a later `final-prepush` phase; null means only that this exact active ledger identity has no push intent yet. The persisted chain/singleton tuple supplies every mapping member before the fence is published. A missing, wrong-phase, wrong-attempt, or wrong-push-intent binding is event/state divergence and returns exact `frozen-chain`. Death/quarantine recovery of `final-prepush` re-enters only the ordinary final-tip observation rows and uses `fetch-failed` if authoritative truth remains unavailable; recovery of `post-push` uses only the push-outcome observation rows and `push-outcome-unknown` when truth remains unavailable.

`attribution-observation` is a standalone-only, read-only operation admitted only by strict-valid `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase` with a non-null DM-017 `process.attribution`. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-attribution-observation-intent/1", repository, destination_ref, attempt_nonce, attribution}`, where repository and destination ref are the current singleton values, `attempt_nonce` equals both the current active nonce and the attribution object's originating attempt nonce, and `attribution` is its exact six-key object byte-for-byte. Its direct argv may perform only FR-238's Git/reflog identity and predicate reads; it cannot rebase, continue, abort, create a push intent, or push. A missing/mismatched active nonce, changed attribution, wrong digest, or use with merge ownership is exact event/state divergence and `frozen-chain`. Its durable result is appended normally but never replaces the original attribution. If its group becomes foreign/unprovable, `foreign-process-state` retains both the original attribution and this observation fence identity; after exact death or quarantine recovery, the transaction restores the same attribution wrapper without classifying or replaying the original rebase/abort, and a later invocation may repeat only the read-only observation. A failed or completed-but-unmatched observation leaves the wrapper unchanged; the same invocation's attempted classification continuation is then unadmitted and returns its existing observation-only `state-precondition`.

The common rebase lock includes a repository-wide subprocess fence at `<git-common-dir>/agent-rebase.inflight`; possession of the mandatory portable arbiter and optional secondary `flock` without resolving that path and any recovery reservation is not possession of the complete common lock. After the operation intent is durable and before any child can execute, the parent forks a child that establishes its isolated process group, acknowledges that PID/PGID to the parent, and blocks on an owner-controlled start pipe; pipe EOF before an explicit start byte makes the child exit without `exec`. The parent writes and fsyncs a complete mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file and publishes it by no-replace hard link at the fence path. Its DM-013-canonical record has exactly `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `operation`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `started_at`, `intent_digest`, and `nonce`; `schema` is `forge-rebase-inflight/1`, `owner_kind` is `merge` or `push`, `chain_id` is the selected chain or null, `operation` is exactly `gate`, `fetch`, `tip-resolution`, `remote-observation`, `attribution-observation`, `rebase`, `continue`, `abort`, `push`, `containment`, `worktree-remove`, or `branch-delete`, `intent_digest` is the SHA-256 of the durable event/singleton intent authorizing that child, and `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 operating-system-RNG bits. `attribution-observation` requires `owner_kind: push` and null `chain_id`; any other owner pairing is invalid. The parent fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the temporary name, fsyncs again, and only then sends the start byte. A publication attempt that does not complete closes the pipe so no command executes, removes only its own proven temporary/inode names, and retries acquisition/publication within the one remaining 300-second budget after revalidation; an existing or unresolved fence is never overwritten. Only deadline exhaustion or proof that no required portable no-replace-link/fsync mechanism exists returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable`; one failed publication attempt never emits that code immediately.

Revision-11 fresh-publication-budget amendment to **FR-236**: Each fenced operation receives one fresh bounded 300-second publication budget; a retry occurs only after the required lock and publication-state revalidation and consumes without resetting that same budget; deadline exhaustion still returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable`; the acquisition deadline never refuses a first publication attempt.

The fence remains while the process group may exist. On ordinary completion, the parent durably records the exit/result tied to the fence digest before it verifies and unlinks that inode/content and fsyncs the common directory; a standalone result is appended to DM-017 `process.results`, and any later observation subprocess receives a new fence and appends a distinct result rather than overwriting it. After a parent crash, a physical-lock holder or FR-235 provisional recovery owner validates the fence. When a same-host process group yields `ESRCH` from `kill(-pgid, 0)`, that exclusive owner first persists the exact group-death proof together with the fence's operation, intent digest, fence digest, host, and PGID in the selected chain's `condition_recorded` event or DM-017 `process`, then may unlink and fsync the fence; the transaction remains nonterminal, and actual Git/remote classification runs next under a newly published observation fence before any new mutating operation is admitted. For standalone rebase or abort, selection of `rebase-attribution-unresolved` atomically copies that persisted operation/intent/fence identity into DM-017 `process.attribution` before any later observation fence may replace the selected-fence fields; merge uses its immutable event payload equivalently. A crash between those steps is safe because group death and the complete operation identity are durable, the physical lock or recovery reservation excludes a concurrent sanctioned mutator, and the nonterminal chain/singleton still requires identity-bound observation. A live group, foreign host, reused/unprovable PGID, or malformed/mismatched fence is never cleared automatically and consumes the same 300-second common-lock budget before `rebase-lock-unavailable`. A timed-out child whose group is not proven gone therefore leaves both the chain/singleton fail-closed condition and this global fence, so no other merge chain or standalone push can mutate the repository concurrently with a survivor. Fence unlink/fsync failure after a primary result is durable is failure to release the complete common rebase lock and uses only `lock-release-failed`.

For a foreign-host, reused/unprovable-PGID, malformed, or mismatched fence, DM-017's `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence` and merge `foreign-git-state` name an operator-reserved path, not an automated transition. The operator MUST establish repository-wide and all-relevant-host quiescence/death, engage `AGENT_HALT`, reconcile every nonterminal chain/singleton plus Git and remote truth, and resolve or quarantine any FR-235 owner/reservation first. The operator then constructs one exact DM-017 `inflight-fence` manifest entry and immediately revalidates the fence's exact `lstat` identity and SHA-256 of its regular bytes or symlink target without following it; a directory or other malformed type additionally requires the same sorted nonrecursive entry manifest as FR-235. Under the halt, the exact unchanged fence is renamed within the common directory to the nonexisting `agent-rebase.inflight.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` destination and the common directory is fsynced; any identity/content/entry/target mismatch stops. The operator re-proves quiescence and reconciliation, retains the quarantine artifact and manifest, and explicitly clears the halt. The first subsequent complete-lock holder MUST acquire the selected chain lease or exclusively CAS the singleton, append that entry after every earlier recovery-stage entry in the exact quarantine history, persist the operation-specific Git/remote observation, and only then transition from `foreign-git-state` or `foreign-process-state`; it cannot launch a new side effect first.
