For a merge, the operator-quarantine `condition_recorded` payload has exactly `condition`, `quarantine`, and `observation_digest`, where condition is `foreign-git-state`, quarantine has DM-017's exact ordered-history shape, and observation digest is lowercase SHA-256 of the applicable canonical Git/remote observation; for standalone push it is stored in `recovery.process.quarantine`. If complete quiescence, manifest revalidation, or transaction reconciliation cannot be proved, the fence and `operator-quiescence` state are permanently blocked in Revision 7.

Each rebase intent records a unique 32-lowercase-hex `operation_nonce` drawn from 128 bits of the operating-system cryptographic RNG, pre-rebase HEAD, fetched tip, full branch ref, generation digest, and the exact action prefix `forge-merge-rebase:<chain-id>:<generation-digest>:<operation-nonce>`. The CLI sets that value as `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` for the rebase and every continuation. A crash-after-rebase integrated result is proven only when all of these hold: no durable result reports launch failure, non-conflict nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach; the recorded branch remains checked out in the recorded worktree; no Git operation metadata remains; status is exactly clean; the recorded fetched tip is an ancestor of observed HEAD; and the contiguous newest branch-reflog segment starts with old OID equal to recorded pre-rebase HEAD, ends with new OID equal to observed HEAD, contains no later entry, and every entry's action begins with that exact unguessable prefix. An exact owned conflict instead follows the conflict row despite Git's nonzero result. Any other failed leg sets `foreign-git-state`; no integrated generation is invented.

For `rebase_conflict`, `--continue` and `--abort-rebase` are mutually exclusive. Recovery MUST prove the rebase metadata belongs to the selected worktree, chain, nonce, and pre-rebase generation. The authorized set is the nonempty, duplicate-free repository-relative path set from exact NUL-delimited `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U -z --`; when each conflict is first observed, the CLI also records SHA-256 digests of exact NUL-delimited `git ls-files --stage -z` and `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all` records after excluding that authorized set. Every `--paths` value is normalized repository-relatively, rejects absolute/empty/`.`/`..` traversal, NUL, duplicate, and pathspec-magic input, and MUST equal the authorized set. Before staging, every non-conflict index/status record MUST remain byte-identical to its recorded baseline; this admits Git's legitimate rebase-staged state but rejects unrelated staged, tracked, or untracked contamination. The CLI stages only with `git --literal-pathspecs add -- <normalized-paths>` and continues noninteractively under the recorded action prefix using recorded commit messages. Further conflicts replace the authorized set and both baselines and remain `rebase_conflict`. If continuation completed before a crash, only the exact integrated-result predicate above advances to `reverifying`; otherwise `foreign-git-state` remains observation-only. `--abort-rebase` MUST prove restoration of the recorded pre-rebase HEAD, absence of Git operation metadata, exact clean state, and unchanged branch/worktree identity before entering `revising`. A Git state matching neither recorded pre-rebase, exact owned conflict, nor proven integrated state sets `foreign-git-state` and returns `state-precondition` for every mutating verb.

Revision-12 explicit-recover-flag amendment to **FR-232** and **FR-236**: Superseding only FR-232's `rebasing` rows insofar as they admit explicit `recover --abort-rebase` outside an exact owned conflict, an explicit `merge recover --continue` or `merge recover --abort-rebase` outside the selected chain's exact owned `rebase_conflict` tuple MUST return exit 1 `state-precondition`, emit a diagnostic naming the actual scalar state, append no event, make no state change, and MUST NOT be routed as bare `merge recover`; active-rebase `merge abort` retains its existing behavior, and explicit flags are never silently ignored.

Before each push, `pushing` records expected old remote tip, intended full HEAD, destination ref, and the append-only attempted-head history. It also proves the current intended HEAD descends from that expected old tip and invokes exactly the ordinary non-force refspec through direct argv `git push --porcelain origin <intended-head>:<destination-ref>` with `LC_ALL=C`: no leading `+`, `--force`, `--force-with-lease`, deletion refspec, or other force/CAS option is permitted, and `--porcelain` changes only stable result reporting. An actual non-fast-forward classification requires a completed nonzero child plus exactly one target status record whose tab-delimited flag is `!`, whose ref field's destination is the exact recorded ref, and whose summary is exactly either `[rejected] (non-fast-forward)` or `[rejected] (fetch first)`; these are Git's two stable ordinary stale-destination rejection summaries. Malformed, missing, duplicate-target, truncated, or every other rejection output never selects that classification. Launch/timeout/cap/survivor ambiguity remains outcome-unknown, while another completed nonzero result is known failure. An authoritative observation is a successful fresh remote ref query/fetch followed, when the ref exists, by ancestry checks for the current intended HEAD and every distinct historical attempted head; a successful query proving the ref absent is authoritative absence, not `fetch-failed` or unobservable. Absence observed before invocation always parks without a push. The ordinary protocol has one explicit limitation: deletion after the successful final read but before the server applies the refspec can make that same non-force invocation recreate the destination instead of rejecting non-fast-forward. Revision 7 permits only that final-window creation because preserving the adopted ordinary-fast-forward-only contract and also promising atomic expected-old no-create semantics would require a force/CAS option; result plus the mandatory post-attempt observation therefore decides truth without claiming that every deletion race rejects.

Containment of the current intended HEAD has precedence over every result, old-tip, movement, inactivity, retry, or abort classification only while that last attempted member is also the current merge generation's final intended candidate HEAD or standalone fresh attempt's locked `rebased_head`: recovery then sets `landed_head` to that member and transitions to `pushed`/`closed`, even if an earlier result was ambiguous or the remote later advanced. If that bound current head is uncontained but an older attempted head is contained, recovery records the latest-contained older member as partial `landed_head`, forbids abort and blind retry, and parks the active merge at `authorized/remote-moved` (or standalone at `recovery_required/remote-moved`) so the current checkout is integrated in a new bounded attempt; it never calls the newer unlanded generation pushed or closed. In that later attempt, the exact fresh-attempt identity — a post-park merge `epoch_intent` digest or standalone rebase nonce/intent digest retained under `remote-moved/start-new-attempt` — has not yet joined the attempted-head history. Prior containment remains evidence but cannot classify that identity, and the final authoritative observation either appends its intent on unchanged tip or takes the ordinary moved/unavailable pre-intent row. This rule applies even when no-op integration produces the same OID as the latest historical member; the new intent then appends one deliberate repeated OID and ends the exception. An inactive merge instead takes only FR-232's exact `historical-landed-superseded` terminal release: retain the clean newer worktree/branch, perform no cleanup/deletion, release ownership, and require a fresh chain. Only an all-false authoritative vector for the currently bound attempted head may proceed to ordinary old-tip, movement, rejection, or not-landed abort handling. If the present remote still equals the current expected old tip with that all-false vector, one ordinary retry is safe regardless of whether the retained current result is success, non-fast-forward, known failure, outcome-unknown, or absent after a crash-before-result; the exact result and all history are preserved. If start-pipe proof shows the complete-lock epoch authorized no push child for its current durable intent, that just-completed reconciliation observation is the immediate pre-push recheck and the existing unlaunched intent authorizes its sole attempt without appending a duplicate history member. Once a push child was authorized, even when no result survived, the epoch instead parks so a newly acquired epoch performs that recheck/retry and appends the deliberate duplicate; retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery. When the destination is authoritatively absent or its OID differs from expected old tip with all attempted heads uncontained, `non-fast-forward` is used only if the retained update result actually rejected that ordinary push as non-fast-forward; observation without such a rejection instead records condition `remote-moved`, returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and re-enters the integration matrix without claiming a rejected update. Only independently proven remote-only defeats increment the FR-235 counter; ambiguous or independently failed attempts reset it. If the destination cannot be observed authoritatively, the chain stays `pushing`, emits `push-outcome-unknown`, and MUST NOT retry blindly. A known push failure other than non-fast-forward stays `pushing` only while observation proves the old tip unchanged; otherwise observation decides.

Cleanup is admitted only after current intended-head containment established scalar `pushed`; it MUST freshly prove that exact current `landed_head` is contained in the remote default branch and that the recorded local branch has not moved. An older-only partial `landed_head` never admits cleanup, and cleanup never substitutes another historical attempt or the current worktree HEAD. It then runs `git worktree remove` without `--force` and deletes the branch only with non-force deletion after containment. An already removed worktree or absent branch is idempotent success for that step. Branch movement, observation failure, residual worktree content, or any partial removal yields `cleanup_pending`/`cleanup-failed`, never rewrites the push as failed, never deletes by force, and never re-runs push. Crash recovery handles each intent window independently: no observed side effect retries the step; observed rebase state routes to its owned recovery; current-head containment routes to `pushed`, older-only containment routes to partial-landed reconciliation, observed removed worktree/branch advances only already-admitted cleanup, and an unclassifiable observation sets `foreign-git-state`. A failed explicit lock release similarly preserves the already-recorded primary state and is retried without replaying the primary side effect.

- **FR-237** (MUST): System of record, archive binding, and concurrency. The DM-014 chain file and event log are the system of record for merge authorization, integration, recovery, push, and cleanup; the journal remains the run-narrative system of record and never authorizes a merge. When a run is open, CLI events use existing `verification`/`decision` shapes, name the explicit chain ID/generation, and cite artifacts inside the run or repository; the durable archive carries the terminal chain, complete event log, review/approval artifacts, and cleanup truth. No model copies state between records.

An `ownership_intent` payload has exactly `worktree_digest`, `claim_path`, `intended_claim_digest`, `predecessor_chain_id`, and `predecessor_release_digest`. The predecessor fields are both null when the deterministic path has no prior terminal owner; after tombstone collection they are the terminal predecessor's chain ID and exact `ownership_released` event digest. The subsequent `ownership_claimed` payload has exactly `ownership_intent_digest`, `claim_inode`, `claim_digest`, `predecessor_chain_id`, and `predecessor_release_digest`; its first field is the exact intent-event digest, inode/digest are the observed published claim and MUST match the intent's intended record, and predecessor fields repeat the intent byte-for-byte. Those fields replay an event durable before materialized `owned` state and create the successor's causal authorization edge without relying on wall-clock ordering.

Atomic ownership is an owner-controlled regular file at `.forge/chains/owners/<lowercase-hex-sha256-of-DM-013-canonical-worktree-identity>.claim` and is only a materialized projection of the chain's ownership events. Before publishing it, `merge start` persists the initial chain plus `ownership_intent` event and materializes `worktree.claim` as `{status: unpublished, path: <absolute-claim-path>, inode: null, digest: <intended-record-digest>}`; it then creates a mode-0600 temporary regular file in the same directory, writes and fsyncs one DM-013-canonical JSON record with exactly `chain_id`, `host`, `pid`, `session`, `started_at`, and `worktree_digest`, and atomically publishes the complete inode with a no-replace hard link to the claim path. It fsyncs the parent, unlinks the temporary name, appends `ownership_claimed`, materializes `worktree.claim.status` as `owned` plus the observed inode/digest, and only then reports admission. A crash before the link leaves no claim and bare recover may retry the same publication or abort the unpublished chain; a crash after the link but before the event/state update is completed only by matching the claim inode/content to the persisted intent. Thus the protocol never exposes a mkdir-without-owner window. A pre-existing claim naming a nonterminal, internally consistent chain returns `live-merge-chain-exists`; a terminal consistent claim is a released tombstone and is collected under that chain's lease before start retries. A malformed or unmatched claim cannot be a protocol partial write and is an irresolvable ownership-event/materialized-state divergence, so it returns exit 2 `frozen-chain`, reports path/inode/digest, and is never stolen automatically.

Claim quarantine is permitted only when repository-wide quiescence/owner-death proof, engaged `AGENT_HALT`, and complete chain/event reconciliation prove that the revalidated inode/content is garbage not named by any authoritative `owned` or `releasing` projection or `ownership_claimed` event. Only then does the CLI print the shell-quoted operator-reserved `mv <absolute-claim-path> <absolute-claim-path>.quarantine-<sha256>`; after the operator moves that exact inode, quiescence/reconciliation are re-proved and the halt is explicitly cleared before `merge start`. If any authoritative acquired owner names the path/inode/digest, even when the on-disk bytes are missing or divergent, logical ownership remains and no quarantine/retry recommendation is emitted: Revision 7 authorizes neither abandonment nor reconstruction of an acquired claim, so a separately reviewed operator recovery is required. A live or unprovable possible owner likewise receives no quarantine recommendation.

Normal acquired-ownership completion appends and fsyncs `ownership_release_intent`, materializes `worktree.claim.status` as `releasing`, and verifies the claim path still names the recorded inode/content. That event's payload has exactly `target_terminal`, `terminal_disposition`, `source_state`, `terminal_preconditions_digest`, and `release_mode`; `release_mode` is exactly `acquired` or `never-published`, and `terminal_disposition` is exactly `ordinary` or `historical-landed-superseded`. `target_terminal` is `aborted` with disposition `ordinary` only after owned-Git restoration plus either (a) no push intent/attempt and durable remote-untouched proof or (b) a prior push intent/attempt, one fresh authoritative observation whose full attempted-head containment vector is all false, and proof no live/unresolved fence/process remains; case (b) preserves all historical push results and does not claim the remote was never transiently changed. Current containment instead selects pushed truth. Older-only containment selects active partial-landed reconciliation, except that an inactive chain MAY select target `aborted` with disposition `historical-landed-superseded` only under FR-232's fresh full-vector, clean/unchanged current worktree/branch, and no-live-fence predicate; that disposition means the current generation was abandoned while the recorded older `landed_head` remains true, performs no cleanup/deletion, and preserves the worktree for a fresh chain. `target_terminal` is `closed` only with disposition `ordinary` after fresh containment of the exact current `landed_head` and every cleanup result are durable. For ordinary aborted-after-attempt, `terminal_preconditions_digest` binds the complete ordered attempted-head array, the complete all-false observation vector and observation digest, every historical push-intent/result event digest, and the no-live-fence/process proof. For historical-landed-superseded it binds the full vector, latest-contained older head, current uncontained intended head, every attempt/result digest, exact clean/unchanged worktree/branch identities, no-live-fence/process proof, and the literal disposition; for closed it binds the current `landed_head`, its containment observation, and all cleanup results. Fsync of this intent is the transaction's observation cutoff: later external remote changes do not supersede or retarget its immutable terminal selection. The subsequent `ownership_released` payload has exactly `release_intent_digest`, `release_mode`, `terminal_disposition`, `claim_inode`, `claim_digest`, and `claim_observation_digest`; its first field is the exact intent-event digest, its disposition repeats the intent byte-for-byte, and its last field is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical `{claim_path, exists, inode, digest}` observed immediately before the result. Acquired mode requires `exists: true` and the matching canonical claim inode/digest throughout release. It appends `ownership_released`, materializes status `released`, and only afterward appends the payload-selected `closed` or `aborted`; those authoritative events, not pathname absence, release logical ownership. The historical-landed-superseded `aborted` payload has exactly `terminal_disposition`, `landed_head`, `superseded_head`, and `observation_digest`; its literal disposition and OIDs repeat the release-intent-bound facts. A crash before the release result resumes solely from the exact intent after verifying immutable event/digest continuity and the local claim identity; it MUST NOT perform or require a fresh remote/cleanup observation. A crash after the result verifies only the release/precondition digests and completes the selected terminal event. Both pending stages are excluded from every new containment-priority row, including if the remote changes after the cutoff. Status `released` permits the old matching claim inode to remain only as a non-owning tombstone. After terminal truth is durable, the invocation best-effort revalidates that exact inode/content immediately before unlink and parent fsync; failure to collect it cannot revoke terminal truth, and a later start collects it under the terminal chain's lease before publishing a new claim.

Revision-10 scope-release clarification to **FR-237**: the post-fetch `run-scope-exceeded` path is ordinary no-push acquired release, and its `terminal_preconditions_digest` has exactly FR-231's `forge-run-scope-abort-preconditions/1` preimage. The fsynced release intent binds the authenticated `fetch_result.scope_proof` and is the cutoff; recovery never re-derives changed paths or scope and cannot select a different terminal reason. The durable archive carries the exact immutable scope-fetch sidecar bytes alongside the event copy and verifies their digest equality; omission, replacement, or disagreement is incomplete chain evidence.

The sole missing-claim exception is a chain whose authoritative `ownership_intent` and materialized projection remain `unpublished` with `inode: null`. Under its acquired per-chain lease, abort/recovery MUST prove the canonical claim path absent and revalidate that absence immediately before each protected append or state replacement. It appends an intent/result with `release_mode: never-published` and `terminal_disposition: ordinary`; the result uses `claim_inode: null`, the unchanged intended-record digest as `claim_digest`, and `claim_observation_digest` over the exact mapping with `exists: false`, `inode: null`, and `digest: null`. It then materializes `released` with null inode and that intended-record digest and appends `aborted`; it never unlinks a claim pathname. A crash at any boundary resumes from those events under the same absence-and-lease proof. Any missing or mismatched claim after ownership was acquired, or any claim appearing during a never-published release, is instead an irresolvable ownership-event/materialized-state divergence and returns `frozen-chain`. Thus abort and close have no ownership-release refusal that could broaden `state-precondition`, `cleanup-failed`, or common-lock-only `lock-release-failed`. Because the claim belongs to the chain rather than the start process, terminal proof permits logical release regardless of whether the recorded session process remains live.

Per-chain serialization uses the regular file `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.lock`, published atomically from a complete, fsynced mode-0600 same-directory temporary inode through the same no-replace hard-link protocol. Its canonical record has exactly `chain_id`, `host`, `nonce`, `pid`, `session`, and `started_at`; `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 OS-RNG bits. Acquisition waits at most 300 seconds. The owning invocation MUST verify that the canonical lease path still names its recorded inode/digest immediately before every protected event append, state replacement, or lease unlink; loss or replacement of the name removes its admitting transition and it performs no write. It unlinks and parent-fsyncs only after the protected update; a crash therefore leaves a complete record, never an empty partial.

Bare recover may reclaim a strict-valid lease only after same-host PID death plus state/event reconciliation and only while holding either the complete common lock or FR-235's sole provisional recovery reservation bound to this chain. That repository-wide exclusion serializes reclaimers. The winner retains the observed stale inode/digest, revalidates the canonical pathname against both immediately before unlink, unlinks and parent-fsyncs it, and publishes its own complete lease through the ordinary no-replace protocol before releasing the exclusion. A mismatch restarts inspection without removal. No contender that did not hold that exclusion may remove a lease, and every later protected write still performs the owner-path revalidation above; therefore two same-host death-proof contenders cannot ABA-remove one another's winning lease. A proven-live record is never quarantined: timeout returns `state-precondition` with exact next step `forge status --chain-id <id>`, and the holder must finish or be safely stopped. A foreign-host, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or mismatched record is likewise never stolen automatically. Operator quarantine is permitted only after the operator establishes repository-wide quiescence/owner death, engages `AGENT_HALT`, and reconciles state/events; only then may the reported fixed-path lease be moved to its same-directory `.quarantine-<sha256>` name. The operator then re-proves quiescence and reconciliation, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may bare recovery compete for a new lease; the CLI never performs or immediately recommends that move for a live or unprovable possible owner. Commands needing both locks acquire the common rebase lock first, then this lease, re-read state after both, and release the lease before the common lock; code holding the lease MUST NOT wait for the common lock. Commands needing no Git mutation hold only the per-chain lease for their bounded state update, so disjoint worktrees may classify/review concurrently. Merge start/refresh/finalize/recover/cleanup, merge abort when owned Git state may require abort/reconciliation, and standalone `forge push` serialize every fetch/rebase/push/cleanup/Git-abort mutation under the complete common rebase lock, including FR-236's fence. Inactive pre-push chains lose authorization but retain safe recovery/abort; pushed truth never expires. Malformed chain state, ownership-event/materialized-state divergence, event divergence not safely replayable, or foreign Git state sets the exact DM-014 fail-closed condition; automation never deletes terminal chain/event evidence.

- **FR-238** (MUST): Standalone `forge push`. The phase-4 command MUST be available while DM-015 is still `legacy-v1`, so activation never removes the only legal default-branch push path. It runs only in the main checkout, on the current branch exactly equal to the committed manifest's configured default branch, with remote exactly `origin`, destination fixed to that full branch ref, exact empty `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all`, and no in-progress Git operation. It performs the halt and read-only preflight, acquires the complete common rebase lock, and then acquires the existing FR-219 `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` before creating or changing DM-017. The order is always common lock then commit lock; push releases commit lock before common lock, and code holding commit lock MUST NOT wait for common lock. `forge commit finalize` continues to take only the commit lock and therefore forms no cycle, but the phase-4 implementation extends its complete transition tuple: after acquiring that lock and before candidate re-verification or `committing` intent, it `lstat`s and strict-parses `.forge/tmp/push-state.json`. Absence or schema-valid `closed` admits ordinary history mutation. The sole nonterminal exception is a schema-valid singleton at exactly `recovery_required` with `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, null `lock_release`, and no live or unresolved fence/process: that tuple admits only DM-015's already-reviewed, approved, candidate-bound canonical `.forge-manifest`-only repair finalize, preserves every singleton byte, and requires the next `forge push` to bind the repaired HEAD as a new attempt. Every other schema-valid nonterminal singleton leaves the commit chain unchanged in `authorized`, makes finalize unadmitted with byte-exact `state-precondition`, and names exact next `forge push`; a malformed/unreadable/symlinked singleton is the exact DM-017 corrupt chain file and returns `frozen-chain`. Because push cannot hold or change the singleton without this same commit lock, the check is stable and a crashed push transaction durably interlocks later sanctioned commits without making finalize acquire the common lock.

Failure or timeout of push's second acquisition returns byte-exact `lock-unavailable`, releases the common lock, and leaves an absent or prior singleton byte-identical. Once acquired for push, the commit lock is the inner layer of that invocation's complete rebase transaction lock. Its release is attempted before common-lock release; failure of either layer persists DM-017's exact `lock_release` identities and primary tuple, retains the portable common owner, returns exact `lock-release-failed`, and admits only recovery. Recovery proves/releases the commit layer through FR-219's existing serialized ownership/stale-owner mechanism before the common layer and never repeats a primary child or side effect. Thus the unchanged `lock-release-failed` precondition still means the complete rebase lock could not be explicitly released after primary truth was durable; `lock-unavailable` remains acquisition-only. DM-015's invalid-mode recovery-only admission covers every strict-valid nonterminal primary or wrapper whose recorded intent/result may have produced the invalid current HEAD: it permits only ordered release, death proof, identity-bound observation/classification, authoritative observation of an existing push intent, or exact owned abort/restoration; it never replays a child or starts integration/push work and stops at restored valid HEAD, current landed truth, or the repairable activation tuple. The push invocation otherwise holds both locks through singleton re-read/recovery, rebase, final HEAD/clean revalidation, push-intent/result persistence, authoritative outcome observation, and the final HEAD/clean revalidation before close. Under both locks it re-reads the singleton: any nonterminal record is observation-recovered first, while only absent/`closed` may be replaced with a new `preparing` record after preflight is repeated. Common-lock failure likewise leaves an absent or prior singleton byte-identical. The serialized invocation then persists fetch intent, fetches the fixed remote tip, rebases the default branch onto that exact tip, proves intended HEAD descends from the fetched expected-old tip, and uses only FR-236's exact ordinary non-force refspec and its explicit final-window deletion limitation. A sanctioned commit cannot move HEAD/index during that interval; an unsanctioned movement makes the complete transition tuple fail its exact pre-push or pre-close HEAD/clean predicate, so the attempted mutation/close has no admitting row and returns byte-exact `state-precondition` without falsely completing an older pushed OID.

Before rebase, the command resolves and validates both DM-017 reflog paths, records their exact existence/size/digest baselines with the nonce, action prefix, and intent digest, fsyncs that state, then sets the exact action prefix as `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` for rebase and any owned abort. A reflog is **baseline-unchanged** only when its current existence, size, and exact-byte digest equal the recorded object. It has an **owned appended suffix** only when the current regular file retains an exact byte-for-byte prefix of the recorded size/digest (or begins at byte zero for a recorded-absent file), every appended byte parses as complete Git reflog records, the suffix is nonempty, and every appended action begins with the exact unguessable prefix. Truncation, replacement, symlink, malformed or partial records, a non-prefix appended record, or bytes after the owned suffix satisfies neither predicate.

Normal completion and crash recovery use the same closed predicate set. Successful-no-op, no-side-effect, and rewritten-result predicates each require the recorded repository still be the main checkout on `destination_ref`, no Git-operation metadata, exact clean status, and no durable result reporting launch failure, nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach. **Successful no-op integration** additionally requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` be an ancestor of that HEAD, and both branch and HEAD reflogs be either baseline-unchanged or contain only an owned appended suffix ending at that same HEAD; because the recorded HEAD already contains the fetched tip, this predicate is safe when command execution is crash-ambiguous but not when a failed result is durable, and it sets `rebased_head = pre_operation_head`. **No side effect requiring retry** instead requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` not be its ancestor, and both reflogs be strictly baseline-unchanged; an absent result or proof that the start-pipe child exited before authorization may select this row. **Rewritten integrated result** requires current HEAD differ from `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` be its ancestor, owned appended suffixes for both reflogs, and a branch suffix whose first old OID is `pre_operation_head` and last new OID is current HEAD. Only successful no-op integration or rewritten integrated result may set `rebased_head` and create push intent. **Owned conflict** instead requires the rebase metadata live in the recorded main checkout, name `destination_ref` as head-name, record `pre_operation_head` as orig-head and `fetched_tip` as onto, require the branch reflog remain strictly baseline-unchanged, and require an owned HEAD-reflog suffix beginning at `pre_operation_head` whose last new OID equals current HEAD. Only that predicate may invoke abort, and it selects exact `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, never `rebase-failed`. **Proven restoration** requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, no Git-operation metadata, exact clean status, unchanged main-checkout/default-branch identity, and reflog bytes consisting only of the baseline plus owned prefix-bearing suffixes ending at the restored HEAD. A durable non-conflict failed result with exact proven restoration or exact clean no-side-effect facts records `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt`; it cannot select the owned-conflict or either integrated-success predicate. A durable actual rebase/abort failure with integrated-looking, contaminated, dirty, or otherwise unmatched state instead preserves the immutable failure result and records `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase`, but its current invocation still emits exact `rebase-failed` because the admitted Git operation actually failed. More generally, an external same-OID rewrite, a contaminated/later reflog entry, mismatched metadata, or any observation satisfying none of these exact predicates without a durable actual Git failure records the same attribution-unresolved recovery condition and returns exact `state-precondition`; that complete transition state admits observation only, never guesses a successful result, and never pushes.

An exact owned conflict therefore causes no push, persists `recovery_required` with `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, and returns exact `rebase-conflict`; the next `forge push` may only abort that owned rebase. Every such abort first appends/fsyncs DM-017's fresh nonce-bound abort-attempt intent; a retained exact conflict permits another attempt only after the predecessor is settled and with a new abort nonce, so no abort reuses the originating rebase intent as its operation identity. Before any new abort child, the invocation observation-classifies a settled predecessor: restoration is parked without replay, exact conflict alone admits the fresh attempt, and unmatched PASS/no-exec/historical-result truth enters the nonce-bound attribution wrapper and returns observation-only `state-precondition`. Successful proven restoration clears the conflict to a no-side-effect recovery waypoint and returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery. A non-PASS abort uses `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt` when restoration is nevertheless exact; if the owned conflict remains exact it retains/returns `rebase-conflict`; and an unmatched state records `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase` but returns exact `rebase-failed` only for an actual abort failure in that invocation. A durable non-conflict failure with exact no-side-effect/restoration proof causes no push and returns `rebase-failed`; a dirty, integrated-looking-after-failure, or otherwise unprovable state likewise returns `rebase-failed` while retaining attribution-unresolved observation-only recovery. Exact `state-precondition` is reserved for a later ambiguous observation with no actual failed Git invocation in that command. Even after an integrated predicate matches, FR-239's intended-HEAD DM-015 check precedes `rebased_head`/push-intent persistence and internally parks invalid mode as `activation-policy-invalid`; only the subsequent unadmitted push continuation returns `state-precondition`. The next `forge push` invocation performs these observation predicates first while holding both locks. Push intent/outcome follows FR-236: authoritative current-head landed observation closes success; older-only containment starts safe reconciliation without close; present old-tip observation permits one locked retry; an actual ordinary-update rejection followed by moved-or-absent observation uses `non-fast-forward`; unrelated observation-only movement or authoritative absence records `remote-moved`, returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and starts a new attempt on the next invocation; and unavailable observation uses `push-outcome-unknown` with no blind retry. Immediately before push-intent persistence and again after outcome observation before `closed`, HEAD MUST equal the recorded intended HEAD and status MUST be exact clean under the commit lock. This verb deliberately performs no ancestry provenance or chain-produced-SHA audit and MAY push commits no closed chain produced. Its `forge-cli/2` envelope always has `chain_id: null`.

- **FR-239** (MUST): Phase-4 activation and raw denial. The hook reads DM-015 only from committed HEAD in each matched Git context and applies this truth table: missing or `legacy-v1` preserves phase-1 dual-accept and raw-push behavior byte-for-byte; `forge-verbs-v1` atomically denies raw `git commit` and raw `git push`; duplicate/malformed/unknown mode denies either raw verb as invalid. Halt denial has precedence over all mutation handling; operator-verb denial applies to a matched Forge operator verb; invalid mode then precedes activated raw denial; in legacy mode existing marker/chain denial remains unchanged. Under either valid mode the sanctioned Forge CLI producer paths are exactly `forge commit finalize`, `forge merge finalize`, and `forge push`; legacy-v1's separately admitted raw behavior remains byte-identical, while `forge-verbs-v1` leaves only those three sanctioned Forge CLI producers and no raw-commit/raw-push admission. That is not an exhaustive claim about other Git porcelain or plumbing. Under an invalid mode only the DM-015 byte-exact repair form of candidate-bound `forge commit finalize` may produce history, while merge finalize and history-producing push receive the invalid-mode denial. The hook admits `forge push` only when a strict-valid nonterminal singleton exists and marks that invocation recovery-only before CLI execution; the CLI then enforces DM-015's closed no-new-work set. An absent/closed or corrupt singleton never uses this exception, an allowed recovery-only segment never blesses an appended raw segment, and the invocation cannot create history even if its observation discovers work to do. Existing FR-221 quote-aware segment splitting, leading-`env` handling, interpreter/path-final-component grammar, decoy rejection, and quoted-separator handling remain normative. Each segment is classified independently: an allowed Forge CLI segment never blesses an appended raw Git segment.

Because a rebase can change the intended HEAD after the outer hook classified its segment, merge finalize and standalone push MUST strict-parse DM-015 again from the exact intended HEAD immediately before push-intent persistence, under their complete locks and through FR-236's qualified no-write committed-blob read. Missing/`legacy-v1`/`forge-verbs-v1` is valid according to DM-015; duplicate, malformed, misplaced, or unknown is not. Invalid final mode creates no push intent. The already-admitted integration-result classification first durably parks merge in `revising` or standalone in `recovery_required` with internal DM-017 `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`; that classification step emits no CLI envelope. The same invocation's attempted continuation from that now-invalid complete transition tuple has no admitting push row, changes no further state, emits the exact invalid-mode line below, and returns byte-exact `state-precondition`. After approved repair, merge requires refresh and standalone begins a new operation rather than reusing the old intent. `activation-policy-invalid` is therefore a persisted standalone internal condition and an optional hook audit label, not a CLI reason. The exact new hook/CLI diagnostic literals are:

```text
forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (merge approve)
forge: raw git commit denied — use Forge CLI commit finalize
forge: raw git push denied — use Forge CLI merge finalize or Forge CLI push
forge: history mutation mode invalid — repair committed .forge-manifest through Forge CLI
```

Hook audit labels `activation-policy-invalid`, `raw-git-commit-denied`, and `raw-git-push-denied` remain outside the CLI reason enum because the hook emits deny JSON rather than an envelope; the same spelling `activation-policy-invalid` also names DM-017's explicitly separate persisted internal condition. The security claim is narrowly denial of raw `git commit`/`git push` under the PreToolUse threat model; phase 4 MUST NOT claim exhaustive prevention of every Git porcelain/plumbing mutation or require chain provenance for `forge push`.

- **FR-240** (MUST): Phase-4 migration, close protocol, and marker retention. Init/re-init writes or preserves DM-015 as specified and never silently activates an existing repository. Activation occurs only in a distinct reviewed, operator-approved control candidate after phase-3 merge, phase-4 push, hook, recovery, v2 corpus, and disable-in-memory checks pass. The same phase-4 implementation packet MUST update the source/template/installed hook registrations, CLI help and routing inventories, init/re-init manifest rendering, `/forge:worktree-merge`, Beads integration in rendered `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, and synchronization/conformance tests. The exact close-protocol replacement block is:

```bash
bd dolt push
python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" push
git status
```

Those lines are executed sequentially with each exit checked: a nonzero `bd dolt push` prevents Forge push from starting, a nonzero Forge push prevents `git status` from masking it as success, and `bd dolt push` itself is unchanged. Phase 4 MUST retain DM-006, the marker grammar/parser, every marker test, the phase-1 dual-accept, and every immutable v1 artifact even when activated raw denial makes marker admission dormant. No installed skill or template may continue to instruct a raw commit/push close path after activation.

- **FR-241** (MUST): V2 corpora, qualification, and last-line controls. Before a phase-3/4 implementation surface ships, every exact DM-016 artifact MUST exist and the v2 manifest MUST bind its digests, schema versions, the complete sorted §8 reason union, the hook-vector corpus, and the implementation/test subjects. Existing v1 artifacts are consumed, never copied or re-minted, and each v1 row is compared byte-for-byte. The v2 matcher corpus covers all three sanctioned producer paths, the merge-approval denial, legacy/enabled/invalid activation, compound commands with an allowed CLI segment plus appended raw Git, quoting/path/interpreter variants, and exact denial literals. The phase-3/4 manifest also requires deterministic transition, re-verification-matrix, recovery, push, cleanup, activated-denial, and last-line test results even though DM-016 names no additional eval prompt/result files. FR-223's harness-qualification tuple and current-major.minor/distribution-channel law continue unchanged for any operator-`!` premise. Every merge-finalize and push last-line check — target identity, clean tuple, evidence, review/approval, halt, lock, rebase result, final intended-HEAD DM-015 validity, final remote observation, push intent, containment, and cleanup safety — MUST be independently disableable in memory with a focused test that fails when that check is disabled. STRICT evals are fail-closed.

Revision-10 amendment to **FR-241**: the DM-016 generation-1 phase-3 manifest MAY carry `pending-phase-4` only in the `push`, `activated-denial`, and `push-last-line` result bindings. The pending token is explicit non-evidence: it authorizes phase 3 only, never phase 4, activation, or marker deletion. Phase-4 activation MUST land the generation-2 predecessor-bound manifest with those exact three slots changed to mechanically resolved PASS bindings and no other v2 artifact changed. A fabricated, unresolved, mismatched, or merely asserted PASS fails closed. Recording a fictional PASS is forbidden.

- **FR-242** (MUST): Phase-5 marker-deletion admission is a conjunction, not authority to delete. A later control revision MAY propose deletion only when one mechanically recorded candidate proves all eleven predicates: (1) release/manifests and executable compatibility tests prove phase-1 dual-accept shipped before phase 3 and was never bypassed in the supported upgrade lineage; (2) committed DM-015 is `forge-verbs-v1`; (3) the recorded DM-016 hashes prove every v1 corpus/eval/manifest byte unchanged; (4) v2 reason, matcher, merge-transition, matrix, recovery, push, cleanup, and activated-denial eval/test results all PASS; (5) every merge-finalize and push last-line check has a focused disable-in-memory test that fails on its mutant; (6) `STRICT=1` evals PASS; (7) full unittest discovery PASSes twice consecutively and serially after the candidate's last fix, with each suite's own tail retained; (8) a closed inventory scan of installed skills, templates, hooks, CLI help, rendered close protocol, and routing proves none produces raw commit/push or marker authorization; (9) an atomic scan under the applicable locks proves no nonterminal legacy chain and no unexpired DM-006 marker remains consumable; (10) marker grammar, parser, and tests remained present through the complete phase-4 candidate; and (11) the exact deletion candidate receives binding independent PASS and explicit operator approval. Missing, unavailable, stale, or non-PASS evidence makes the predicate false. Revision 7 does not authorize deletion, and a phase-5 implementation MUST preserve compatibility for every supported repository that has not yet proven activation rather than globally removing its only admission path.

For this predicate, the **supported upgrade lineage** is a closed ordered list of full released plugin commit OIDs declared by the phase-5 candidate, beginning with the first phase-1 dual-accept release and ending with the complete phase-4 release; every adjacent pair must have an immutable executable upgrade replay proving the marker and chain paths were simultaneously accepted. A missing release, unresolved OID, or release in that list whose committed hook lacks either path makes predicate (1) false. Each predicate record names the exact deletion-candidate full commit OID and tree OID, policy and artifact digests, command argv, append-only evidence sequence, start/end timestamps, exit, and bounded suite tail. “After the last fix” means the candidate commit/tree was fixed before the first run, both distinct records bind those same exact OIDs, their evidence sequence proves they ran serially in order with no intervening candidate mutation, and any later path change necessarily creates a new candidate commit/tree and invalidates both records; blob OIDs are never ordered by recency.

The predicate-(8) closed scan roots are the source and rendered/installed copies of `skills/`, `system/`, `hooks/`, `agents/`, `rules/`, CLI help/routing, `.forge-manifest` rendering, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `forge-project.md`; an unreadable or uninventoried consumer is failure. For predicate (9), every DM-012 `forge-chain/1` record with `kind: commit` is a legacy-admission chain regardless of DM-015 mode; “nonterminal” means any state other than `closed` or `aborted`, plus any terminal record with an unconsumed authorization or unresolved `committing` intent. “Consumable marker” is conservatively any owner-controlled regular file in `.forge/tmp/authorized/` whose 64-lowercase-hex name and content parse as any DM-006 form and whose recorded timestamp is no more than 30 minutes old under the same UTC clock law; malformed, symlinked, unreadable, or clock-ambiguous entries make the scan fail rather than count as absent.

The scan window begins only after the operator has engaged global `AGENT_HALT`; it then acquires the common rebase lock, the commit lock, and every existing merge-chain lease in that order, verifies no new chain/marker directory entry appeared between two complete sorted snapshots, and retains those locks through evidence persistence. No code holding a later lock may wait for an earlier one. The halt is operator-created and operator-cleared; phase-5 automation neither creates nor clears it. These definitions make predicate (9) independent of any history-mode field in DM-012 and make missing/malformed state fail closed.

- **FR-243** (MUST): Phase-6 declaration and deferral. The future names are exactly:

```text
forge plan seal --file <plan>
forge proposal unseal
```

They are reserved for a best-effort ordering/audit control only. It provides no confidentiality, does not make proposal bytes unreadable to a same-user process, and MUST NOT be described as an information-flow guarantee. Revision 7 adds no parser branch, state/schema key, storage, skill routing, reason code, permission, or success authority for either name. A later control revision must define run binding, canonical schema/digest, transition and resealing/overwrite policy, proposal identity, path/symlink containment, concurrency/inactivity/crash recovery, journal/archive records, output codes, tests, and operator authority before implementation.

---

## 8. API / Schema Contracts

### `validate <run_dir> [--gates]` — structural + gate check

Output (stdout, sorted-key JSON): `{ok: bool, issues: [str], warnings: [str], non_passing_verifications: [obj]}`, plus `profile: "gates"` iff `--gates`. Exit 0 iff `ok` (`ok == not issues`). Gate issues use the exact strings of FR-021..FR-023.

### Revision-9 typed journal builders and batch transaction

Every directly invoked mutating verb below receives exactly one nonempty global `--repo <absolute-repository>`, `--run-id <run-id>`, and `--idempotency-key <64-lowercase-hex>`; `run-open` uses the run ID as the new identity. Singleton duplication/emptiness is rejected before repository access, and an invalid transaction key uses exactly `forge: journal batch refused — idempotency key must be 64 lowercase hex`. The key is caller-stable transaction metadata and is excluded from the record; chain outbox drains instead use DM-012's source-event digest and expose no key flag. Builders inject `recorded_at`, `run_id`, allocated IDs, writer contract, and derived facts under the stable append lock. Repeated flags preserve order unless a row says canonical order. All paths pass FR-017, all records pass FR-019, and no raw-record input exists.

| Typed verb | Exact caller inputs and resulting fields |
|---|---|
| `run-open` | `--goal`, repeated `--scope`, `--plugin-ref`, optional `--successor-of`; injects `repo_head`, `repo_status`, canonical scope, and `writer_contract: "forge-journal-binding/1"`. |
| `run-readmit` | Repeated `--scope`, optional `--replace`; accepts the scope array only when it is already canonical, UTF-8-bytewise sorted, and duplicate-free. Normalized inputs are exactly `{"scope":<accepted-scope-array>,"replace":<boolean>}`; writes the existing `scope-readmission: locked` decision and publishes the registry only through the retained batch-sidecar-plus-registry-lock transaction. |
| `journal task-start` | `--task`, `--goal`, repeated `--acceptance`, repeated `--file`; writes an ordinary `task` with `status=active`. |
| `journal task-finish` | `--task`, existing terminal `--status`; inherits goal/acceptance/files and refuses until every same-task bound chain has either landed (landing decision and receipts preceding it) or been explicitly aborted with a null outbox and, when drained, its single replay-exact `chain-abort` decision; chains bound to other tasks of the run are skipped. No generation, supersession, or binding-summary flag exists. |
| `journal execution-start` | `--agent`, `--task`, `--provider`, `--role`, `--mode`, `--model`, `--effort`, `--worktree`, `--head`, `--prompt`, `--handoff`, `--event-source`, conditional `--events`; allocates `execution-NN` and verifies HEAD/worktree. |
| `journal execution-result` | `--execution`, `--agent`, `--task`, existing terminal `--status`, `--summary`, repeated `--file-changed`, repeated `--caveat`, conditional `--handoff`; verifies the paired execution. |
| `journal verification-add` | `--task`, `--criterion`, `--method`, `--check`, existing `--result`, `--observation`, repeated `--evidence`; allocates `check-NN`. An activated gate additionally requires both `--binding-chain` and `--binding-id`, which resolve the complete DM-001 object from replay; there is no generation flag. |
| `journal decision-add` | Optional `--task`, required `--resolution`, optional `--finding`, `--outcome`, `--risk`, repeated `--basis`, optional singular `--binding-id` paired with `--binding-chain`; allocates `decision-NN`. Chain outcomes are the exact DM-001 values. |
| `journal ingest-chain` | `--task`, `--state-file`, `--events-file`, `--outcome-map`, `--closing-head`, existing terminal `--task-status`; captures sources, proves all sixteen FR-210 predicates, and emits one typed batch. |
| `run-close` | Existing `--judgment`, `--summary`, repeated `--risk`, repeated `--follow-up`; validation and complete receipt/landing correlation are engine-injected. No manifest caller input or generated manifest field exists. |

For a run-bound commit or merge chain, an event is consequential exactly when it first makes one of these current-candidate facts durable. If one event establishes multiple facts, records use the row order below and Gate-1/Gate-2 configured order within their row. Every other chain event carries no journal batch.

| Durable chain fact | Exact ordinary journal output |
|---|---|
| Gate-1 or Gate-2 completion, passing or non-passing | One `verification` per completed counted gate, with its exact configured `criterion`, result, evidence, and DM-001 binding. |
| Gate-3 final-review verdict | One `verification` whose criterion is exactly `gate-3: review-final verdict`, whose result agrees with PASS/BLOCK, and whose binding carries the review tuple. |
| Required operator approval recorded | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-approval"` and the current candidate binding. |
| Operator-directed permitted skip recorded | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-skip"` and the current candidate binding. |
| Commit or merge landing first becomes authoritative | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-landing"` and the landed candidate binding. |
| Explicit `commit abort` of a never-landed commit chain that holds a staged candidate (Revision 13) | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-abort"` and the abandoned candidate binding; merge aborts carry nothing (pending), and terminal chains refuse the verb. |
| Retrospective `commit abort-disposition` on a run-bound chain aborted without a carried decision (Revision 13) | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-abort"` sourced from the `abort_disposition_recorded` self-event; nothing else changes. |
| `commit abort-disposition --run-id <run>` on an operator-tombstoned chain the journal cites (Revision 13, bead forge-plugin-11a) | One `decision` with `outcome: "chain-abort"` whose binding is sourced from the canonical tombstone digest and whose `basis` is the tombstone path; no chain event exists to carry it. |

`journal batch-recover` accepts only the global repository/run identity, resolves the single `.journal-batch.intent`, and performs FR-019's suffix-classification recovery. It cannot start a new batch and accepts no idempotency-key flag. The intent and receipt schemas are `forge-journal-batch-intent/1` and `forge-journal-batch-receipt/1`; `batch_bytes` is the exact canonical journal suffix encoded as unpadded base64url. Before any regenerated field, the completed retry identity is `(idempotency_key,request_sha256)`, which resolves the receipt's original `batch_sha256`; a conflicting request refuses and a matching intent recovers stored bytes. Readers use the same `.journal-batch.lock` shared and issue `forge: journal read refused — pending or changed batch transaction` without recovery.

Revision-11 extends that recovery surface only with FR-019's canonical single-interior-gap repair; it adds no verb, flag, journal field, or reason-enum member. For an unpublished readmission intent, standalone recovery supplies no `--replace` authority and therefore retains every exact current scope member; only the matching keyed `run-readmit` retry can supply and revalidate `--replace`.

### `forge-gate-binding/1` and archive carriage

The exact binding member sets, candidate variants, null/non-null review rule, digest preimage, activation, and discrepancy enum are DM-001. Resolution precedence is: (1) an activated record requires its complete structured object; (2) the producing builder or ingest replays the named immutable chain event and requires exact equality plus current-generation freshness; (3) FR-021 later correlates only the resulting journal fields; (4) pre-activation prose is escaped display only. There is no `legacy-inferred` authority. Missing or ambiguous legacy authority renders `UNBOUND`.

For each cited chain, the archive writes `<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-STATE v1 bytes=N sha256=H fence=F -->`, the deterministic exact-byte JSON fence, and `<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-STATE -->`. It then writes exactly one event block:

```text
<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE v1 encoding=base64url bytes=N sha256=H -->
<one-line base64url payload without padding>
<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE -->
```

or, only above 2,097,152 raw event bytes:

```text
<!-- FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE v1 encoding=UNEMBEDDED bytes=N sha256=H -->
<!-- /FORGE:CHAIN-EVIDENCE -->
```

`N` is decimal and `H` is 64 lowercase hex. Base64url has no padding or whitespace and decodes to the exact event-log bytes. The renderer verifies the event digest chain, state equality, and before/after identities, and enforces the final 16,777,216-byte UTF-8 archive limit before destination mutation. Normal archive commit mode is `commit start --archive-run-id <run-id>`. Legacy mode adds the inseparable pair `--legacy-recovered-head <full-object-id> --legacy-approval <recovery-run-id>:<decision-id>` and requires the exact FR-172 decision grammar and labeled archive lines. Neither mode consumes a manifest.

### PreToolUse commit guard — hook contract

Input: Claude Code PreToolUse hook JSON on stdin (`tool_name`, `tool_input.command`). Output: on block, JSON `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "permissionDecision": "deny", "permissionDecisionReason": "<reason>"}}`; on allow, exit 0 with no decision. Reasons: `forge: operator halt engaged (<sentinel>)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker missing)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker malformed)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker stale)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker hash mismatch)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (fast-path policy drift)`; and `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (fast-path eligibility drift)`.

### PostToolUse invariant guard — hook contract

Input: Claude Code PostToolUse hook JSON on stdin for Edit/Write. Output: advisory text beginning `forge: invariant advisory — ` on a hook-row non-pass; always exit 0 and never emit a deny decision. Outside an initialized forge repository: no output, exit 0.

### `run-evals.sh [STRICT=1]`

Stdout lines `PASS <id>` / `FAIL <id> (expected X, got Y)` / `PENDING <id>` plus summary. Exit: 0 ok, 1 regression (or STRICT+PENDING), 2 malformed/empty.

### `check-halt.sh [<scope>]`

Exit 0 clear, 1 halted (message names the sentinel); outside git repo: warning + exit 0.

### Gate-pass marker (DM-006)

Written only by `/forge:commit` after Step 4's reviewed PASS, mechanically eligible fast completion, or FR-056 skip path at `.forge/tmp/authorized/<staged-diff-sha256>`; only that candidate path is consumed and deleted by Step 5. The guard recomputes `git diff --cached | shasum -a 256` in its invoking Git context, resolves the corresponding common-root path, validates the exact DM-006 shape, and independently recomputes fast policy/eligibility for the fast form.

### `aggregate-telemetry.sh` — CSV and decision-event contract

The script accepts the `.forge/tmp/decisions` directory, continues to parse its existing `*.md` fenced `telemetry` blocks, and additionally parses FR-157's `events.jsonl`. Its standalone CSV CLI is `aggregate-telemetry.sh <decisions-dir> --csv <path> [--since <UTC-ISO-8601> --until <UTC-ISO-8601>]`; its concurrent Stop mode is `--append-csv <path> --session <session-id>`. The two window flags MUST appear together, define a half-open `[since, until)` filter over parsed event `at` values, and fail with exit 2 when malformed or reversed. Without the flags, the deterministic window is 00:00:00Z on the first day of the current UTC calendar quarter through the invocation instant. The Stop invocation in FR-093 uses that default and appends a `session` column value without replacing prior rows. Separately, `drift-check.sh` MUST apply the same paired `--since`/`--until` bounds—FR-155's `window_start` and `window_end`—to its own direct FR-157 event aggregation when constructing the JSON `telemetry` object; the drift path MUST NOT invoke a CSV-writing mode or request, write, retain, or read any CSV artifact. In a Forge-initialized repository, whenever `<decisions-dir>/events.jsonl` exists, standalone mode MUST emit the requested CSV with the exact header and exactly one `__decision_totals__` row, even when there are no per-unit `*.md` files or no fenced telemetry blocks; per-unit Markdown availability MUST NOT gate event parsing or CSV creation. If no valid in-window event remains, the row's thirteen counters are all zero. Its standalone CSV header has exactly 22 columns:

```csv
unit,feature,model,elapsed_s,critical_path_s,tokens,cost_usd,review_iterations,rework_s,eligible_commits,fast_allowed,fast_denied_policy,fast_denied_eligibility,user_skips,review_blocks,halt_events,guard_denies,assertion_blocking,assertion_advisory,assertion_waived,review_cheap_findings,review_final_findings
```

Existing per-unit rows retain their first nine fields and use empty counter fields. The script appends exactly one row whose `unit` field has the literal value `__decision_totals__`, whose other eight legacy fields are empty, and whose thirteen appended fields are nonnegative base-10 integers for the selected window. `eligible_commits` counts `gate_commit`; `guard_denies` counts `fast_denied_policy`, `fast_denied_eligibility`, and `guard_deny`; the other five new counters map to their same-named singular event literals, with `review_cheap_finding` and `review_final_finding` aggregated into plural columns. Aggregation deduplicates `gate_commit`, `fast_allowed`, `fast_denied_policy`, `fast_denied_eligibility`, `user_skip`, `review_block`, and `guard_deny` by `(event, candidate)` whenever `candidate` is nonempty. For those seven gate-outcome event types, records with `candidate == ""` count as separate occurrences. `halt_event` and the five measurement event types remain occurrence-counted. Thus FR-155's `fast_allowed` numerator and `eligible_commits` denominator use the same candidate rule. CSV quoting follows RFC 4180. The prior-quarter comparison reads only committed drift reports. The current report's window and counters are sourced only from the Drift summary JSON's `telemetry` object; neither a standalone CSV nor the append-only `.forge/tmp/telemetry.csv` is an input to `/forge:drift`, `drift-check.sh`, or the committed drift report.

Append mode has exactly 23 columns: the literal leading `session` column followed by the standalone 22-column header above. A nonempty `session-id` is required and is RFC-4180 encoded in every row emitted by that invocation. Under `.forge/tmp/telemetry.lock`, the writer MUST create an absent or zero-byte target by writing that exact 23-column header once followed by the invocation's ordinary per-unit rows and exactly one `__decision_totals__` row, each prefixed by the same session value. For a nonempty target it MUST first parse the first record and require byte-for-byte equality with the exact 23-column header; a missing, duplicate, or different header is malformed and MUST cause exit 2 without changing the file. It then MUST append only complete LF-terminated records at EOF, MUST NOT repeat the header, and MUST preserve every existing byte. An invocation with no valid telemetry source follows §9's missing-input exit-0 contract and appends no rows. A read, validation, locking, or append failure exits 2 and leaves the pre-invocation file bytes unchanged; implementations MUST stage the invocation's complete encoded record block before one locked append so another session cannot interleave records.

### Drift summary schema v1

Every drift summary is a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly the top-level keys `checks`, `findings`, `generated_at`, `journal_patterns`, `policy_sha`, `schema_version`, `status`, and `telemetry`; canonical output sorts every object key lexicographically, renders one line followed by one LF, and sets the integer `schema_version` to `1`. `generated_at` is a UTC ISO-8601 string. `policy_sha` is a full commit SHA, or the empty string only when resolving HEAD is the represented failure.

Each `checks` element has exactly `check` (stable check identifier string), `duration_ms` (nonnegative integer), `outcome` (`passed`, `finding`, `failed`, or `skipped`), and `summary` (non-secret string). `telemetry` always has exactly 17 direct members: `available` (boolean), the thirteen nonnegative integer counters from the window-bounded aggregation, `event_prune`, and `window_start`/`window_end` (UTC ISO-8601 strings when available, otherwise empty strings). `event_prune` has exactly `entries_removed` (nonnegative integer), `failure` (a stable non-secret failure-code string, or `""` when pruning succeeded or did not run), and `new_oldest_at` (the oldest retained event's UTC ISO-8601 `at`, or `""` when no entry remains, pruning did not run, or pruning failed). A prune failure leaves aggregation telemetry available, sets `entries_removed` to `0` and `new_oldest_at` to `""`, and sets `failure` to the applicable nonempty code. When `available` is false because telemetry aggregation and pruning did not run, every one of the thirteen counters and `entries_removed` is `0`, `failure` and `new_oldest_at` are `""`, and both window strings are empty.

`journal_patterns` always has exactly `available`, `decision_outcomes`, `diagnostics`, `failure`, `findings`, `routing`, and `tasks`. `available` is boolean and `failure` is `""` when extraction succeeded, `not-run` when a prior precondition prevented extraction, or a stable non-secret failure code when extraction failed. `decision_outcomes` is an object mapping each exact observed decision `outcome` string to a nonnegative integer count, with keys in bytewise order. `diagnostics` is a bytewise-by-`diagnostic` sorted array whose elements have exactly `count` (positive integer) and `diagnostic` (the byte-preserved exact diagnostic string). `findings` has exactly `by_reviewer_role` and `by_severity`; each is a bytewise-key-sorted object mapping an exact observed role or severity string to a nonnegative integer count. `routing` is sorted by `(run_id, execution)` and each element has exactly `agent`, `committed_effort`, `committed_model`, `execution`, `recorded_effort`, `recorded_model`, `run_id`, and `status`; all but `status` are strings, absent comparison values are `""`, and `status` is exactly `matched`, `mismatched`, or `unavailable`. `tasks` is sorted by `(run_id, task)` and each element has exactly `block_to_pass_latency_ms`, `iterations`, `results`, `run_id`, and `task`; `results` preserves journal order and contains exact verification-result strings, `iterations` is a nonnegative integer, and `block_to_pass_latency_ms` is the nonnegative integer UTC-millisecond difference from the `recorded_at` of the first BLOCK-equivalent failed verification to its first later passed verification, or `null` when there is no such pair or either timestamp is unavailable or malformed. A successful empty-corpus extraction is available with empty objects/arrays and `failure: ""`. When unavailable, every object/array is empty; a dirty precondition or any path skipped before extraction uses `failure: "not-run"`, while an extractor failure uses its stable failure code and makes the Drift summary an exit-2 failure. `/forge:learn` MUST NOT start unless `journal_patterns.available` is true.

`status` is always an object: state `ok` and `findings` have only `state`; state `failed` also has a stable `failure` string and has `dirty_paths` only for `dirty-worktree`, where it is a bytewise-sorted array of unique repository-relative path strings. Each finding has exactly `check`, `code`, `evidence`, `severity`, and `summary`; `check` and `code` are stable nonempty strings, `evidence` is an array of non-secret strings, `severity` is exactly `CRITICAL`, `MAJOR`, or `MINOR`, and `summary` is nonempty. This is the single finding-object shape consumed by FR-162.

The four outcome shapes below are literal schema examples; concrete arrays may contain more objects but may not change their element shapes.

Exit 0:

```json
{"checks":[{"check":"worktree-clean","duration_ms":4,"outcome":"passed","summary":"clean"}],"findings":[],"generated_at":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","journal_patterns":{"available":true,"decision_outcomes":{"consensus":2},"diagnostics":[{"count":3,"diagnostic":"forge: repeated failure"}],"failure":"","findings":{"by_reviewer_role":{"review-final":2},"by_severity":{"MAJOR":2}},"routing":[{"agent":"codex-review-01","committed_effort":"high","committed_model":"gpt-5.6-sol","execution":"execution-02","recorded_effort":"high","recorded_model":"gpt-5.6-sol","run_id":"run-01","status":"matched"}],"tasks":[{"block_to_pass_latency_ms":42000,"iterations":2,"results":["failed","passed"],"run_id":"run-01","task":"task-02"}]},"policy_sha":"0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567","schema_version":1,"status":{"state":"ok"},"telemetry":{"assertion_advisory":2,"assertion_blocking":1,"assertion_waived":1,"available":true,"eligible_commits":12,"event_prune":{"entries_removed":3,"failure":"","new_oldest_at":"2025-08-01T09:00:00Z"},"fast_allowed":4,"fast_denied_eligibility":1,"fast_denied_policy":2,"guard_denies":5,"halt_events":0,"review_blocks":1,"review_cheap_findings":3,"review_final_findings":2,"user_skips":0,"window_end":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","window_start":"2026-07-01T00:00:00Z"}}
```

Exit 1:

```json
{"checks":[{"check":"mutation-full","duration_ms":600000,"outcome":"finding","summary":"surviving mutant"}],"findings":[{"check":"mutation-full","code":"mutation-survivor","evidence":["category=python"],"severity":"MAJOR","summary":"full-suite mutation left a surviving mutant"}],"generated_at":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","journal_patterns":{"available":true,"decision_outcomes":{"consensus":2},"diagnostics":[{"count":3,"diagnostic":"forge: repeated failure"}],"failure":"","findings":{"by_reviewer_role":{"review-final":2},"by_severity":{"MAJOR":2}},"routing":[{"agent":"codex-review-01","committed_effort":"high","committed_model":"gpt-5.6-sol","execution":"execution-02","recorded_effort":"high","recorded_model":"gpt-5.6-sol","run_id":"run-01","status":"matched"}],"tasks":[{"block_to_pass_latency_ms":42000,"iterations":2,"results":["failed","passed"],"run_id":"run-01","task":"task-02"}]},"policy_sha":"0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567","schema_version":1,"status":{"state":"findings"},"telemetry":{"assertion_advisory":2,"assertion_blocking":1,"assertion_waived":1,"available":true,"eligible_commits":12,"event_prune":{"entries_removed":3,"failure":"","new_oldest_at":"2025-08-01T09:00:00Z"},"fast_allowed":4,"fast_denied_eligibility":1,"fast_denied_policy":2,"guard_denies":5,"halt_events":0,"review_blocks":1,"review_cheap_findings":3,"review_final_findings":2,"user_skips":0,"window_end":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","window_start":"2026-07-01T00:00:00Z"}}
```

Dirty-precondition exit 2:

```json
{"checks":[{"check":"worktree-clean","duration_ms":3,"outcome":"failed","summary":"dirty worktree"}],"findings":[],"generated_at":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","journal_patterns":{"available":false,"decision_outcomes":{},"diagnostics":[],"failure":"not-run","findings":{"by_reviewer_role":{},"by_severity":{}},"routing":[],"tasks":[]},"policy_sha":"0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567","schema_version":1,"status":{"dirty_paths":["docs/spec.md","scratch.txt"],"failure":"dirty-worktree","state":"failed"},"telemetry":{"assertion_advisory":0,"assertion_blocking":0,"assertion_waived":0,"available":false,"eligible_commits":0,"event_prune":{"entries_removed":0,"failure":"","new_oldest_at":""},"fast_allowed":0,"fast_denied_eligibility":0,"fast_denied_policy":0,"guard_denies":0,"halt_events":0,"review_blocks":0,"review_cheap_findings":0,"review_final_findings":0,"user_skips":0,"window_end":"","window_start":""}}
```

Non-dirty exit 2:

```json
{"checks":[{"check":"invariant-sweep","duration_ms":300000,"outcome":"failed","summary":"runner failed"}],"findings":[],"generated_at":"2026-08-11T12:00:00Z","journal_patterns":{"available":false,"decision_outcomes":{},"diagnostics":[],"failure":"not-run","findings":{"by_reviewer_role":{},"by_severity":{}},"routing":[],"tasks":[]},"policy_sha":"0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567","schema_version":1,"status":{"failure":"invariant-execution","state":"failed"},"telemetry":{"assertion_advisory":0,"assertion_blocking":0,"assertion_waived":0,"available":false,"eligible_commits":0,"event_prune":{"entries_removed":0,"failure":"","new_oldest_at":""},"fast_allowed":0,"fast_denied_eligibility":0,"fast_denied_policy":0,"guard_denies":0,"halt_events":0,"review_blocks":0,"review_cheap_findings":0,"review_final_findings":0,"user_skips":0,"window_end":"","window_start":""}}
```

### `cli.py` — Forge CLI output envelope (FR-220)

Without `--json`, stdout is human-oriented and ends with exactly one `next required step: <exact command>` line (or `next required step: none — chain closed`). With `--json`, stdout is exactly one UTF-8 sorted-key JSON object and nothing else: `{"chain_id": str|null, "evidence_refs": [str], "expected": str|null, "message": str, "next_required_step": str, "observed": str|null, "ok": bool, "reason_code": str, "remediation": str|null, "schema": "forge-cli/1", "state": str|null}`. Exit 0 success; exit 1 refusal with `reason_code` drawn from the fixed spec'd enum; exit 2 internal failure/frozen chain. Refusal strings and reason codes are pinned control surface per FR-220.

### Phase-1.1 CLI grammars — shipped contract

The complete phase-1 mechanical gate-ID registry is:

```text
changelog
gate-1
stack:<category>
assertion-sensor
invariant:<row-number>
secret-scan
strict-evals
```

`<category>` is normalized lowercase `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`. `<row-number>` is the one-based invariant data-row ordinal and only a row whose enforcement is `commit` is required. Gate 1 appears twice under the one ID. `classification` is internal; `review` is only a skip target; index drift uses `--index-drift`. Unknown/unconfigured IDs return `state-precondition`. The shipped `int()` resolver can execute `invariant:01` or `invariant:+1`, but evidence remains keyed by that literal and cannot satisfy canonical `invariant:1`.

There is no generic mutating-gate grammar. `changelog` is the sole mutating gate. Its no-gate body is a case-insensitive, dot-all full match of:

```regex
No changelog gate (?:is configured|applies)(?: for| to)?(?: this)? .*?repository\.
```

Otherwise DM-003's exact one-cell/output grammar applies. Only the first matching prose `Output path(s):` declaration contributes, every qualifying Markdown row contributes, prose tokens precede Markdown tokens, and later prose declarations are ignored; the two parser channels retain DM-003's distinct backtick normalization and shared first-occurrence deduplication. Success extends the explicit path set, stages those outputs, recomputes candidate identity, invalidates downstream evidence, and reclassifies.

The shared review-verdict transport is:

```text
VERDICT: PASS|BLOCK
candidate: <exact-current-candidate>
package: <exact-issued-package-digest>
finding: <CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR> <nonempty text>
```

Input is strict UTF-8; each split line is stripped and blanks discarded. The verdict is first. Candidate and package each occur exactly once later in either order. Findings are optional/repeatable and every other line is invalid. PASS permits MINOR only; BLOCK may contain no finding. `OBSERVATION` is not a transport severity. This is transport validation, not the complete substantive review constitution.

DM-013 canonicalization is compact sorted-key UTF-8 JSON with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, no trailing LF, and no prefix. `command_digest` hashes the exact subprocess argv array. `env_fingerprint` hashes exactly `{command_digest, cwd, platform, policy_digest, python_version, repo_head}`, with `cwd = os.path.realpath(repository_root)`, `platform = sys.platform`, Python `major.minor.micro`, the exact pinned-policy digest, and full live HEAD. Nothing else contributes. The mismatch comparison fires only when the two newest current-candidate records are adjacent PASSes, neither has `pair_voided` true, and their fingerprints differ; it voids all unvoided current-candidate PASSes and applies exactly `DM-013 env_fingerprint mismatch voided the Gate-1 pair`. An intervening non-PASS or voided member suppresses that comparison; the first fresh PASS after a voided PASS does not retrigger, and two later adjacent unvoided matching PASSes can complete Gate 1.

### `forge-merge-chain/1` — merge argv and state contract

The persisted top-level key set is exactly:

```text
schema, chain_id, kind, state, created_at, last_event_at,
inactive_after, owner, run, repository, worktree, branch, target,
policy_source, candidate, tier, steps, review, approval,
authorization, integration, cleanup
```

`schema` is `forge-merge-chain/1`, `kind` is `merge`, and DM-014 defines candidate canonicalization and persistence. Its orthogonal condition enum is exactly:

```text
integration.condition = none | fetch-failed | rebase-failed |
  remote-moved | remote-churn | push-failed | non-fast-forward |
  push-outcome-unknown | lock-release-failed | foreign-git-state
cleanup.condition = none | cleanup-failed
```

DM-014's condition table is part of the state contract: a condition cannot invent another state, reason-code meaning, or admitted verb.

The accepted argv forms are exactly:

```text
forge status --chain-id <id>
forge merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]
forge merge refresh --chain-id <id>
forge merge verify --chain-id <id>
forge merge gate run <gate-id> --chain-id <id>
forge review request --chain-id <id>
forge review attach --verdict-file <file> --chain-id <id>
forge review disposition --finding <n> --severity <sev> --resolution <text> --chain-id <id>
forge merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>
forge merge finalize --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --continue --paths <path>... --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --abort-rebase --chain-id <id>
forge merge cleanup --chain-id <id>
forge merge abort [--reason <text>] --chain-id <id>
```

The state value is exactly one of:

```text
classifying, verifying, reviewing, revising,
```
